## A. Project identity and architecture

**British Trade Awards CMS** — a web-based admin system for managing awards ceremonies, organisations, entries, judging, events, payments, email marketing, and CRM.

- **Repository:** `billy-strummer/awards-cms` — **Verified** (confirmed via `git remote -v` and GitHub API calls throughout this engagement).
- **Working branch:** `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza` — **Verified**.
- **Supabase project:** `qdzyknercdqwhwijbcxf` (name `british-trade-awards-cms`, region `eu-west-2`) — **Previously reported** (via `mcp__Supabase__get_project`). ~~Live database status is currently Uncertain~~ — **Corrected in BTA-030**: production was explicitly resumed during BTA-012 (2026-10-23, see Section D.4.0) and has been independently reconfirmed `ACTIVE_HEALTHY` in every subsequent BTA task through BTA-029 that checked it. Its *current RLS/grant/policy content* (as opposed to its mere active/paused status) remains partially addressed by migration 086 (unapplied) — see Section D.4.

**Technology stack** (per `CLAUDE.md`'s own self-description — **Verified as the document's content**, not independently re-audited feature-by-feature against the live codebase):
- Frontend: Vanilla JavaScript + Bootstrap 5 (single-page app)
- Backend: Supabase (PostgreSQL) + Vercel serverless functions (`/api/`, capped at 12 functions on the Vercel Hobby plan)
- Email: Resend API · Payments: Stripe · AI vetting: Anthropic Claude API
- Build: esbuild (`node build.js`) · Tests: Jest (`npm test`) · Deployment: Vercel

Distinguish: the architecture description reflects `CLAUDE.md`'s own content, read directly from the current checkout. It has not been independently re-verified feature-by-feature in this task.

## B. Permanent operating rules

Agreed working process for all subsequent tasks on this project (mirrored in `CLAUDE.md`'s "MANDATORY: Project Continuity Protocol" section — that file holds the permanent rule, this file holds the changing project state and history):

1. Read this task sheet (if present) and verify the actual current repository state — do not assume prior reports still hold without rechecking git state, branch, HEAD, and remote tips.
2. Work on exactly one approved, clearly scoped task at a time. Do not expand scope mid-task.
3. Prefer read-only, evidence-first investigation before any implementation. Propose a bounded plan and get explicit approval before writing code or migrations.
4. Run the relevant tests after any change and report the **exact** results (pass/fail counts, suite names) — never characterize untested work as verified.
5. Update this task sheet at the end of each task: work completed, files changed, tests actually run and their results, current git state, decisions made, risks identified, and the proposed next task.
6. Before stopping, report the current branch, HEAD, working-tree state, and explicitly whether any change is **local-only**, **committed locally**, or **committed and pushed** (see Section H for what each level actually guarantees).
7. **Never** commit, push, merge, rebase, rewrite history, restore/resume the Supabase project, or execute live migrations without explicit, task-specific approval. A general earlier approval does not carry forward to a new action.
8. **Never** mark a task complete unless the evidence directly supports that status.
9. If this task sheet is ever found missing, contradictory, or inconsistent with repository evidence, stop and report the gap — do not silently proceed as though project history is known from memory.
10. Preserve the existing substantive CMS roadmap (Section D's BTA-00x sequence) whenever updating this sheet — continuity administration must never silently replace, reorder, or simplify away the underlying project work.

| **BTA-044** | "Per-Action Role Gates for certificates-qr.js and email-automation.js" (found and fixed during BTA-043A) | **Implemented and tested.** `api/certificates-qr.js`: traced real frontend callers for all 8 actions (`winners.js` → `preview-certificate`, `generate-bulk-certificates`, `generate-and-email`; `events.js` → `generate-qr-ticket`; `generate-certificate`/`generate-badge`/`generate-all-badges`/`verify-qr` have **zero** callers anywhere in the repo, confirmed by grep — unlike BTA-039's assumption that `verify-qr` might be a low-privilege door-staff feature, there is no such live feature at all). Added a `CERTIFICATES_QR_ACTION_ROLES` map (mirroring `data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_RPCS` pattern) and one role check after the existing `verifyAuth`: `preview-certificate` → `viewer` (read-only, no mutation, no email — matches `rbac.js`'s existing read/export right on the `winners` module); every other action → `editor` (all create a file, mutate a check-in/ticket record, or send an email — matches `rbac.js`'s existing create/update requirement on `winners`/`events`). **Also found and fixed a real functional bug while tracing `generate-and-email`**: its internal server-to-server call to `/api/email-automation` sent **no Authorization header at all**, so the email send has always failed with 401 (silently ignored — the response was never checked), while the code still stamped `certificate_sent_at` and reported `success: true` regardless. Fixed by forwarding the caller's own `Authorization` header and only stamping `certificate_sent_at` (and returning the new `email_sent` field, now honestly reflecting the real outcome) when the downstream send actually succeeds. `api/email-automation.js`: `send-deadline-reminders`/`send-winner-announcements` (confirmed, via grep, to have zero live frontend caller — only the internal scheduler and tests invoke the underlying functions directly) are genuine mass-sends with the same blast radius as `send-campaign` in the sibling `api/resend-email.js` file, which BTA-037 already gated to `admin` — applied the identical floor here, not a new rule; `send-template`/`send-email` (used broadly, including internally by the fixed `generate-and-email`) deliberately left ungated. New test files `tests/certificates-qr-role-gate.test.js` (13 tests: per-action 403s, the viewer-allowed `preview-certificate` case, and 2 tests proving the auth-forwarding fix with a fully-sequenced mock of the real certificate-generation pipeline) and additions to `tests/email-automation.test.js` (5 new tests: 403 for viewer/editor on both mass-send actions, no-403 for admin, `send-template` unaffected). **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/certificates-qr-role-gate.test.js tests/certificates-qr.test.js` → 54/54 passed; `npx jest tests/email-automation.test.js` → 37/37 passed. `npx eslint api/certificates-qr.js api/email-automation.js tests/certificates-qr-role-gate.test.js tests/email-automation.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed. |

### D.5 — BTA-051 comprehensive CMS functional/quality review workstream (admin workflows + content inventory)

**Distinct from D.4** (the Supabase RLS/data-proxy security-hardening workstream, which is complete through BTA-054 above and deliberately kept separate per the governing prompt's own instruction). This workstream is the "comprehensive review" part of BTA-051 — admin-CMS workflow tracing (parts A continued/B/C) and the front-end content inventory (part A/the separate content-inventory request) — carried out via four parallel, read-only research passes. Findings are added below as each completes; this is a live, in-progress section.

#### BTA-055 — Admin-CMS workflow trace: Organisations, Sectors, Categories, Locations/Areas, Awards & Award Years, Imports

**Read-only trace, no code changed.** Full chains followed from admin UI → API → table, with file:line citations; classification is not inferred from a page/table's mere existence.

| Workflow | Classification | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Organisations | **Working and verified** | `organisations.js` → `apiClient.select/insert/update('organisations',...)` → `/api/data-proxy.js` (`organisations` in `ALLOWED_TABLES`, writable by `editor`+) | Full chain consistent. |
| Sectors/Categories (`custom_sectors`/`custom_categories`) | **Working and verified, admin/super_admin only** | `settings.js:1644,1797,1819,1914,1941,1958` uses `sort_order`/`is_active`/`available_for_small`/`sector_name` — **confirms the app targets `database-sectors-categories.sql`'s shape, not migration 058's relational shape** (the already-documented tension in Decision register items 2–4; not re-decided here, just newly confirmed from current code rather than the historical F.1 comparison). Neither table appears in any role's `write` Set in `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` — consistent, not a bug, since `rbac.js` already excludes the `settings` module (where these are managed) from `editor`'s module list entirely. | No gap found. |
| Locations/Areas | **Partially implemented — confirmed dead admin control, more precise than previously recorded** | `location.js:133`/`areas-manager.js:47,196-344`/`awards.js:599` all read/write the live `areas.is_small` column; but `submit-entry.js:165,536,553` and `submit-entry-payment.js:152,386,403` **ignore `areas.is_small` entirely**, hardcoding their own `SMALL_COUNTIES` array (also independently duplicated in `scripts/award-categories-config.js:164`) for the actual public small-business eligibility check. | **New precision on Decision register item 1**: it isn't just "application logic generally" bypassing `is_small` — the admin-editable `is_small` toggle in the Locations manager has **zero effect** on the real public entry form for any of the ~36 flagged areas; editing it there does nothing live. Still the same open business decision (not re-decided); now backed by an exact code citation instead of a general statement. |
| Awards & Award Years | **Partially implemented; one stated business-rule premise not found in the repository** | Writes go through the `awards` view (`database-schema.sql:21`) to `award_years.status`, whose real values are `'Draft'`/`'Active'`/`'Archived'`/`'pending'` (`awards.js`, many lines). | **Discrepancy flagged, not silently resolved, per this project's own standing rule**: the governing BTA-051 prompt's "business rules to preserve" list states `award_years.results_status = 'finalised'` — an exhaustive repo-wide grep (all `.sql` and `.js` files) found **zero occurrences of `results_status`, `finalised`, or `finalized` anywhere in this repository.** The real season-lifecycle field is `award_years.status` (Draft/Active/Archived) — a *publish-state* concept, not a *results-finalisation* concept, and a different thing from what the prompt described. **This needs the user's attention**: either the `results_status`/`finalised` concept was never actually built (and the stated business rule describes intended-but-unimplemented behaviour), or it exists under a different name/table this review hasn't yet found. Not assumed either way — recorded as open. |
| Imports (CSV / Award-Area import) | **Working and verified** | `nominee-uploads.js` → `api/data-proxy.js`'s `award_area_import` → `executeAwardAreaImport`: validates rows, resolves categories (`api/_lib/award-categories.js`), dedups organisations (`api/_lib/organisation-matching.js`), keys `award_years` on `area_id` (not the legacy free-text `county` column — a deliberate, commented fix already in place), generates sequential `BTA-<year>-NNNN` entry numbers. | Consistent with BTA-006's earlier, deeper, test-backed audit of this same feature (70/70 tests, Section J). The already-fixed tenant-isolation check (`rejectUntrustedTenantId`) was not re-investigated, per this pass's scope. |

#### BTA-056 — Admin-CMS workflow trace: Entries, Nominations, Judging, Results/Winners

**Read-only trace. Headline finding: two parallel, disconnected result-tracking systems exist** — `entries`/`award_assignments` (admin-facing) and `winners` (the ONLY table the public site reads) are never kept in sync, and no writer of `winners` ever sets the one column (`placement`) the public page uses to distinguish Winner/Top 3/Finalist.

| Workflow | Classification | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Entries (direct submission) | **Working and verified** | `submit-entry.js`→`api/entry-proxy.js:589` `handleSubmitEntry` creates one `entries` row (`status:'submitted'`) via org-matching + `generateEntryNumber()`; admin review lifecycle (`entries.js`) operates entirely on `entries`. | Self-contained and consistent on its own terms. |
| **Nominations (`nominate.html`)** | **Broken — confirmed, high-severity, live** | `nominate.js:126-138`'s `buildPayload()` sends only `{action, nomineeName, nomineeWebsite, nomineePhone, nomineeWorkDesc, nominationReason, nominatorName, nominatorEmail, nominatorPhone}`. `api/entry-proxy.js:823,853-887`'s `handleSubmitNomination` requires `awardCategory` (400 if absent) and, unless `isNewBusiness`, `nomineeCompany` (400 if absent) — **neither field exists anywhere in `nominate.html`** (zero matches for "award"/"category" in that file). **Every nomination submission currently fails with a 400.** This is a live, linked, deployed page (`about.html`, `submit-entry.html` link to it; `build.js` bundles it for production). The client-validated `nomineeWorkDesc` field (20+ chars required) is also silently dropped even if the 400s were fixed — no server field reads it. | **Not fixed — needs a UX/business decision**, not a guess: either add the missing fields to `nominate.html` (award category selector, nominee company field) and map `nomineeWorkDesc` to an accepted field, or relax the backend's required-field list for this deliberately lightweight nomination flow. Flagging for the user to choose the intended UX rather than assuming one. |
| Judging | **Working and verified**, feeds a pipeline Results doesn't fully consume | `judge-portal.js` scores via `judge_scores` against `entries`; `api/judge-automation.js:271-365`'s `generateShortlist()` aggregates scores and writes `entries.is_shortlisted`/`status='shortlisted'` — self-consistent on `entries`/`shortlists`. | Shortlisting never creates or touches `award_assignments` — see Results row below for why that matters. |
| **Results/Winners** | **Broken — confirmed, high-severity**: the public Winners page cannot currently show anything other than "Winner" | See detailed evidence below. | |

**Results/Winners — detailed evidence:**
1. The public site's only data source is `winners.placement`: `public-winners-app.js:9-17`'s `placementLabel()` defaults to `'Winner'` when `placement` is falsy, `'2'`/`'runner_up'` → `'Top 3'`, else `'Finalist'`. Fed by `api/voting-proxy.js:412-427`'s `loadWinners()`, which selects `.from('winners')` and maps `placement: w.placement`. **`award_assignments.winner_position` is never read by this page or its API at all.**
2. **No writer of `winners` ever sets `placement`**: `winners.js`'s `saveAddWinner()` (the "Add Winner" admin modal, no `placement` input exists), `winners.js`'s CSV `importWinners()` (never maps a `placement`/position column even if the CSV supplies one), and `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` (computes a `positionInt` from the Winner/Runner-up buttons but only ever sends it to `award_assignments.winner_position` — never into the `winners` insert). **Net effect: clicking "Runner-up" still shows "Winner" on the public site.**
3. `award_assignments.winner_position` and `winners.placement` are not synced, and no code path anywhere promotes an `entries` row into `award_assignments` automatically — an admin must manually use "Assign Company" (`assignments.js`) for a web-submitted entry to ever enter the position-setting pipeline. May be intentional (a deliberate manual gate) or a gap; not assumed either way.
4. The admin's own two result-setting UIs are mutually blind: `assignments.js`'s `setWinnerPosition()` (the Assignments-tab dropdown, BTA-032/034's already-tested surface) writes `award_assignments.winner_position` only, never `winners`; `winners.js`'s `showAwardPlacements()` reads `award_assignments` only. **Setting a position via the Assignments tab never reaches the public site**, since the public site reads only `winners`.
5. **Corroborates BTA-055's `results_status` discrepancy** (Decision register item 17): `award_years.results_status`/`'finalised'` does not exist anywhere in this repository. The only actual public-page gating is a per-row `embargo_until` date filter (`public-winners-app.js:124-128`).

**Found issues (not fixed by this fork — for the coordinator/user to review):**
1. Nomination-form field mismatch (above) — needs a UX decision.
2. `winners.placement` never set by any of its 3 writers — **attempted a narrow fix to `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` this task (adding the already-computed `positionInt` to its `winners` insert as `placement`), but reverted it before it reached the task sheet**: no SQL migration in this repository creates a `placement` column on `winners` at all (grepped every `.sql` file), while multiple JS read-paths (`winners.js`, `public-winners-app.js`'s already-shipped BTA-036 shield/banner generators, `api/voting-proxy.js`) treat it as an established, real column. Live schema verification to resolve this (`information_schema.columns` for `winners`) was attempted but the Supabase MCP connection was returning connection timeouts on every query (including a bare `SELECT 1`) for the whole remainder of this task — a genuine, currently-unresolved environmental blocker, not a decision not to check. **Shipping the fix without that confirmation risked turning "wrong label" into "the Confirm Winner button throws and does nothing," a worse regression** — INSERTing a non-existent column is a hard Postgres error, not a silent no-op, and the function's own `catch` block only swallows `duplicate`-type errors. Reverted via `git checkout -- winner-pipeline.js`; confirmed clean via `git diff` showing no remaining change. **This is the single most important unresolved item from this review — needs a live schema check (query below) before anyone attempts this fix again.**
   - **Exact re-check needed**: `SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema='public' AND table_name='winners' ORDER BY ordinal_position;` against `htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp` (test), read-only. If `placement` is present: the fix described above (add `placement: positionInt` to `winner-pipeline.js`'s `winners` insert in `confirmWinner()`) is safe to re-apply with a regression test. If absent: the column needs to be added via a proper migration first (and every other writer — `saveAddWinner()`, `importWinners()` — updated to accept/map it too), which is a larger, still-non-architectural but multi-file task.
3. `award_assignments`↔`winners` remain two unsynced systems even once (2) above is fixed — **this is a genuine architecture question (which table is canonical) flagged for the user/business-decision list, not attempted unilaterally**, consistent with "do not redesign the results model."
4. No automatic `entries`→`award_assignments` promotion exists — flagged, not assumed to be a gap vs. an intentional manual gate.

#### BTA-057 — Admin-CMS workflow trace: Users & Permissions, Communications, Packages, Payments, Reporting/Exports

| Workflow | Classification | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Users & Permissions | **Working and verified, but zero test coverage** | `settings.js:2132-2243` (invite/role/disable/reset-password UI) → `api/data-proxy.js:2260-2283` (`user_invite`/`user_set_role`/`user_set_status`/`user_reset_password`/`user_list`, all gated `super_admin`) → `executeUserInvite` etc. (`:1669-1749`) using Supabase's real Auth Admin API. Self-demotion and self-disable are both blocked. | **Confirmed gap**: zero test matches anywhere in `tests/` for any of these 5 actions — the only path to create/manage CMS users has no test coverage at all. Not fixed this task (shared file, `tests/data-proxy.test.js`, deferred to avoid collision with the concurrent security workstream touching the same file) — recommended as a follow-up task: add `tests/data-proxy-user-management.test.js` covering 403-for-non-super_admin, self-demotion/self-disable blocks, and the happy path for each action. |

The underlying Postgres function reads its Resend API key from a separate `cms_config` table (`migrations/006-fix-email-sending.sql:105`), not the Vercel `RESEND_API_KEY` env var — genuinely dependent on whether that table is populated, unverifiable without a DB connection. | **See the dedicated finding below — this is the most important result of this fork.** |

Sponsor *assignment* to specific awards/events (`marketing.js`, `sponsor_assignments`) is a separate, also-working flow. | No gap found; `ticket-management.js`'s event packages were spot-checked only, no red flags. |

Admin UI → DB write → DB read is a closed, consistent loop. | No gap found. |

**Major finding — migration 081 (`081-revoke-anon-execute-email-functions.sql`), never tracked in this document, documents a severe, empirically-proven live exploit whose current status is unknown:**

The migration's own header (dated 2026-07-22, same wave as the already-tracked-and-applied migrations 080/084) documents **real, executed anon-key RPC calls against the TEST project** proving `check_email_config()`, `process_scheduled_campaigns()`, `send_campaign_emails()`, and `send_entry_confirmation_email()` were all directly callable by `anon`/`authenticated` via PostgREST's auto-exposed `/rest/v1/rpc/<name>` endpoint — **completely bypassing `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_RPCS` role gate**, because Postgres `GRANT EXECUTE` exists independently of the app's own authorization logic. `check_email_config()` returned real config-disclosure data to an unauthenticated caller; `send_campaign_emails()` and `send_entry_confirmation_email()` only avoided a real mass-send/real send because the specific test conditions happened to hit a safe no-op branch (0 subscribers / entry too old) — against real data they would not be safe. `send_single_email()`/`send_test_email()` were not executed (their source has no safe branch and would have sent a real, uncontrolled email), but their identical unprotected grant plus confirmed source is conclusive on its own. The migration's fix (`REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` for all 6 functions) exists in the repository. **A grep of this entire task sheet for "081" before this task found zero matches** — despite the file being dated the same week as several already-tracked, already-applied migrations, its live application status has never once been checked in this whole BTA-007–057 security workstream.

**Attempted live re-check this task, blocked by environment, not by policy:** queried `information_schema.routine_privileges` for all 6 function names against `anon`/`authenticated`/`PUBLIC` on the test project (`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`) — every `execute_sql` call this task made returned `"Connection terminated due to connection timeout"`, including a bare `SELECT 1`, while `list_projects` continued to work normally and one `get_advisors` call separately reported `"Project ... is currently hibernated and will wake on next supported request"`. This looks like a transient, environment-level outage in the query-execution path specifically, not a connectivity problem with the MCP server as a whole, and not something resolved by retrying (6 consecutive attempts across this task, all identical). **Not resolved — this needs a fresh attempt in a follow-up task.**
- **Exact re-check needed**: `SELECT routine_name, grantee, privilege_type FROM information_schema.routine_privileges WHERE routine_name IN ('check_email_config','process_scheduled_campaigns','send_campaign_emails','send_entry_confirmation_email','send_single_email','send_test_email') AND grantee IN ('anon','authenticated','PUBLIC') ORDER BY routine_name, grantee;` against `htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp` (test), read-only. **Empty result = already fixed** (migration 081 applied or an equivalent revoke already happened). **Rows present = still exploitable exactly as migration 081's header describes** — in which case migration 081 should be queued for the same "apply to test, re-verify, then consider production" treatment already used for 086/087/088, with the same explicit authorisation requirement.
- This is **more urgent than migrations 086/087/088** if still unapplied: those three control RLS/row-grants on secondary tables; migration 081 controls direct, unauthenticated execution of functions that send real email and disclose config — a more direct, higher-severity exposure. Recommend checking this first once the Supabase connection issue clears.

#### BTA-058 — Front-end content inventory and CMS-editability map

**Read-only inventory across all public pages + email templates.** Full table omitted here for length (preserved in the originating research pass); headline items:

- **Entry-open countdown/date** (`home.html`): CMS-managed via `award_seasons`, live-fetched each page load (not build-time baked), editable in Settings/Awards — clean, no gap.
- **Sector/category taxonomy** on the public entry form: **confirmed 3-way duplicated hardcoded base** (`home-data.js`'s `SECTOR_CATEGORIES`, a second inline `STANDARD_CATEGORIES` copy in `submit-entry.js`, and `scripts/award-categories-config.js` — the first file's own header comment claims "update this ONE file... every page updates automatically," which is not true) plus a DB-backed additive overlay (`custom_sectors`/`custom_categories`) that only *adds* categories, never edits/removes the hardcoded base. This sharpens, from 2-way to 3-way, the already-tracked Decision register items 1–6 — not re-decided here.
- **Promotional/sponsor banners**: fully built admin CRUD (`marketing.js`, `banners` table) with **zero public consumer** — confirmed by grep that no public page fetches or renders anything from `banners`. A complete, working CMS feature that currently does nothing visible. Flagged as a scoping question (wire it up, or remove the unused admin feature) rather than guessed at.
- **Email "Placeholder Defaults" panel** (Marketing tab, `user_preferences` table): editable in the CMS, but **confirmed to have zero effect on real outgoing email** — the real send path (`api/email-automation.js`) builds its own separate `variables` object and never reads `user_preferences`. The panel only affects the in-admin preview/test-send tool. Minor additional oddity: a *global* (non-per-user) setting stored in a table named `user_preferences` with no `user_id` scoping.
- **FAQ content**: confirmed **absent entirely** — zero matches for "FAQ"/"Frequently Asked" across all public HTML. Not a defect, just confirmed absent.
- **`privacy-policy.html`/`terms-and-conditions.html`**: correctly static, correctly excluded from CMS/design work per `CLAUDE.md` — not flagged as a gap, no action proposed.
- **Two confirmed real email bugs** (fixed this task — see BTA-059 below): `sendEntryConfirmation()` and `sendNominationConfirmation()` were missing several variables the DB-backed CMS default templates actually use, so real entrants/nominators received literal unsubstituted placeholder text and (for entry confirmations) a dead "Upload Supporting Documents" link whenever a DB template row existed for either type.

**Phased plan for routine CMS-editable content (proposal only, nothing implemented beyond BTA-059's narrow bug fix):**
- **Phase 1 (low-risk wiring, no new tables)**: the two email-variable bugs (done, BTA-059); decide whether to wire the existing `banners` table into the public pages or remove the unused admin feature; either make the Email Placeholder Defaults panel's date fields genuinely drive the real send path (read `user_preferences` as a fallback when no season date exists) or relabel it clearly as preview-only.
- **Phase 2 (moderate effort, replacing specific named hardcoded elements)**: consolidate the sector/category taxonomy's 3 independent hardcoded copies into one, generating or loading the other two from it at build time — reduces drift risk regardless of which taxonomy model is ultimately chosen; does not itself resolve that model decision.
- **Phase 3 (genuine business/architecture decisions, not buildable without one)**: the taxonomy-model decision itself (Decision register items 1–6, now with 3-way-duplication evidence); whether `industry-leader.html`/the legal pages should ever gain CMS backing; whether an FAQ feature is wanted at all.

#### BTA-059 — Fix: entry/nomination confirmation emails were missing CMS-template variables (found during BTA-058)

**Implemented and tested.** `api/email-automation.js`'s `sendEntryConfirmation()` built a `variables` object for the DB-backed "Entry Confirmation" template substitution path (`sendTemplateEmail()`'s uppercase-`{KEY}` branch, which automatically uppercases every supplied variable key — confirmed by reading `sendTemplateEmail()` directly, not assumed) that omitted `sector`, `region`, `upload_link`, `deadline_date`, `announcement_date`, and `contact_email` — all six of which the real DB-default "Entry Confirmation" template content (`email-templates.js`'s `_defaultTemplates`, what's seeded/shown/reset-to in the CMS editor) actually references as `{SECTOR}`/`{REGION}`/`{UPLOAD_LINK}`/`{DEADLINE_DATE}`/`{ANNOUNCEMENT_DATE}`/`{CONTACT_EMAIL}`. Once any DB row exists for this template type, real entrants would receive an email with literal unsubstituted placeholder text and a dead `href="{UPLOAD_LINK}"` button — confirmed this was a genuine, intended feature (not stale): `upload-documents.js:50-55` reads an `?entry=` query param expecting exactly this link to have been sent, with its own error state literally saying "Please use the link from your confirmation email." Fixed by adding all six variables, sourced from already-available data on the same `entries`/`award_years` read (`entry.sector`, `entry.county_city` → `region`, `award_years.entry_close_date` → `deadline_date`, the already-computed `winner_announcement_date` reused for `announcement_date`, a new `${appUrl}/upload-documents.html?entry=${entry.entry_number}` link matching `upload-documents.js`'s actual expected param exactly, and `process.env.CONTACT_EMAIL || process.env.FROM_EMAIL || ''` matching the identical fallback chain already established in `api/stripe-payment.js`). Applied the identical `contact_email` fix to both call sites of `sendNominationConfirmation()` in `api/entry-proxy.js` (the DB-backed "Nomination Confirmation" template also references `{CONTACT_EMAIL}`, never previously supplied). The code-only fallback templates (`api/_lib/email-templates.js`, used only when no DB row exists) were already self-consistent with the old variable set and needed no change — confirmed by reading them, not assumed. Added one new regression test (`tests/email-automation.test.js`, "supplies every placeholder the DB-backed Entry Confirmation template uses, leaving none unsubstituted") that mocks a DB template containing all 6 placeholders and asserts the sent HTML contains none of the literal `{...}` tokens and does contain the real substituted values, including the exact `entry=BTA-001` upload link. **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/email-automation.test.js tests/entry-proxy.test.js` → 70/70 passed (1 new). `npx eslint api/email-automation.js api/entry-proxy.js tests/email-automation.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. **Full regression after this change**: `npx jest` → 74 suites, 6588 tests, 6585 passed, 3 pre-existing skips, 0 failed (up from 6587/6584 before this task — the +1 is this task's new test). `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, 8 pre-existing warnings (none in a file this session touched). `node build.js` → succeeded (50 public pages copied, including the modified `api/`-independent frontend unaffected by this backend-only change). No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

**Note on the Supabase connection outage**: every `mcp__Supabase__execute_sql` call attempted after the fork notifications landed (7 consecutive attempts across this task, including a bare `SELECT 1`) returned `"Connection terminated due to connection timeout"`; one `get_advisors` call separately reported the project as "currently hibernated and will wake on next supported request." `list_projects` continued to work normally throughout. This blocked both the migration-081 live re-check (BTA-057) and the `winners.placement` schema check (BTA-056) — neither is a decision not to check, both are a genuine, unresolved environmental blocker to retry in a follow-up task.

#### BTA-060 — Resolve Core Entry and Results Workflow Findings

**Re-verified starting state (this task):** branch/HEAD/upstream unchanged (`claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, `fd2e145`, in sync, 0 ahead/0 behind); working tree exactly as BTA-059 left it (9 modified files + 2 untracked migrations) — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat` before starting, not assumed from the prior report.

**1. Nomination workflow — root cause established with full evidence, including WHY, via git history (not previously traced this precisely):**

Traced the complete journey: `nominate.html` → `nominate.js`'s `buildPayload()` → `POST /api/entry-proxy` (`action: 'submit_nomination'`) → `handleSubmitNomination()` → organisation match/create → `insertEntryWithRetry()` → `sendNominationConfirmation()`.

- **`nominate.js`'s payload** (confirmed by direct read): `{action, nomineeName, nomineeWebsite, nomineePhone, nomineeWorkDesc, nominationReason, nominatorName, nominatorEmail, nominatorPhone}` — 8 fields. The HTML form also collects a 9th field, `nominatorArea` ("In what county or city are you based?"), but `buildPayload()` never includes it in the POST body at all — a second, smaller, confirmed bug, dependent on the same decision as the main one below (see consequence table).
- **`handleSubmitNomination()`'s actual required fields** (confirmed by direct read, `api/entry-proxy.js:823-887`): `awardCategory` (400 if absent — the very first check, so this is what actually fires today) and, unless `awardCategory === 'New Business of the Year'`, both `nomineeName` **and** `nomineeCompany` (400 if either absent). None of `awardCategory`/`nomineeCompany` exist in `nominate.html`. **This is why every real submission 400s**, confirmed precisely rather than inferred.
- **Why the mismatch exists — traced via `git log`, not assumed:** `handleSubmitNomination` was introduced in commit `d26e2ed` ("Add Industry Leadership nomination form for public nominations", 2026-03-09) alongside the *original* `nominate.html`/`nominate.js`, which at that time **was** the full category-aware "Industry Leadership" wizard and correctly sent `awardCategory`/`nomineeCompany`/the business-vs-person branch fields. Commit `ca18254` ("Add simple nominate.html, rename wizard to industry-leader, fix step 0 buttons", 2026-06-16) **deliberately split this in two**: the original 6-step wizard was renamed to `industry-leader.html`/`industry-leader.js` (confirmed: `industry-leader.js:751-752` still sends `action: 'submit_nomination', awardCategory: d.awardCategory, ...` — **this flow still works correctly against the existing backend, unchanged**), and a **brand new, deliberately simpler** `nominate.html`/`nominate.js` was created at the same URL, per that commit's own message: *"single-page form (nominee name/website/phone/work desc, why they deserve entry, nominator contact details)"*. **The new simple form was never given its own backend action, and `handleSubmitNomination` was never updated to accept its lighter payload** — it still posts to the same `submit_nomination` action, which still enforces the original wizard's requirements. This is not a regression in the sense of something that used to work and broke — **the simple form has never worked, since the commit that created it**, five months ago.
- **A matching, confirmed test-coverage gap explains why this was never caught**: `tests/nominate.test.js` mocks `window.fetch` entirely, so it only ever asserts the client correctly *calls* `/api/entry-proxy` with the simple payload shape — it never exercises the real backend logic. `tests/entry-proxy.test.js`'s own `submit_nomination` tests (`validNominationBody()`) construct the **old, rich** payload (`awardCategory`, `nomineeCompany`, etc.) — matching `industry-leader.js`, not `nominate.js`. **Both test suites are green; the actual end-to-end path neither tests has ever covered is the one that's broken.** Flagged under Section 5/testing-maintainability, not fixed (adding a new test alone doesn't fix the underlying contract mismatch, and writing one that asserts the current 400 would just encode the bug as "expected").
- **Not fixed — this is a genuine business/product decision, not a code defect with one safe answer.** The task's own instruction not to weaken backend validation merely to make the form submit is directly on point here: relaxing `handleSubmitNomination`'s required fields to match the simple form's shape would require deciding what database state a company-less, category-less nomination should produce (does it get an `organisation_id` at all — `nomineeName` on the simple form could be a person or a company, unlike the rich form's distinct `nomineeCompany`; what `award_category` value does it get — none, or a placeholder pending admin triage; does it become a real `entries` row immediately, exactly like a rich nomination, or something lighter for an admin to triage first). Recorded as **Decision register item 19** with three concrete options:
  1. **Add the missing fields to `nominate.html`** (an award-category selector, a nominee-company field) so it satisfies the existing rich contract — makes the simple form behave like a cut-down version of `industry-leader.html`, preserving all existing backend/data-model logic unchanged.
  2. **Give `nominate.html` its own, new, lighter backend action** (e.g. `submit_simple_nomination`) that stores a minimal record for admin triage (assign category, convert to a formal entry, or reject) — preserves the simple form's actual UX intent (from the commit that created it) but requires deciding the new record's exact shape and where it surfaces in the admin UI.
  3. **Relax `handleSubmitNomination`'s validation conditionally** when the payload lacks `nomineeCompany`/business fields — the fastest code change, but risks producing `entries` rows with no award category or company match that the rest of the admin pipeline (judging, shortlisting, results) isn't designed to handle gracefully; this option carries the most downstream risk and is the one most likely to silently "invent" behaviour no one asked for.
  **No option was chosen or implemented.** `nominatorArea`'s separate missing-from-payload bug is dependent on whichever option is chosen (it maps to the backend's existing `county_city`/`region` field, used for area-scoped organisation matching) — fixing it in isolation right now would change nothing, since the whole endpoint 400s before reaching that logic.

**2. Results/Winners architecture — full writer/reader trace and source-of-truth comparison (extends BTA-056 with the explicitly requested comparison table and schema-verification attempt):**

| Concept | Where it's written | Where it's read | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `award_assignments.status` | Defaults `'nominated'` on insert (`assignments.js`'s "Assign Company", CSV import); set to `'winner'` by both `assignments.js`'s `setWinnerPosition()` and `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` | `assignments.js`'s `getStatusBadge()` (admin only) | Matches the stated business rule exactly — confirmed unchanged. |
| `award_assignments.winner_position` | `assignments.js`'s `setWinnerPosition()` (admin dropdown, 1/2/3); `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` (via `updateByFilters`, only if a matching row already exists) | `assignments.js`'s `getWinnerPositionBadge()` (admin only — correctly shows nothing when null, Winner/Top 3/Finalist per the stated rule when set) | **Admin-only.** No public page reads this column at all — confirmed by repo-wide grep, unchanged from BTA-056. |
| `winners.placement` | **No writer sets it** — `winners.js`'s `saveAddWinner()` and `importWinners()` never include it; `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` computes a `positionInt` but never adds it to the `winners` insert | `public-winners-app.js`'s `placementLabel()` (the **only** public results page) and `winners.js`'s `_resultBadgeWord()` (shield/banner generators) | **This is the only column the public site reads for results**, and it is always null in practice. Both reader functions default a falsy/null `placement` to `'Winner'` (not `'Finalist'` or "no badge") — **this specific default is why every published result currently displays as "Winner."** |
| `award_assignments` ↔ `winners` sync | None — no code path copies `winner_position` into `winners.placement` or vice versa | — | Confirmed, not new: two independent systems, same finding as BTA-056, now with the exact default-value root cause pinned down precisely (not just "placement is never set" but "and the specific fallback behaviour of its two readers is what converts 'never set' into 'always shows Winner', rather than, say, showing nothing"). |
| `award_results` | **Does not exist** — zero matches for this name as a table, view, or function anywhere in the repository (`.sql` or `.js`) | — | The stated business rule's phrasing ("award_results derives Winner, Top 3, Finalist or Nominee") does not correspond to any actual object in this codebase. The derivation it describes is real, but it happens inline in 3 separate JS functions (`placementLabel`, `_resultBadgeWord`, `getWinnerPositionBadge`), not in a named database object. |
| "Nominee" as a 4th result tier | N/A | N/A | **Confirmed absent as a result tier.** Every occurrence of "Nominee" in this codebase is either (a) a generic term for "everyone currently assigned/entered, pre-judging" (`awards.js`'s "Nominees (count)"), or (b) an unrelated CRM organisation-lifecycle status value (`organisations.js`'s status dropdown), or (c) an email-campaign-type label. The actual result model found everywhere else in this codebase (BTA-032/034/036's already-shipped fix) is exactly 3 tiers — Winner/Top 3/Finalist — not 4. |
| `award_years.results_status` | **Does not exist** — see Section 4 below | — | |

**Source-of-truth comparison, prepared per this task's explicit request — not decided here:**

| Option | What changes | Effect on existing records | Effect on admin workflow | Effect on public display |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — `award_assignments` becomes canonical** | `winners` is either retired or becomes a derived/denormalised copy kept in sync by a trigger or application-level write-through; the public page (`api/voting-proxy.js`'s `loadWinners()`) is rewritten to read `award_assignments.winner_position` (joined to `organisations`/`award_years`) instead of `winners.placement` | None destroyed — existing `award_assignments.winner_position` values (already correctly set via `assignments.js`, BTA-032/034-tested) become immediately correct on the public site for the first time; existing `winners` rows (created via `confirmWinner`) become redundant/historical | `assignments.js`'s existing, already-tested "Assign Company" + position-setting flow becomes the single place admins set a result — `winner-pipeline.js`'s separate "Confirm Winner" UI would need to be retired or rewired to write the same column | Immediately correct for every assignment an admin has already positioned via the Assignments tab, with no new admin action needed |
| **B — `winners` becomes canonical** | `award_assignments.winner_position` is either retired or kept only as a working/deliberation field; `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()` is fixed to actually set `placement` on insert (the narrow fix this task attempted and reverted — see BTA-056); `assignments.js`'s `setWinnerPosition()` would need to additionally write to `winners` | Existing `winners` rows (created by `confirmWinner`/`saveAddWinner`/CSV import) remain the live records; existing `award_assignments.winner_position` values set via the Assignments tab would need a one-time backfill into `winners` to not be silently lost from the public page | `winner-pipeline.js`'s "Confirm Winner" flow becomes the single place admins set a result; `assignments.js`'s dropdown would need to write through to `winners` too, or be retired | Requires either the backfill above or re-confirming every existing position via `winner-pipeline.js` before anything shows correctly |
| **C — Neither; introduce a genuine single source of truth (e.g. a new `award_results` table/view actually matching the business-rule description)** | The larger, most architecturally "correct" option — but is new schema/application work, not a safe local fix, and risks re-introducing the exact "two systems" problem a third time if not scoped carefully | Requires a migration and a backfill decision from both existing tables | Both existing admin UIs would need to be rewritten against the new table | Public page rewritten once, correctly, against the new table |

**Recommendation (not a decision made on the user's behalf):** Option A has the smallest blast radius — `award_assignments.winner_position` is already correctly set by the already-tested, already-shipped Assignments-tab workflow (BTA-032/034), so making it canonical fixes the public display immediately for every assignment already positioned, without needing a backfill or touching historical `winners` rows at all. Option B requires a backfill to avoid regressing already-correct Assignments-tab data. Option C is the architecturally cleanest but is new work, not a fix. **Recorded as Decision register item 18; no option implemented.**

**Fork A — Season-stage configuration ("Enter Now" / "Vote Now" / "Order/Purchase Packages"): completed.**

Of the four date columns, only `entry_open_date` is ever read anywhere in application code, and only to drive a cosmetic pre-launch countdown (`home.js:852-876` → `window.initBTAEntryCountdown`) — it does not gate, hide, or change any button, CTA, or page content.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Enter Now | CTA shown while entries are open | Static "Enter Now" button, always present, always links to `submit-entry.html`, regardless of any date | Hardcoded button; the one date-aware element (the countdown) is cosmetic only | `home.html:2428,2448,2476,2505,2667`; `home.js:852-876` |
| Vote Now | Voting becomes available once entries close | **No gating of any kind** — `public-voting.js`, `vote.html`, `api/voting-proxy.js` contain zero references to any of the four season-date columns; voting is always available | Fully unimplemented | Exhaustive grep, confirmed zero matches beyond the one countdown read |
| Order/Purchase Packages | Post-voting, winners/packages focus | No such transition exists anywhere | Unimplemented | Same exhaustive grep |

Both are real, narrower gaps than the full stage system, but each still needs a decision on the exact enforcement behaviour (hard reject vs. a flag vs. leaving as-is) before any code changes — not assumed or implemented here.

Now wired to the same `get_public_data` endpoint for all 6 season-date fields, with the previous hardcoded text preserved only as the fallback for any date an admin hasn't set. See BTA-064 for full detail, tests, and verification limits.

This is the same finding as Fork A's season-stage sub-question (b) above, found independently from the content-inventory angle — corroborating evidence, not a new issue.
- **Confirmed gap, not fixed (needs a decision)**: `become-a-sponsor.html`'s sponsorship package tiers (names/pricing/benefits) are a separate, hardcoded 3-tier HTML block, entirely disconnected from the admin-managed, CRUD-editable `sponsorship_packages` table — changing a package's price in the CMS has zero effect on what a prospective sponsor actually sees.
- **Minor, confirmed, not fixed**: `become-a-sponsor.html`'s `<title>` hardcodes "2026" directly into the SEO title string (an annual-maintenance trap); 6 of ~16 public pages have no `<meta name="description">` at all, inconsistent with the other 4 that do.

**Fork C — Taxonomy/location architecture defect hunt: completed, one confirmed defect found and fixed (see BTA-062 below).** The `areas` table has a clean `area_type` discriminator (county/city/borough/region, `migrations/067-create-missing-areas-table.sql`) — no structural ambiguity there; `area_id` is a properly nullable FK on `award_years`/`organisations`; `findMatchingOrganisation()` is used identically and defensively (returns `null` rather than matching globally when area can't be resolved) by all legitimate call sites; `scripts/award-categories-config.js`'s `SMALL_COUNTIES` and `scripts/generate-awards.js` share one single import, not a second duplicate. **One real defect found**: `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleSubmitEntry` (the single most-used public entry point) matched awards on the legacy, usually-empty `awards.county` text column instead of `area_id` — a bug already found and fixed once before in two sibling code paths (`executeAwardAreaImport` in `api/data-proxy.js`, and `handleSubmitNomination` in the same file) but missed in this third, busiest path. Net effect: a real public entry submitted for any award created through the normal admin "Add Award" form (which only ever sets `area_id`, never `county`) would match **zero** award rows, silently falling through with no award linkage and no area-scoped organisation matching — no error surfaced to the submitter. **Fixed and tested, see BTA-062.** No other taxonomy/location code defect was found beyond the already-documented, correctly-undecided business-model tension (Decision register items 1-6) — the fork's own words: confirmed nothing else to report there.

**Fork D — Admin-CMS remaining-areas inventory: completed.** Events create/edit, certificate PDF generation (real pdf-lib pipeline, not a stub), media/entry uploads, judge-conflict admin review, and calendar/ICS export are all **working and verified**. `generate-certificate`/`generate-badge`/`generate-all-badges`/`verify-qr` remain confirmed present-but-unused (re-confirmed, unchanged since BTA-044). `scripts/csv-import-master.js` is a developer/ops-only Node CLI tool, not reachable from the admin web UI at all — not a bug, just precisely recorded. **Two confirmed reliability gaps found, neither attempted as a same-task fix given their complexity/risk:**
1. **Significant — event check-in uses a full delete-all-and-reinsert on every single attendee action** (`events.js:831-858` `saveAttendees()`, called from 8 sites including the actual door check-in actions). Every call wipes and reinserts **every** attendee row for the event, so each attendee gets a brand-new database ID every time *anyone's* record changes — `event_tickets.attendee_id` is an explicitly-commented "soft FK (no constraint)" to that ID, so a previously-issued ticket silently orphans on the next unrelated check-in. With two check-in stations active at once (the normal case at a real event), this is also a textbook lost-update race condition. **Not fixed** — the fork's own assessment, which this review agrees with, is that the correct fix (a targeted per-row insert/update/delete) is a real, non-trivial change that needs its own dedicated, carefully-tested task, not a same-pass correction alongside dozens of other findings. Recorded as the most operationally significant unfixed finding in this entire BTA-061 pass.
2. **GDPR erasure cascade has no transaction** (`gdpr.js:527-552`, 13 sequential delete calls with no rollback) — a failure partway through leaves a partially-erased, non-compliant record with no automatic recovery. Flagged as a compliance-relevant reliability gap, not fixed, for the same reason as above.

#### BTA-062 — Fix: `handleSubmitEntry` matched awards on the legacy `county` column instead of `area_id` (found by Fork C)

**Implemented and tested.** `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleSubmitEntry` (lines ~655-676) queried `awards` with `.eq('county', safe.countyCity)` — but `awards.js`'s `saveAward()` (the normal admin "Add/Edit Award" form) only ever sets `area_id`, never the legacy free-text `county` column (confirmed by reading its full `awardData` object directly — no `county:` key exists there at all). This exact bug was already found and independently fixed twice before in sibling code paths: `executeAwardAreaImport` (`api/data-proxy.js`) already matches on `area_id` with a comment explicitly documenting why; `handleSubmitNomination` (same file as the bug) independently resolves area via its own `areas` lookup rather than relying on `county` at all. Only the busiest of the three paths, `handleSubmitEntry`, was missed. Fixed by mirroring the already-established pattern exactly: resolve the submitted `county_city` to an `area_id` via an `areas.display_name` lookup (identical to `handleSubmitNomination`'s own resolution step), then match the award on `area_id` instead of `county`; if the area doesn't resolve, the award-match query is skipped entirely (consistent with `findMatchingOrganisation`'s existing "unresolvable area ⇒ never match" contract) rather than running a query that would behave unpredictably with a null filter. Updated 6 existing tests in `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` whose mocked call sequence shifted by one (the new `areas` lookup); added 2 new regression tests: one asserting the award-match query is actually called with `area_id` and never with `county`, and one asserting the award-match query is skipped entirely when the area can't be resolved. **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/entry-proxy.test.js` → 34/34 passed (2 new). `npx eslint api/entry-proxy.js tests/entry-proxy.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

**Real-world impact of this bug, stated plainly**: before this fix, almost every public entry submission for an award created through the normal admin UI (i.e., not CSV-imported) would silently fail to link to its award and skip area-scoped organisation matching — with no error ever shown to the submitter, and no admin-visible symptom beyond entries quietly missing their award linkage. Given `executeAwardAreaImport`'s own comment already flagged this exact risk for awards created via the Add Award form, and CSV import is the less-common path for most awards, this was likely affecting a meaningful fraction of real entries before this fix.

#### BTA-063 — Fix: `api/email-automation.js` read a non-existent column name, `winner_announcement_date` instead of `winners_announcement_date` (found by Fork B)

**Implemented and tested.** `award_years.winners_announcement_date` (plural) is the real column — confirmed in 4 separate migration files (`migrations/000-complete-database-setup.sql`, `069-refresh-awards-view.sql`, `add-prev-year-results.sql`, `create-award-seasons.sql`) and used correctly, consistently, and extensively in the admin UI (`awards.js`, `settings.js` — confirmed via grep, zero instances of the wrong spelling anywhere in admin code). `api/email-automation.js` read the singular `winner_announcement_date` in 5 places: `sendEntryConfirmation()` (2 uses, both already present before BTA-059's own edit to this function — that earlier fix inherited and extended the existing typo rather than introducing it), and `sendShortlistNotifications()` (3 uses, including a `.select('winner_announcement_date')` against `award_years` that selected a column that doesn't exist at all — this didn't error visibly because the destructuring (`const { data: activeSeason } = await supabase...`) never checked for a query error). Net effect: the real "Winners announced" date has always silently rendered as the hardcoded `'TBC'` fallback in every entry-confirmation and shortlist-notification email sent by this codebase, regardless of what an admin actually set. Fixed all 5 occurrences to the correct plural spelling. Fixed the 2 existing test fixtures (`tests/email-automation.test.js`) that used the same wrong spelling (one from BTA-059's own test, one pre-existing in the `sendShortlistNotifications` test) — the pre-existing one never asserted the actual rendered date value, only that an email was sent, which is precisely why this bug went undetected; strengthened it to assert the real date appears in the sent HTML and that `'TBC'` does not. **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/email-automation.test.js` → 38/38 passed. `npx eslint api/email-automation.js tests/email-automation.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

#### BTA-064 — Priority 1: Wire the homepage's Key Dates list and Vote box to the CMS-managed season dates

**Implemented and tested.** Traced the full chain: Settings → "Award Seasons" admin form (`settings.js:790-841`, `editSeason()`/`saveSeason()`) already provides full CRUD for every field needed (`entry_open_date`, `entry_close_date`, `nominees_announcement_date`, `voting_open_date`, `voting_close_date`, `winners_announcement_date` — all defined on `award_seasons`, `migrations/create-award-seasons.sql:9-16`) → `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleGetPublicData` (the single shared `get_public_data` endpoint `home.js` already calls for the countdown) previously selected and exposed only `entry_open_date` → `home.js`'s `initPublicData()` → `home.html`'s Key Dates list (4 hardcoded literal date ranges) and Vote box ("Voting runs 3 Aug to 1 Oct 2026", also hardcoded). Confirmed this was a pure wiring gap, not a missing-feature gap: every field needed already exists, is already admin-editable, and already flows correctly for `entry_open_date` alone (the countdown) — only the Key Dates list and Vote box text were never connected to it.

**Change made** (no new dates, no new business/voting rules invented, no new season-stage model introduced):
1. `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleGetPublicData` now selects and exposes all 6 season-date fields from the same single default-season query, under the **exact same existing safety rule** already established for `entry_open_date` (only exposed when exactly one default `award_seasons` row exists; never falls back to another season; never leaks how many seasons exist). Each field is surfaced independently — a season with only some dates filled in still exposes the ones it has, rather than being all-or-nothing.
2. `home.html`: added stable `id`s to the 4 Key Dates `<div class="key-date-label">` elements and wrapped the Vote box's date-range text in a `<span id="vote-box-dates">` — the existing hardcoded text in each now serves as the **fallback** shown until/unless JS replaces it, not a value that's always shown.
3. `home.js`'s `initPublicData()`: after the existing countdown logic (left untouched), now formats and writes each available date pair into its corresponding element — `entry_open_date`+`entry_close_date` → Key Dates "Entries" line and nowhere else; `nominees_announcement_date` → "Nominees Announced" line; `voting_open_date`+`voting_close_date` → both the Key Dates "Voting" line and the Vote box span; `winners_announcement_date` → "Winners Announced" line. Any date (or pair) the CMS hasn't set is simply left untouched, preserving the existing hardcoded fallback text exactly as before — matching the countdown's own already-established "stay hidden/unchanged if data is unavailable" convention, not inventing a new one.

**Tested, including the first-ever test coverage for `home.js`** (confirmed via `ls tests/*.test.js` — no `home.test.js` existed before this task, despite the file being 900+ lines with real logic). New `tests/home.test.js` (6 tests, following the exact jsdom-based, no-exports convention already established by `tests/public-winners-app.test.js`): all 6 fields set → all 5 elements updated with correctly formatted dates and the existing countdown call still fires correctly; no fields set → every fallback text preserved exactly; only entry dates set → only the Entries line updates, the other 3 stay on their fallback; a malformed date string → fallback preserved, no crash; a rejected fetch → fallback preserved, no throw; pre-existing `sponsors_visible` toggle behaviour confirmed still intact alongside the new code. Added 3 new regression tests to `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` for the extended `get_public_data` response (all 6 fields exposed when fully set; only-set fields exposed when partially set, rest null; all fields null when multiple default seasons exist — extending the exact same ambiguity-safety pattern already tested for `entry_open_date` alone to all 6 fields). **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/home.test.js tests/entry-proxy.test.js` → 43/43 passed (6 new in `home.test.js`, 3 new in `entry-proxy.test.js`). `npx eslint home.js api/entry-proxy.js tests/home.test.js tests/entry-proxy.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. **Full regression**: `npx jest` → 76 suites, 6615 tests, 6612 passed, 3 pre-existing skips, 0 failed. `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, 8 pre-existing warnings (none in a touched file). `node build.js` → succeeded.

**Verification limits, stated plainly**: this was verified via jsdom-based unit tests (DOM construction + mocked `fetch`, checking the resulting `textContent`), not a real browser — no visual/rendering verification, no check against a live Supabase `award_seasons` row, and no confirmation that an admin's actual edit in the Settings UI reaches this endpoint in production (the data path is the same already-proven `get_public_data` endpoint the countdown already uses successfully, but that is inference from an already-working sibling field, not independent proof for the 5 new ones). No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

**Not touched, correctly left alone**: the "Entries are now open" message and the countdown digits themselves (both already correct, pre-existing, not part of this gap); the season-stage gating question (Decision register item 20) — this task wires *display* of dates that already exist in the CMS, it does not add any entry/voting *enforcement* based on them, which remains the separate, still-undecided item.

#### BTA-065 — Priority 2: Sponsorship-package public connection — investigated, confirmed as a genuine blocker, not implemented

Recorded in full as **Decision register item 21** (added above the existing item 20) rather than duplicated here: investigated both `sponsorship_packages` (a per-organisation CRM deal record, not a public catalog — connecting it publicly would leak other sponsors' actual confidential terms) and `sponsorship_opportunities` (the right shape for a public catalog, but with zero admin UI and zero rows ever written to it, confirmed by exhaustive grep). Concluded that making `become-a-sponsor.html`'s tiers genuinely CMS-managed requires first building a new admin CRUD screen — real feature work needing its own scope decision, not a same-task wiring fix — so nothing was implemented. Three options recorded with their trade-offs; none chosen. No code changed, no tests needed (no behaviour changed); nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

#### BTA-066 — Priority 3: Fix event check-in data-integrity (delete-all-and-reinsert → targeted per-attendee operations)

**Implemented and tested.** Traced the complete attendee lifecycle across `events.js`'s "Attendees" modal (the admin-side RSVP/guest-list management screen — confirmed via `check-in-app.js`/`check-in.html` that the actual event-day door-scanning tool operates on a **separate table, `event_guests`**, with its own already-safe, targeted `.update({checked_in}).eq('id', guestId)` pattern; it was never affected by this bug). All 8 call sites of `saveAttendees(eventId, attendees)` (`addAttendee`, `updateAttendeeStatus`, a group-booking flow, `deleteAttendee`, `toggleCheckIn`, `checkInAll`, `promoteFromWaitlist`, and a CSV-import flow) were traced individually and found to need exactly one of three safe, targeted operations — none of them actually needed a full-list replace: `addAttendee`/group-booking/`promoteFromWaitlist`/CSV-import only ever **add** new rows, never mutating existing ones; `updateAttendeeStatus`/`toggleCheckIn` mutate **exactly one** existing row by its already-known, stable database id (confirmed via `getAttendees()`'s own id mapping that this id is real and stable once an attendee has been saved at least once — the only unstable id was `addAttendee`'s own transient client-side placeholder, discarded on the very next full-list save regardless); `deleteAttendee` removes exactly one row by id; `checkInAll` updates a **known subset** of existing rows with one shared patch.

**Fix**: added two new targeted helpers to `events.js` — `insertAttendees(eventId, attendees)` (bulk-inserts only the given new rows, touching nothing else) and `updateAttendeeRecord(attendeeId, patch)` (updates exactly one row by id, via `apiClient.update`, with the same JS-to-DB field-name mapping `saveAttendees()`/`getAttendees()` already use). Rewired all 8 call sites to use these instead of `saveAttendees`; `checkInAll` uses a single `apiClient.updateByFilters('event_attendees', { id: { in: [...] } }, { checked_in: true, check_in_time })` call, confirmed supported by `api/data-proxy.js`'s existing `in` filter operator (`:731-732`). `saveAttendees()` itself is left in place, unchanged, with a new comment explaining why none of its own callers use it anymore — kept rather than deleted, since it still has its own dedicated, valid unit tests (`tests/events.test.js`'s "Events Module - saveAttendees" block) as a correctly-documented "full replace" primitive that a genuine future bulk-replace use case could still reach for.

**Not touched, by design**: `event_guests`/`check-in-app.js` (confirmed already safe, out of scope); the two call sites that build genuinely new rows alongside existing, untouched ones (group-booking, CSV import) were fixed to insert-only but were not given new dedicated tests this task (no pre-existing tests covered them either — flagged as a smaller follow-up, not a regression risk, since their fix is the same, simpler "insert only" pattern already thoroughly tested via `addAttendee`/`toggleCheckIn`/`checkInAll`).

**Tests**: fixed 3 existing tests whose mocks assumed the old delete+insert call shape (`deleteAttendee removes attendee...`, `updateAttendeeStatus modifies attendee...` ×2) to assert the new targeted `apiClient.update`/`apiClient.delete` calls instead; strengthened `addAttendee`'s existing test to assert `deleteByFilters` is no longer called and that the insert payload is correct. **Added first-ever test coverage for `toggleCheckIn`/`checkInAll`** (6 new tests, previously completely untested despite being the exact functions this bug report was about): toggling one attendee updates only that attendee and leaves `deleteByFilters`/`insert` uncalled; toggling an already-checked-in attendee undoes it (repeated/idempotent toggling); an unknown attendee id calls nothing; bulk check-in updates only the unchecked *attending* guests (not already-checked-in or non-attending ones) in one `updateByFilters` call with the exact expected `id: {in: [...]}` filter, never touching `deleteByFilters`/`insert`; bulk check-in with nothing to do shows an info toast and calls nothing; cancelling the confirmation dialog calls nothing. **Verification limits, stated plainly**: these are sequential jsdom/mock unit tests — they confirm each call site now sends exactly one targeted, correctly-shaped API call instead of a full delete-and-reinsert, which is what eliminates the id-churn/orphaning risk; they do **not** and cannot prove true concurrent-request safety between two simultaneous check-in stations, since that requires a real database/HTTP race, not a mocked sequential test. **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/events.test.js` → 371/371 passed (6 new, 3 fixed). `npx eslint events.js tests/events.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. **Full regression**: `npx jest` → 76 suites, 6621 tests, 6618 passed, 3 pre-existing skips, 0 failed. `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, 8 pre-existing warnings (none in a touched file). `node build.js` → succeeded. No database, migration, or Supabase change; nothing staged, committed, or pushed.

**Remaining, correctly not attempted this task**: `event_tickets.attendee_id`'s "soft FK (no constraint)" (`database-phase2-tables-setup.sql:13`) is unchanged — this fix removes the *cause* of routine id churn (so tickets issued today will no longer be orphaned by an unrelated attendee's later check-in), but does not retroactively repair any ticket that was already orphaned by the bug before this fix, and does not add a real foreign-key constraint (a schema change requiring its own migration decision, not attempted or authored this task since it wasn't requested and isn't needed to fix the behavioural bug itself).

#### BTA-067 — Priority 4: GDPR erasure made atomic; a related, newly-found RPC security gap fixed

**Implemented (code + an authored, unapplied migration) and tested.** Traced the full erasure flow: `processRequest('delete', entityId)` → `_deleteEntityData(entityId)` — previously 13 sequential, unguarded `apiClient.deleteByFilters()` calls, one per table (`email_logs`, `event_guests`, `public_votes`, `organisation_contacts`, `award_assignments`, `entries`, `organisation_images`, `crm_communications`, `crm_deals`, `crm_meetings`, `organisation_notes`, `organisation_follow_ups`, `organisations`, in that order), each a separate HTTP round-trip with no transaction across them — a failure partway through left a partially-erased, non-compliant record with no rollback. **Confirmed `invoices`/`payments` are deliberately excluded from this list already** (present in `_exportEntityData`'s export but absent from the delete cascade) — read as an existing, sensible financial-retention decision already baked into the code, not a bug; left unchanged, and not newly "fixed" to delete them, since inventing a retention policy is explicitly out of scope for this task.

**Fix, following existing precedent exactly, not a new architecture**: this file already has a sibling GDPR action, `'anonymize'`, that calls a database function (`anonymize_organisation`, migration 046) via `apiClient.rpc(...)` instead of client-side table operations — the correct existing pattern for "this needs to be atomic," since a Postgres function body runs inside the calling transaction and rolls back entirely on any error, with no explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` needed. Authored `migrations/089-secure-gdpr-erasure-rpc.sql` (**not applied to any database**) defining `erase_organisation_gdpr(p_org_id UUID)`, performing the exact same 13-statement cascade in the exact same order as the code it replaces — no table added, removed, or reordered. Changed `gdpr.js`'s `_deleteEntityData` to a single `await apiClient.rpc('erase_organisation_gdpr', { p_org_id: entityId })` call. Added `erase_organisation_gdpr: 'super_admin'` to `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_RPCS` map, matching `anonymize_organisation`'s existing role requirement for the sibling action.

**Related security gap found and fixed in the same migration**: while authoring this, checked whether `anonymize_organisation` (the sibling, pre-existing RPC) has ever had its direct PostgREST execute privilege restricted — it has not, confirmed by grep across every migration file. This is the **exact same vulnerability class as migration 081** (already tracked in this task sheet): Postgres auto-exposes every function as a public `/rest/v1/rpc/<name>` endpoint regardless of `api/data-proxy.js`'s own `ALLOWED_RPCS` role gate, so `anonymize_organisation` — a real, irreversible data-redaction action — has likely been directly callable by the anon/authenticated key this whole time, bypassing the app's `super_admin` requirement entirely, the same way migration 081 found for the email-sending functions. Migration 089 also revokes execute on `anonymize_organisation` (alongside the new function) from `PUBLIC`/`anon`/`authenticated`, restricting both to `service_role` only — **not applied**, same as migration 081/086/087/088, needing the same test-then-production authorisation process. This is a new, previously-untracked finding, not a re-statement of 081.

**A real deployment-sequencing dependency, stated plainly, not glossed over**: because the RPC this task wires `gdpr.js` to does not yet exist in any live database, the GDPR "delete" action will **fail outright** (a clear RPC-not-found error, surfaced via `processRequest`'s existing catch block) until migration 089 is applied — it will not silently fall back to the old, unsafe per-table loop (that code path no longer exists). This is a deliberate choice: failing loudly and erasing nothing is safer than the previous behaviour of possibly erasing some tables but not others. **Migration 089 must be applied to the test project (and verified) before this code change is deployed anywhere it could actually be exercised** — until then, this is authored-but-inert, exactly like 086/087/088's own "not yet live" status, except this one also requires the application code and the migration to ship together, not independently.

**Tests**: rewrote `tests/gdpr.test.js`'s "`_deleteEntityData`" describe block (previously asserting the 13-call sequential pattern) to assert the new behaviour: a single `apiClient.rpc('erase_organisation_gdpr', { p_org_id })` call with zero `apiClient.deleteByFilters` calls; and that an RPC failure (including, explicitly, the exact "function does not exist" error this will produce before migration 089 is applied) propagates rather than being masked. **Not added** (an honestly-scoped limit, not an oversight): a dedicated `api/data-proxy.js`-level test confirming `erase_organisation_gdpr`'s `super_admin` role gate — checked and found that **no RPC entry in `ALLOWED_RPCS` has ever had its own role-gate unit test** (the mocked Supabase client in `tests/data-proxy.test.js` has no `rpc()` method defined at all), a pre-existing gap across the whole RPC mechanism, not something newly introduced here; backfilling it for every RPC is a larger, separate testing-infrastructure task, flagged here rather than attempted partially. **Tests run**: `npx jest tests/gdpr.test.js` → 83/83 passed. `npx eslint gdpr.js api/data-proxy.js tests/gdpr.test.js` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. **Full regression**: `npx jest` → 76 suites, 6621 tests, 6618 passed, 3 pre-existing skips, 0 failed. `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, 8 pre-existing warnings (none in a touched file). `node build.js` → succeeded. No database write, migration application, or git action occurred.



 verify the exact affected tables, existing policies and grants, and any legitimate non-frontend dependencies as far as the available evidence permits." This re-runs and extends BTA-052's queries with more completeness and one new dependency category (Edge Functions / server-side `api/` code), strictly read-only, against the **test project only** (`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`) — no write, no production connection.

**(a) Full, re-confirmed policy list for all 22 tables** (not just confirming the one permissive policy's presence, but every policy on each table): matches BTA-052's finding exactly — each table has the single `"Allow all access to <table>"` permissive policy (`cmd=ALL`, `roles={public}`, `qual=true`); 13 of the 22 (`event_budgets`, `event_budget_items`, `event_milestones`, `event_post_data`, `event_special_requirements`, `event_tickets`, `event_vendors`, `event_waitlist`, `notification_queue`, `notifications`, `org_activity_notes`, `scheduled_reports`, `tenant_branding`) additionally carry one or two later, narrower policies (`"Allow anon read on <table>"` / `"Allow authenticated full access on <table>"`, or the `_select`/`_service` pairs on `notifications`/`org_activity_notes`/`tenant_branding`) that never took effect because the old permissive one was never dropped — exactly as BTA-052 reported, now re-confirmed with the complete per-table policy list rather than a summary.
- **(b) Full, re-confirmed grants** for `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` on all 22 tables: `anon` and `authenticated` both hold the complete `SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER` set on every one of the 22 (confirmed table-by-table, not sampled); `service_role` also holds the full set on all 22, as expected — migration 088 does not touch `service_role`'s grants at all, only `anon`/`authenticated`.
- **(c) New this task — dependent-views check**: queried `information_schema.view_table_usage` for any view defined on top of any of the 22 tables — **zero rows returned**. No view (and therefore no `security_invoker` consideration, unlike `email_list_members`/migration 083's situation) depends on any of these 22 tables.
- **(d) New this task — triggers check**: 9 of the 22 tables (`event_budget_items`, `event_budgets`, `event_milestones`, `event_post_data`, `event_special_requirements`, `event_tickets`, `event_vendors`, `event_waitlist`, `scheduled_reports`) carry one `update_<table>_updated_at` trigger each, all enabled (`tgenabled='O'`) — standard `updated_at`-maintenance triggers, unrelated to RLS/grants and unaffected by migration 088 (it changes policies/grants, not triggers).
- **(e) New this task — non-frontend dependency check, beyond the already-completed frontend grep**: grepped `supabase/functions/` and `api/` for `.from('<table>')` against all 22 names. Found and traced the only two genuine non-frontend dependencies that exist:
  - `supabase/functions/_shared/email-wrapper.ts:167`'s `fetchBranding()` reads `tenant_branding` — but it accepts an already-constructed `supabaseClient` as a parameter; traced both of its two callers, `supabase/functions/send-campaign/index.ts:38-41` and `supabase/functions/send-entry-confirmation/index.ts:29-32`, and **confirmed both construct that client with `Deno.env.get('SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY')`**, not the anon key — so this dependency is on the **service role**, which migration 088 does not revoke anything from. **Not broken by migration 088.** (These two Edge Functions are the same ones already flagged in BTA-050/052 as having no internal auth check of their own and relying entirely on the platform `verify_jwt` setting — that finding is unrelated to and unaffected by this one.)
  - `api/stripe-payment.js` (`event_ticket_types`, `notifications`), `api/_lib/automation-scheduler.js` + `api/resend-email.js` (`notification_queue`), `api/email-automation.js` + `api/resend-email.js` + `api/stripe-payment.js` (`tenant_branding`) — all of these are server-side `/api/` handlers, which (per this project's established, already-verified architecture) construct their Supabase client with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` throughout, the same service-role pattern as the Edge Functions above. **Confirmed not broken by migration 088** for the same reason — informational, not a new risk.
- **Conclusion, now with deepened evidence**: migration 088 remains safe to apply to the test project once separately authorised. All 22 tables' current exposure is re-confirmed at the full-detail level (not just summarised); zero dependent views; triggers are unaffected; and the only two real non-frontend dependencies found (one Edge-Function, several `api/` handlers) both use the service-role key, which migration 088 leaves fully untouched. **Still not applied — this task made no write of any kind.**

**Tests run**: none apply (read-only SQL queries plus repository grep/read; no code changed). No database write, migration application, secret exposure, or git action occurred.
