#### Migration 086 verification package (prepared BTA-035, finalised BTA-039, re-reviewed and confirmed complete BTA-043 — no Supabase connection made for any of this)

**BTA-043 re-review:** checked this package against its own six-point requirement list (project identity; minimal read-only queries for policies/privileges; expected results for applied/absent/partial; the `email_list_members`/migration-083 `security_invoker` check; pre-flight guidance; exact permission needed) — all six are already present below, unchanged. No gaps found; nothing added or removed.

**1. Intended project:** the repository's documented test project is **`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`** ("Claude TEST CMS > Website", Section A). Run every query below against **this project only**. Production (`qdzyknercdqwhwijbcxf`) is not touched until the test result is reviewed and a separate, explicit decision is made to proceed.

**2. Minimum read-only queries (run as a role that can read `pg_policies`/`information_schema`, e.g. via the Supabase SQL editor or an MCP `execute_sql`-style read-only call — never with credentials printed into any file or chat):**
```sql
-- (a) Current policies on the table migration 086 changes
SELECT policyname, cmd, roles, qual
FROM pg_policies
WHERE schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'email_list_subscribers';

-- (b) Current anon/authenticated grants on that table
SELECT grantee, privilege_type
FROM information_schema.role_table_grants
WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_name = 'email_list_subscribers'
  AND grantee IN ('anon', 'authenticated');

-- (c) Additional check (identified this task): confirm migration 083's
-- security_invoker setting actually reached the one view this table feeds,
-- since migration 086's own design assumes it did (see point 5 below).
SELECT c.relname, c.reloptions
FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'public' AND c.relname = 'email_list_members';
```

**3. Expected outputs and how to interpret them:**
- **Absent (migration never applied):** (a) returns exactly one row — `policyname = "Allow all access to email_list_subscribers"`, `cmd = ALL`, `roles = {public}`. (b) returns rows for both `anon` and `authenticated` covering `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` (and `TRUNCATE`, if the Postgres version/role surfaces it in this view). (c) returns one row for `email_list_members`; its `reloptions` should already show `security_invoker=true` (migration 083, a separate, earlier migration — its own status is independent of 086's).
- **Applied correctly:** (a) returns exactly two rows — `service_role_all` and `no_direct_client_access`, and the old permissive policy is **absent**. (b) returns **zero rows** for `anon`/`authenticated`. (c) unaffected by 086 either way (083 is a separate migration on a different object).
- **Partially applied** (a real possibility, since the migration runs several DDL statements that are not wrapped in one transaction in the file as written): e.g. the old policy already dropped but the new ones not yet created, or policies correct but grants not yet revoked (or vice versa) — any state where (a) and (b) don't both match one of the two clean patterns above. If this is what's found, do not assume the rest completed silently; treat it as its own finding and re-run each statement individually rather than re-running the whole file.

**4. Additional checks needed before this migration is considered safe to apply (identified this task, not run):**
- Confirm migration 083 is actually applied to the same project first (query (c) above) — 086's safety reasoning (BTA-027's header comment: the `email_list_members` view "reads through to this table's own, now-hardened, restrictions") depends on that view already running with `security_invoker = true`. If 083 is unapplied, the view may still run with the view owner's privileges after 086 tightens the base table, which would need separate evaluation, not just being applied alongside 086 by assumption.
- Confirm no other legitimate, currently-working feature queries `email_list_subscribers` directly as `anon`/`authenticated` that BTA-025/026's path trace missed — re-grep `test-data-manager.js` and any remaining `.from('email_list_subscribers')` call in client-side (non-`api/`) `.js` files immediately before applying, since those paths are what the revoke would break.

**5. Exact permission needed:** explicit authorisation to open a **read-only** Supabase connection to the **test** project (`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`) and run queries (a)–(c) above — nothing else, no write, no RLS/grant change, no production connection. Applying the migration itself is a separate, later decision this package does not make or recommend.

**BTA-035's wider CMS-completion survey (its own task prompt's area 4):** checked the project's own documented backlogs rather than writing a new one — `TECHNICAL-DEBT-TODO.md` (19/19 checked), `UX-AUDIT-TODO.md` (360/360), `DEEP-AUDIT-TODO.md` (34/34) and `PROFESSIONAL-AUDIT-TODO.md` (20/20) are all fully complete. `CMS-AUDIT-TODO.md` has exactly 6 of 118 items still unchecked (10.1–10.6) — **every one of them is marked "⚠️ LIVE TEST REQUIRED"** in the file itself: send a real email via Resend, trigger a real Stripe checkout via `stripe listen`, upload to Supabase Storage and confirm from the bucket, confirm production login against live Supabase Auth, run AI vetting (needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, listed "Not Yet Configured" in `CLAUDE.md`), and post to live social-media platforms (needs per-platform keys, also "Not Yet Configured"). A repo-wide grep for `TODO`/`FIXME`/`NOT IMPLEMENTED` across the JS source found **zero** self-flagged incomplete work. **Conclusion: the project's own documented completion backlogs are exhausted except for these 6 items, and all 6 require either a live Supabase/Stripe/Resend connection or external platform credentials this task was not authorised to use.** This is not a new finding to fix — it is the evidence that no further safe, independent, bounded implementation work is available right now without one of: a Supabase/external-service connection, a credential that is not configured, or a business/architecture decision (tenant isolation; shield/certificate visual redesign). See the final report for the precise decision/authorisation needed for each.

Recommend **BTA-035 — Verify (and, only if separately authorised, apply) migration 086 against the test Supabase project (`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`)**: connect read-only first and query `pg_policies` for `email_list_subscribers` to establish, with evidence, whether the old permissive policy or the new `service_role_all`/`no_direct_client_access` policies are currently in place, before any write is considered. **Is tenant isolation safe under the documented deployment model? Yes, as of BTA-048.** The documented/actual deployment is single-tenant (one seeded tenant, 'bta'; no membership table ever existed; no live UI path ever requests a different tenant). Before this task, the server still *honoured* a client-supplied `tenantId` for scoping if one was sent, with no check that the requester belonged to it — a latent trust-boundary defect, exploitable only via a direct/crafted API call (never through the live app). `api/data-proxy.js` now rejects any `tenantId` other than `'default'` outright, at the one shared validation point plus the one independent code path (`award_area_import`) that bypassed it. This fully removes the exploitable path for the single-tenant deployment this app actually runs today. It does **not** by itself make multi-tenancy safe to enable — that still needs the membership mechanism described below — but no code anywhere currently depends on multi-tenancy working, so nothing is lost by closing it.

- None — everything implemented across BTA-036–049 has passing targeted tests, lint, and (for the JS changes) a successful full-suite run and build.

Confirmed via grep that neither function is called anywhere in the current live frontend, and neither has any internal authorisation check of its own — so `verify_jwt` is the *only* thing that would stop anyone holding the project's public anon key (visible in any page's source) from invoking `send-campaign` directly and mass-sending to a real subscriber list, independent of whether the app's own UI ever uses that path. `EMAIL_AUTOMATION_SETUP.md` (which describes deploying/using `send-entry-confirmation`) has been annotated as describing a superseded architecture. **Exact dashboard check needed**: Supabase Dashboard → Edge Functions → `send-campaign` and `send-entry-confirmation` → confirm "Enforce JWT Verification" is ON for both; cannot be checked via SQL or MCP tooling available in this session.

If yes, the minimum additional work (not started, not specified anywhere, and not something to build speculatively) is: (1) a table binding an authenticated user/email to one or more tenant IDs, (2) deriving `tenantId` server-side from that binding instead of trusting the client, (3) a UI to actually switch/assign tenants (currently absent — `switchTenant()` has no caller). None of this is needed for the CMS as it is actually used today.

#### D.4.1 — BTA-007–021 recovery attempt via Git history only (BTA-029, 2026-10-27)

**Scope note added by BTA-030:** everything in this subsection is accurate but **scoped to Git evidence only**. BTA-029's own conclusion ("unrecoverable from available evidence") incorrectly generalised a true git-specific negative result into a false blanket one. The actual task detail was recovered in BTA-030 from this session's local transcript — see Section D.4.0 above. This subsection remains correct and useful as the git-side half of the picture (confirming these tasks left no commit trail, which is itself a real, still-true fact) and is preserved unedited below.

**Method, in the priority order the task required:** (1) `git log --all --oneline -i --grep=...` across every local and remote ref (read-only; no fetch, no branch switch); (2) existing repository documentation already read in earlier BTA tasks this session (`SECURITY-SWEEP-FINAL-2026-07.md`, migration file header comments); (3) the migration files themselves; (4) this session's own direct knowledge.

**Confirmed negative finding (Verified):** `git log --all --oneline -i --grep="BTA-0"` was run across **every** local branch and **every** remote-tracking ref present in this checkout (`main`, `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, and 17 other `origin/claude/*` branches — the same branch set as the original reconstruction's 16-branch search, plus one). The **only** match anywhere in this repository's history is BTA-028's own preservation commit (`44de8ee8`, which happens to mention "BTA-027" in its message). **No commit anywhere in this repository, on any ref, is labelled with or references a BTA-007 through BTA-021 task ID.** This is consistent with — and is now direct confirmation of — the hypothesis already recorded in Section D.4's confidence note: these were read-only, chat-report-only audit tasks that produced no file changes and therefore could never leave a commit trail. **This detail remains genuinely unrecoverable from git history, not merely unsearched.**

**What WAS found (Verified git history — real, dated, substantial pre-existing commits directly relevant to the subject matter BTA-007–027 investigated, but NOT themselves BTA-numbered and NOT claimed to be BTA-007–021):**

Only test-data-manager.js and diagnose.js retain direct calls (intentional)" — this is the documented **origin** of exactly the "test-data-manager.js is the one remaining intentional exception" fact BTA-025 independently re-derived from source five months later, not a newly-introduced gap. Also the origin of `TENANT_SCOPED_TABLES`/`tenantModule`-driven scoping itself ("Client sends tenantId via apiClient (auto-populated from tenantModule)") — the same mechanism BTA-025/026 found relies on an unauthenticated `localStorage` value. **The tenant-isolation deferred finding therefore has a real, dated origin commit, even though it has no BTA task ID** — it was a deliberate 2026-03-07 fix whose residual gap (client-controlled tenant selection) went unnoticed until BTA-025. BTA-055's exhaustive repo-wide grep found **zero occurrences** of `results_status`, `finalised`, or `finalized` anywhere in this repository's SQL or JS source. The actual field governing an award year's lifecycle is `award_years.status` (`'Draft'`/`'Active'`/`'Archived'`/`'pending'`), a publish-state concept, not a results-finalisation one. **Not resolved by this review** — reported per this project's own standing rule to surface a task-sheet/prompt-vs-repository contradiction rather than silently resolve it. Possible explanations, none assumed: the results-finalisation concept was described as intended but never actually implemented; it exists under a different, not-yet-found name; or the business rule as stated is simply inaccurate for this codebase. **Needs the user's clarification before this review can confidently classify anything downstream of "results finalisation" (e.g., whether/how public result visibility is gated).**
18. **Which table is the canonical source of a winner's result/placement — `award_assignments` or `winners`?** BTA-056 confirmed both exist, are both actively written by different admin UIs (`assignments.js`'s `setWinnerPosition()` vs. `winner-pipeline.js`'s `confirmWinner()`), and are never synced — the public site reads only `winners.placement`, which no writer ever sets, so every published result currently displays as "Winner" regardless of actual position. Not re-designed or guessed at here; needs a decision on which table is canonical (with the other either deriving from it or being retired) before a correct fix can be implemented. **Likely the single highest-impact confirmed defect found in this entire BTA-007–060 review.** **BTA-060 prepared the explicitly-requested 3-option comparison** (Option A: `award_assignments` canonical, smallest blast radius, no backfill needed; Option B: `winners` canonical, needs a backfill from `award_assignments`; Option C: a new genuine source of truth, most correct but new work) with effects on existing records/admin workflow/public display for each — recommended Option A but did not decide.
19. **What should `nominate.html` actually collect?** BTA-056 confirmed its current payload doesn't satisfy the backend's required fields, so every submission 400s today. Needs a decision: add the missing fields to the form (award category, nominee company) to match the existing backend contract, or relax the backend's requirements to match the form's current, deliberately lighter-weight fields. Not guessed at here. **BTA-060 traced the exact root cause via git history**: `nominate.html` was deliberately rebuilt as a simpler form in commit `ca18254` (2026-06-16) when the original category-aware wizard was split off to `industry-leader.html` (which still works), but the simple form was never given a matching backend path — it has never worked since that commit. Refined to three concrete options: (1) add the missing fields to `nominate.html` to match the existing rich contract; (2) give the simple form its own, lighter backend action for admin triage; (3) relax `handleSubmitNomination`'s validation conditionally — flagged as carrying the most downstream data-integrity risk of the three. No option chosen.
20. **Season-stage gating ("Enter Now" / "Vote Now" / "Order/Purchase Packages") — confirmed entirely unimplemented, BTA-061.** No code anywhere computes a "current stage," and entry/voting submission are never gated by their own season dates (`award_years.entry_close_date`/`voting_open_date`/`voting_close_date` are read nowhere outside one cosmetic countdown). Building the full 3-stage system is new functionality requiring exact transition-rule decisions (automatic-by-date vs. admin-toggled; what each stage changes on each page) — not attempted. Two narrower, more tractable sub-questions, each still needing a decision before any code change: (a) should entry submission reject/flag entries submitted after `entry_close_date` (currently accepted unconditionally)? (b) should voting reject/flag votes outside `voting_open_date`/`voting_close_date` (currently also unconditional)? Recommend resolving (a)/(b) first, independently of the larger 3-stage UI question, since they're pure backend-validation decisions with no UI/CMS-editability dependency.
21. **`become-a-sponsor.html`'s sponsorship tiers cannot be safely connected to a real CMS catalog today — confirmed genuine blocker, BTA-064, not a wiring gap after all.** Investigated both candidate tables: `sponsorship_packages` (`organisations.js:7719-7792`) is scoped to `organisation_id` — it is a CRM record of an **already-signed sponsor's own deal** (amount, payment schedule, renewal date), not a public catalog of purchasable tiers, and exposing it publicly would leak other sponsors' actual confidential deal terms. `sponsorship_opportunities` (`database-crm-setup.sql`/migration 046) has exactly the right shape for a public catalog (`opportunity_name`, `description`, `tier`, `value`, `benefits`, `is_active`, `valid_from`/`valid_until`, `total_slots`/`available_slots`) — but an exhaustive repo-wide grep found **zero JS or HTML references to it anywhere**: no admin UI exists to create, edit, or view a single row, and the table itself (per the already-completed BTA-052 Supabase security work) has never had anything written to it. Wiring the public page to this table today would make it render an empty state, since nothing can populate it through any existing screen. **This is not a "smallest coherent correction"** — the only way to deliver working public package display is to first build a genuinely new admin CRUD screen for `sponsorship_opportunities` (what fields, where in the nav, who can edit it), which is new feature work requiring its own scope decision, not a same-task wiring fix. **Not implemented.** Options: (a) build the new `sponsorship_opportunities` admin screen, then wire the public page to it (most correct, most work); (b) leave `become-a-sponsor.html`'s tiers as deliberately-curated, hand-maintained marketing copy, accepting they are a different concept from the CRM's per-sponsor records (least work, matches the apparent original design intent, but keeps routine price/benefit changes as a code edit); (c) something in between (e.g. a simple settings-table-backed JSON blob for the 3 tier definitions, without the full `sponsorship_opportunities` model's slots/events/awards linkage). No option chosen or implemented. **BTA-069 addendum:** direct re-verification found `sponsorship_opportunities` actually has **two conflicting `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` definitions** with incompatible column names (`database-crm-setup.sql:215` uses `opportunity_name`/`value`/`total_slots`/`available_slots`/`event_id`/`award_id`; `migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql:134` uses `name`/`price`/`max_sponsors`/`current_sponsors`, with no event/award link at all) — whichever ran first against a given database silently wins, so the live column shape is unknown without a direct check. This blocks option (a) until resolved: building an admin screen now would risk assuming the wrong column names. See BTA-069 Section L.8 for full detail.

## F. Evidence and validation

### F.1 — Migration/schema comparison: `migrations/058-missing-tables-and-columns.sql` vs. `database-sectors-categories.sql`

**Completed investigation, recorded here as evidence for BTA-004/BTA-005 — not a new project objective.** Both files were read in full; this is local file + current-codebase evidence only. **No Supabase connection was attempted and no SQL was executed.**

| Object | `migrations/058...sql` | `database-sectors-categories.sql` |
|---|---|---|
| `custom_sectors` | `id`, `name TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE`, `description`, `is_active`, `created_at NOT NULL` (L143–149) | `id`, `name text NOT NULL` (uniqueness via separate `lower(name)` index, L22), `sort_order`, `is_active`, `created_at` nullable (L2–8) |
| `custom_categories` | `id`, `sector_id UUID REFERENCES custom_sectors(id)` (relational FK), `name`, `description`, `is_active`, `created_at NOT NULL` (L151–158) | `id`, `sector_name text NOT NULL` (free-text match, no FK — its own comment says it matches either a hardcoded sector *or* a `custom_sectors.name`), `name`, `available_for_small`, `sort_order`, `is_active`, `created_at` nullable (L11–19) |
| RLS | Enabled, 3 policies each (service_role/authenticated/anon) | None at all |

**Findings:**
- The two files define **incompatible shapes** for both tables — not additive variants. Because both use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, whichever runs first on a given database fully determines the table's shape; the other's columns are never added.
- **Current application code was checked directly and conclusively prefers the `database-sectors-categories.sql` model**, not migration 058's: `api/entry-proxy.js:564` and `api/voting-proxy.js:392` `SELECT ... sector_name` (not `sector_id`); `awards.js:505`/`settings.js:1797` insert `custom_sectors` with `sort_order` (058 has no such column); `awards.js:556-561`/`settings.js:1914-1919` insert `custom_categories` with `sector_name`, `available_for_small`, `sort_order` (058 has none of these).
- The app's live small-area category filter (`submit-entry.js:577`, `submit-entry-payment.js:427`: `if (isSmall && !c.available_for_small) return;`) depends entirely on `available_for_small` — a column only `database-sectors-categories.sql` defines.
- The application code also conditionally inserts `country`, `region_id`, `area_id` on `custom_categories` (`settings.js:1914-1921`) — columns **neither file defines**. The live schema the app expects has evolved past both files on record, from a source not identified in this comparison.
- **Neither file should be run as-is.** Recommended (not applied): inspect live `information_schema.columns` for both tables first, then write one new migration reconciling to whatever the app code actually requires — which the file/code evidence points toward being closer to `database-sectors-categories.sql`'s model plus the undocumented `country`/`region_id`/`area_id` columns.
- **This comparison does not establish the live Supabase schema.** Which shape (058's, the other file's, or a third, undocumented one) is actually live is unknown without a fresh, authorized connection.
- **The `areas.is_small` policy question (Decision register item 1) is untouched by this comparison** and remains open.

## G. Git and recovery history

### G.1 — PR #308

`https://github.com/billy-strummer/awards-cms/pull/308`, titled *"Fix organisation-matching cross-area collision and ILIKE wildcard bugs"* — **a misleading title for its actual scope.** Open, unmerged. As of its last check (not re-verified this task), GitHub reported `mergeable_state: dirty`. This means only: GitHub detected a mergeability problem at that time; it does **not** establish conflict count/nature, does **not** mean the PR is unusable, and does **not** by itself justify merging, splitting, replacing, or closing it — any such decision needs a fresh reassessment against the then-current `main`.

At last check: 72 commits, 147 changed files, +9,541/−16,147 lines — roughly three months of accumulated branch work (migrations, Award Areas CSV import, judge-portal/mobile fixes, User Management, Vercel Cron automation, Sentry/OG-tag fixes, documentation suite), of which organisation-matching is only the latest addition. Never received human review. Has a disclosed legacy-data limitation: pre-fix organisations with `area_id IS NULL` won't be matched by the new area-scoped lookup, risking duplicates on resubmission (documented by the PR itself as a non-blocking follow-up).

**Unreviewed areas:** migration changes bundled in the PR; Award Areas/CSV import; judge portal/mobile fixes; User Management; Vercel Cron automation; security/operational docs; general compatibility with current `main`.

## H. Persistence state

Three distinct levels, and what each actually guarantees for a **future, possibly-fresh** Claude session or container:

- **Working-tree only (uncommitted):** the file exists on disk right now but is invisible to `git log`/`git show` and **will be lost** if this container is recreated. This is the current state of both `BTA-CMS-PROJECT-TASKS.md` and the `CLAUDE.md` edit made in this task.
- **Committed locally:** the file is in this container's local git history, retrievable by `git show`/`git log` from this container — but a **new** container that only clones from `origin` will not have it, since nothing has been pushed.
- **Pushed to the intended remote branch:** retrievable by any future session that can access `origin/claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza` (or wherever it's pushed), subject to normal remote availability/permissions.

**Current actual state, verified that task (now superseded — see below):** both `BTA-CMS-PROJECT-TASKS.md` and the `CLAUDE.md` continuity-protocol addition were **currently staged** (`git status`: `A BTA-CMS-PROJECT-TASKS.md`, `M CLAUDE.md`) — **not yet committed, not pushed**. No upstream branch was even configured for this branch (`git rev-parse @{u}` failed — "no upstream configured"), so a push would have needed an explicit target spelled out, not just `git push`.

**Updated current actual state, verified in the continuity audit on 2026-10-27 (this supersedes the paragraph above — kept for history, not because it is still accurate):** the claim above is now stale. Both files **were in fact committed** — they are part of commit `ba6c9810c622c58e0c469b3844d86690070ebc7d` ("docs: save BTA CMS project continuity checkpoint") — and that commit **is** the current HEAD, which **is pushed**: `origin/claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza` matches HEAD exactly (`git rev-list --left-right --count` → `0  0`), confirmed fresh in this task. So this task sheet, as it stood at the end of the task that wrote the paragraph above, actually reached the **"pushed to the intended remote branch"** level — the strongest of the three this section defines — genuinely safe against container loss. This is a normal, expected kind of staleness (a file's own prose, written just before its final commit, necessarily describes the parent HEAD at authoring time, not the hash the commit will end up with) — not evidence of a new loss.

**However**, everything implemented *since* that checkpoint — the entire BTA-007–BTA-027 workstream's actual code output, i.e. BTA-027's five files (`api/data-proxy.js`, `test-data-manager.js`, `tests/data-proxy.test.js`, `tests/test-data-manager.test.js`; `migrations/086-harden-email-list-subscribers-rls.sql`) — was at the **working-tree-only** level at the time this paragraph was written: present on disk in this container, invisible to `git log`, and at risk of loss if the container were recreated before it was committed and pushed.

**Resolved in BTA-028, re-verified in BTA-029:** those five files, plus the then-current `BTA-CMS-PROJECT-TASKS.md`, were committed as `44de8ee8a58aa242844571876f43dab992d0af0d` ("Preserve BTA-027 email fixture hardening and project records") and pushed to `origin/claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`. BTA-029 independently re-confirmed this is still true: `git show --stat` on that commit lists exactly those six paths, and `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza` returns the same hash as local HEAD. **BTA-027's implementation has therefore reached the "pushed to the intended remote branch" level** — the strongest of the three, genuinely safe against container loss. This BTA-029 edit to this file is, in turn, working-tree-only until it is itself committed and pushed (not done by this task, per its own instructions).

**A report, summary, or task sheet described only in a chat conversation — on this platform or any other, including a ChatGPT Library — is not automatically available to a new Claude session**, exactly as Section G.3 demonstrated in practice.

**Committing and pushing require your explicit authorisation, each time — not inferred from any task's instructions to "make documentation changes."**

## J. BTA-006 — Award Areas / CSV Import Safety Audit (checkpoint)

**Checkpoint date: 2026-10-22.** Two read-only tasks performed this session, both against the current `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza` checkout (= PR #308's head, `3f3f2b02536f5b3f654751c3bf3d882d4b9af3a2`). **No fixes have been implemented. This section records findings only.**

### J.1 — What was audited

The Award Areas bulk-CSV-nomination-import feature: `src/partials/03a-award-areas.html` (UI), `nominee-uploads.js` (frontend CSV parse/validate/upload orchestration), `api/data-proxy.js`'s `executeAwardAreaImport` (`award_area_import` operation, lines ~1315–1595) and its RBAC gate, `api/_lib/organisation-matching.js` (shared area-scoped matching helper — this PR's named security fix), `api/_lib/award-categories.js` (category allowlist), and the supporting migrations (`067`, `068`, `073`, `076`) and tables (`areas`, `organisations`, `award_years`, `entries`, `nominee_upload_batches`). This feature exists only on the PR #308 branch; it has **not** been separately merged into `main`.

### J.2 — What passed / was verified directly from code or a safe local run

- `award_area_import` requires an authenticated session (`verifyAuth`) and `editor` role or above, for both `validate` and `import` modes — confirmed from `api/data-proxy.js`'s handler dispatch order.
- `api/_lib/organisation-matching.js` escapes ILIKE wildcard characters (`%`, `_`, `\`) and returns `null` rather than matching globally when no `areaId` is resolvable — confirmed by direct code reading and by the one test that exercises the full validate→import path.
- `mode: 'import'` always re-validates server-side before writing — the client's own validation result is never trusted.
- A hard 2,000-row-per-call input limit is enforced.
- Unknown award categories are rejected as validation errors; they are never auto-created. Only `award_years` records are found-or-created per category+area+year.
- **Targeted test run reproduced this session:** `npx jest tests/nominee-uploads.test.js tests/organisation-matching.test.js tests/data-proxy.test.js` → **70/70 passed**, local mocks only, no network access, no live database connection. No file, test, migration, or git state was changed by running this.

### J.3 — What remains risky (not fixed — findings only)

- **R1 — No reliable undo for a completed import.** The audit-trail write (`nominee_upload_batches`) records a batch summary (filename/area/counts) only — it does not record which specific `organisations`/`entries` rows that batch created. The older `nominee_delete_all` operation deletes from a different table pair (`nominee_upload_rows`/`nominee_upload_batches` rows written by the *legacy* pipeline) and does **not** undo an Award Areas import.
- **R2 — A failed import can leave partial writes.** Each row's writes are sequential, unwrapped Supabase calls with no transaction; the frontend additionally splits large files into 25-row chunks. A failure partway through a row, or in a later chunk, leaves every already-processed row (and every already-completed chunk) already committed and already publicly visible — there is no rollback.
- **R3 — Two similar nominee-import pipelines coexist.** The older staging-table pipeline (`executeNomineeUpload` / `nominee_upload` operation / `nominee_delete_all`, backed by `database-nominee-uploads.sql`) was checked by grep across all `.js`/`.html` files in the repository and has **no frontend callers anywhere**. The live pipeline is `award_area_import` only. The dead pipeline's delete-all should not be mistaken for an Award Areas undo mechanism (see R1).

### J.4 — Other findings (minor; not fixed)

- **M1:** Migration 073's own comment states `database-nominee-uploads.sql` "was never actually checked into this repo" — that file exists and was read directly this session. Harmless (073 only does idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`), but the comment itself is wrong.
- **M2:** `migrations/058` and `database-nominee-uploads.sql` define **incompatible shapes** for `nominee_upload_batches`/`nominee_upload_rows` (same `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` conflict pattern as the `custom_sectors`/`custom_categories` finding from BTA-005/Section F.1). Migration 073 patches in the columns the live code needs regardless of which base won — very likely fine in practice, but **the actual live schema was not checked this session** (no Supabase connection was made, per task boundaries) and remains unverified.
- **M3:** Existing automated tests do not cover: duplicate-row detection within one CSV, unknown-category rejection via `resolveCategory`, email-format validation, the award found-or-create branch, sequential entry-number generation, any of the three `duplicateStrategy` (skip/update/replace) branches' actual field-merge behaviour, the audit-trail insert, or a multi-row validate→import round trip. The only end-to-end test covers the ILIKE-escaping/area-scoping fix specifically, not the feature's broader business logic.
- **M4:** Area-type labelling has no explicit Northern Ireland handling — already tracked in PR #308's own `POST-PHASE-1-FOLLOW-UP.md` as a pending business decision, not a new finding.

### J.5 — Recommendation on record

**Investigate the import-safety gaps (R1/R2 particularly) and improve confidence before processing the remaining large volume of county workbooks.** The feature is **not** described as fixed, approved for wider use, or deployed by this checkpoint — only audited. Any implementation remains subject to its own separate authorisation.

## K. BTA-068 — Complete Website-to-CMS Coverage: Audit, Verification and Implementation (2026-11-01)

### K.1 — Executive verdict

**The public website is NOT yet completely CMS-driven. Verdict: NO.**

Evidence for this verdict (detail in K.3–K.6 below): (1) the sector/category taxonomy that drives the entry form, nomination forms and award matching is hardcoded in source code in three separate files with no database table or admin screen; (2) the public region/county/London-borough option lists are a second, independent hardcoded taxonomy, structurally separate from the `areas` table that award-matching actually resolves against; (3) the "Package of Interest" sponsorship tiers shown on `become-a-sponsor.html` are hardcoded with no CMS source, and the one table shaped correctly for a public sponsorship catalogue (`sponsorship_opportunities`) has zero admin UI and zero rows; (4) social-media footer links are non-functional `href="#"` placeholders on at least 14 of ~19 public pages; (5) the master template's footer contains literal unfilled placeholder text (`[Company Name Placeholder]`, `Reg. No. [PLACEHOLDER]`, `[Address Placeholder]`); (6) season/award key dates are genuinely CMS-managed and now correctly flow to the homepage (BTA-064), but a **second, independent, unlinked copy** of the same 8 date fields exists per-award (`awards` table) with no stored link back to the season it was copied from and no UI indication of which copy is authoritative for the public site.

What **is** genuinely, verifiably CMS-driven and confirmed end-to-end this task: homepage season dates (Key Dates list + Vote box, BTA-064, re-verified below), award/organisation/winner/entry core CRUD, email template bodies (including the sponsor-enquiry confirmation), GDPR erasure (now atomic, BTA-067, re-verified below), event attendee management (now free of the delete-and-reinsert data-integrity bug, BTA-066, re-verified below).

### K.2 — Website inventory

Built on, not replacing, the inventories already on record from BTA-061 and BTA-064/065's own investigations. This task's two background forks extended that inventory with full navigation, forms, and SEO/metadata coverage, and resolved the sectors/categories/awards/award_years/areas/results taxonomy distinction explicitly (see K.3 below for the consolidated table and the fork reports' own findings, preserved verbatim in the conversation record and summarised here).

Public pages covered this task (forms + navigation + SEO depth, beyond the homepage-only focus of BTA-064): `submit-entry.html`, `nominate.html`, `industry-leader.html`, `become-a-sponsor.html`, plus a footer/social-link sweep across all ~19 public HTML pages.

### K.4 — Verified data flows (re-confirmed or newly confirmed this task)

- **Season dates → homepage**: admin edit (`settings.js saveSeason`) → `award_seasons` table → `api/entry-proxy.js handleGetPublicData` (ambiguity-safe, only exposes when exactly one default season exists) → `home.js initPublicData()` → DOM text, with hardcoded fallback text preserved when a field is null. Caching/staleness risk: none identified (no caching layer between DB and the public fetch). Cross-page consistency: only `home.html` reads these fields for display; no other public page shows season dates, so no cross-page inconsistency risk exists for this specific flow.
- **GDPR erasure**: admin action (`gdpr.js processRequest`) → `apiClient.rpc('erase_organisation_gdpr', ...)` → `api/data-proxy.js executeRpc` (role-gated via `ALLOWED_RPCS`, `super_admin` required) → Postgres RPC (migration 089, not yet applied) → 13-table cascade + organisations delete, atomic. Re-verified by direct read of `gdpr.js`, `api/data-proxy.js`, and the full migration file — **exact match, zero discrepancies** from BTA-067's documentation.
- **Event attendee check-in**: admin/door-staff action → `events.js` (`toggleCheckIn`/`checkInAll`/`updateAttendeeStatus`/`deleteAttendee`/`addAttendee`) → targeted `apiClient.update`/`updateByFilters`/`insert`/`delete` calls on `event_attendees` by stable id → no full-table delete-and-reinsert. Re-verified by direct read of all 5 functions plus `checkInAll` — **exact match, zero discrepancies** from BTA-066's documentation.

### K.5 — New technical findings this task (not previously on record)

1. **`erase_organisation_gdpr` (migration 089) and `anonymize_organisation` (migration 046) execution-role/security-definer analysis (BTA-068 section 8's new, specific requirement — not previously checked):**
   - Both functions are `LANGUAGE plpgsql` with **no `SECURITY DEFINER` clause** — i.e. both run as `SECURITY INVOKER` (the Postgres default).
   - The only application code path that calls either (`api/data-proxy.js executeRpc`) uses a Supabase client created with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` (confirmed: `createClient(process.env.SUPABASE_URL, process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY)`, `api/data-proxy.js:31`), i.e. every real invocation already runs as `service_role`, which bypasses RLS and holds broad privileges by Supabase platform design — independent of any `SECURITY DEFINER` clause on the function itself.
   - **Conclusion: `SECURITY DEFINER` is correctly *not* used here, and is not needed.** The classic Postgres `SECURITY DEFINER` search-path-hijack vulnerability class (an attacker-controlled, lower-privileged caller manipulating `search_path` before invoking a function that runs with a *different, elevated* role's privileges) does not apply to a `SECURITY INVOKER` function whose only caller already holds the privileges it needs. Migration 089's own `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` closes the actual risk (anon/authenticated calling it directly over PostgREST) at the correct layer — the grant system, not a search-path fix.
   - This is a considered, evidence-based conclusion, not an assumption: it was reached by checking the actual client construction in `api/data-proxy.js`, not by inferring Supabase's general behaviour. No change to migration 089 is needed on this point; adding `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` would be harmless defense-in-depth but is not a fix for a real vulnerability here, so it was not added (avoiding changing an unapplied migration without a concrete reason, per section 10's "no speculative schema changes" instruction).
2. **Foreign-key behaviour on `organisations` deletion (BTA-068 section 8's "foreign-key/dependent-record behaviour" requirement):** grepped every `REFERENCES organisations(id)` across all `*.sql`/`migrations/*.sql`/`database-*.sql` files. Every reference found uses `ON DELETE SET NULL` (none use `RESTRICT`, `CASCADE`, or the Postgres default `NO ACTION` that would block the delete). This means: (a) deleting the `organisations` row at the end of `erase_organisation_gdpr`'s cascade will **not** fail with a foreign-key violation from any table outside the function's own 13-table list; (b) tables outside that list that still reference the deleted organisation (e.g. `table_assignments`, `running_order`, `event_budgets`, social-media-post tables, marketing tables) will have their `organisation_id`/`company_id`/`org_id` column set to `NULL` automatically — not erased of other fields, but no longer linked to the erased organisation. This confirms the erasure is atomic and will not silently half-fail on an unexpected FK constraint; it does **not** claim those other tables contain no further personal data of their own (out of scope for this check, not contradicted by it either).
3. **GDPR failure communication (BTA-068 section 8's "whether the admin UI communicates failures accurately" requirement):** `gdpr.js processRequest`'s `catch` block (`utils.showToast('Failed to process: ' + e.message, 'error')`) surfaces the real thrown error message to the admin, not a generic string. Traced the propagation path: `executeRpc` (`api/data-proxy.js`) does `const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc(...); if (error) throw error;` — the real Postgres error (e.g. `function erase_organisation_gdpr(uuid) does not exist` if migration 089 is unapplied) propagates unmodified through `apiClient.rpc` to this catch block. **Confirmed: the admin UI will accurately report the specific failure reason, including the "migration not applied" case**, rather than silently appearing to succeed or showing a generic error. This matches the deliberate "fail loudly and safely" design choice already recorded in BTA-067.

### K.6 — Admin CMS usability/discoverability findings (from background fork, this task)

Full fork report preserved in the conversation record. Headline finding, **confirmed and partially addressed this task** (see K.7): season dates are independently editable from two screens (the Season modal → `award_seasons`, and the Award modal → `awards`' own copy) with no stored link and, before this task, no on-screen indication of which one the public site actually reads. Other findings (all **recommendations, not defects**): season/award form labels and date-field grouping are already clear and free of DB jargon; draft/published status is already shown via badges in all three relevant list views (Awards, Seasons, Entries); the `applySeasonToAll` bulk action already has an adequately specific confirmation; `deleteSeason`'s confirmation and the award-form season-select help text under-described their own effects (fixed this task); no admin form previously told the admin which public page/section a field's value would appear on (partially fixed this task for the Season modal specifically).

### K.7 — Implemented improvements this task (safe, independent, tested)

All four of the fork's ranked recommendations #1, #2, #3 and #5 were implemented (its #4 — a "copied from season on [date]" indicator computed and stored at copy time — was judged to need a new column/stored timestamp, i.e. a schema decision, and was left unimplemented per section 10's restriction against unauthorised schema changes):

1. **`src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`** — added a one-line public-impact hint inside the Season modal: *"These dates are shown live on the public homepage's Key Dates and Vote sections for whichever season is set as default below."* Directly closes the "admin has no in-context indication a field is public-facing" gap for this form.
2. **`src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`** — corrected the Award form's season-select help text, which previously said selecting a season auto-fills only "Entry Opens/Closes and Judging dates" when `applySeasonDates()` actually copies all 8 fields (entries, nominees, judging, voting, winners). New text also explicitly states that later edits to the season will not update the award automatically, directly flagging the dual-copy architecture at the point where an admin would otherwise be misled about it.
3. **`settings.js saveSeason()`** — added a confirmation dialog (*"Setting this as the default season will change the dates shown on the public homepage's Key Dates and Vote sections. Continue?"*), shown **only** when a season is newly being set as default (i.e. the checkbox is checked and it was not already the default) — not shown when creating/editing a non-default season or when an already-default season is re-saved. Implemented via a new `existingSeason`/`becomingDefault` comparison against `this.allSeasons` before the save proceeds; cancelling aborts the save with no API calls made.
4. **`settings.js deleteSeason()`** — strengthened the confirmation text from a generic "Delete this season?" to name the actual consequences: *"...Awards that previously copied dates from it will keep those dates unchanged. If this is the default season, the public homepage's Key Dates and Vote sections will show no season dates until a new default is set."*

**Testing added:** `tests/settings.test.js` — new `describe('Settings Module - saveSeason default-change confirmation')` block, 4 new tests: (a) confirmation shown and save aborted on cancel when newly becoming default; (b) save proceeds when confirmed; (c) no confirmation shown when the season was already the default; (d) no confirmation shown when the default checkbox is not checked. All 4 pass. Pre-existing `deleteSeason`/`saveSeason`-adjacent tests (which mock `confirmDialog` wholesale rather than asserting its exact message) continue to pass unchanged, confirming no regression.

No change was made to any database table, no migration was authored or applied, and no public-facing page content changed — all 4 changes are admin-UI-only text/confirmation additions.

### K.10 — Decision register — new item

**22. Per-award vs. per-season date duplication.** `award_seasons` (season-level, read by the public homepage) and `awards` (award-level, used elsewhere in admin screens) each hold their own independent copy of the same up-to-8 date fields, linked only by a one-time, one-way copy action with no stored reference back to the source season. *Why it matters*: an admin editing an award's own dates, or a season's dates after awards have already copied from it, has no way to know from the UI alone which copy — if either — currently drives a given public display, and the two can silently drift apart. *Options*: (a) keep both, but store the source `season_id` on the award at copy time and surface a "last synced from {season}" indicator (schema change: one new nullable FK column); (b) make the award-level fields fully derived (read-only, always computed from the linked season) rather than copied, removing the drift risk entirely but requiring a decision on what happens to awards with no linked season; (c) leave the architecture as-is and rely on the admin-UI text clarifications already made this task (K.7) as the full mitigation. *Recommendation*: (a) is the smallest-blast-radius option and is consistent with this project's existing "add an FK rather than restructure" pattern (e.g. `areas.id` on `awards`), but it is a schema decision and has not been made — not implemented.

## L. BTA-069 — Central Website Content Management and CMS Architecture Completion (2026-11-02)

### L.1 — Executive verdict

**The public website is NOT yet completely CMS-driven. Verdict: NO** (unchanged from BTA-068, for different remaining reasons — several BTA-068 gaps are now closed).

What changed this task: Priority A (central company/social/footer settings) is now genuinely CMS-managed end-to-end, browser-verified. A previously-undiscovered, severe defect was found and fixed: the entire custom sectors/categories admin UI (`settingsModule.loadCustomSectorsCategories`/`openAddSectorModal`/`openAddCategoryModal`/etc. in `settings.js`) had **no corresponding HTML anywhere in the current checkout** — the container, both modals, and every form field it reads (`customSectorsContainer`, `addSectorModal`, `addCategoryModal`, `newSectorName`, `newCategorySector`, etc.) were entirely absent from `src/partials/*.html`, almost certainly lost during the earlier "monolithic index.html → partials" refactor (`TECHNICAL-DEBT-TODO.md` phase 1). This meant a real, DB-backed, otherwise-complete CMS feature was **100% inaccessible to admins** — exactly the "CMS feature doesn't actually reach the public website" failure mode this whole task series exists to catch, except here it was the admin side that was broken, not the public side. Restored and tested this task.

Still remaining and blocking a "yes" verdict: sector/category and region/county taxonomies are still hardcoded/duplicated in source (now with a concrete, evidence-based path to fix, not yet executed); sponsorship catalogue blocked by a newly-found schema ambiguity (see L.5); social-media/footer-link destination URLs and company footer details still need real business input (not fabricated); nomination form 400 (Decision 19) and winners/results canonical-source question (Decisions 17–18) remain open from prior tasks, untouched and not re-litigated.

### L.4 — Priority A: Central website settings — IMPLEMENTED, TESTED, BROWSER-VERIFIED

**Architecture chosen:** extended the existing `cms_config` key-value table (`migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql:1215`, `key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value TEXT`) — the same mechanism already proven end-to-end for `sponsors_visible` — rather than a new table. New keys: `company_name`, `company_registration_number`, `company_address`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone`, `social_links` (JSON array of `{platform, url, visible}`), `footer_contact_url`, `footer_sitemap_url`.

**Data flow:** `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleGetPublicData` now also selects these keys and returns a `site_settings` object (parses `social_links` JSON defensively, falling back to `[]` on malformed data rather than failing the whole response) — `api/entry-proxy.js` lines ~564-626.

**Admin UI:** `settings.js`'s `renderPublicSiteSettings()` rewritten into three cards (Company Details, Social Media Links, Footer Links) inside the existing "Public Site Settings" section of the General settings tab. New functions `saveCompanyInfo()`, `saveSocialLinks()`, `saveFooterLinks()`, `isValidOptionalUrl()` — all URL fields validated as empty-or-http(s) before saving (rejects `javascript:`/other schemes).

**Public consumption:** new shared script `public-site-settings.js`, included on all 14 public pages that have a `<footer>` (about.html, become-a-sponsor.html, home.html, judge-login.html, judge-portal.html, payment-cancelled.html, payment-success.html, public-voting.html, public-winners.html, register.html, submit-entry-payment.html, upload-documents.html, vote.html, winners-portal.html — matching BTA-068's "14 of ~19 pages" finding exactly). On load it:
- Replaces/cleanly removes the `[Company Name Placeholder]`/`[PLACEHOLDER]`/`[Address Placeholder]` segments (with their separators) depending on whether each is configured — never leaves literal brackets showing.
- Sets social icon `href`s from `social_links`, matched by `aria-label` against **two known exact label conventions** (bare `"LinkedIn"` on 12 pages vs. `"British Trade Awards on LinkedIn"` on `home.html`/`become-a-sponsor.html` — a real discrepancy this task found and handled); hides an icon (rather than leaving it pointing at `#`) when its platform isn't configured/visible.
- Fixes the footer's "Contact Us" (`mailto:` default, or an admin override) and "Sitemap" (hidden unless a real URL is configured — no sitemap page exists anywhere in the repo, confirmed by `ls`) links.
- Updates the copyright year live (matches `©`/`&copy;` followed by 4 digits and "British Trade Awards" — note: `innerHTML` decodes `&copy;` to the literal `©` character, which the regex must match directly, not the entity text; this was caught and fixed via the browser-verification pass below).
- `build.js`'s `publicJsFiles` list updated to copy `public-site-settings.js` into `dist/`.

**Testing:**
- `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` — 3 new tests for `site_settings` (all-unset → all null/`[]`; fully-configured → correctly parsed including JSON `social_links`; malformed `social_links` JSON → falls back to `[]` without erroring).
- `tests/settings.test.js` — 11 new tests for the 3 save functions + `renderPublicSiteSettings` + `isValidOptionalUrl`.
- `tests/public-site-settings.test.js` (new file, 9 tests) — jsdom end-to-end tests of the shared script against both the "full" (home.html/become-a-sponsor.html) and "minimal" (12-page) footer layouts: full replacement, clean placeholder removal, mailto fallback preserved on already-working pages, non-http(s) URL rejection, no-footer no-op, fetch-failure graceful fallback, and (added after a genuine bug was found) actual year replacement from a stale year.
- **Real-browser verification (Playwright, not just jsdom):** served `dist/` statically, mocked `/api/entry-proxy`'s `get_public_data` response, and loaded `home.html`, `judge-login.html`, `register.html`, and `about.html` in headless Chromium. This caught and fixed a genuine defect before it shipped: the social-icon matcher only tried the bare `aria-label` convention, so it silently did nothing on `home.html` (the master template) and `become-a-sponsor.html`, which use `"British Trade Awards on LinkedIn"` etc. Fixed by matching against both known label variants; re-verified afterward — footer company details, social hrefs (including the correctly-hidden Facebook icon when unconfigured), Sitemap/Contact Us links, and the live copyright year all rendered correctly across all 4 pages tested, with zero console errors attributable to this change.

### L.5 — Priority B: Sector/category taxonomy — INVESTIGATED (fork), ONE SAFE FIX IMPLEMENTED

Full findings preserved in the conversation record (background fork report). Corrected the task's own premise: there are **4** duplicate copies of the trade-category base list (`config.js`, `api/_lib/award-categories.js`, `home-data.js`, `submit-entry.js`), not 3, plus a 5th, **intentionally separate** taxonomy (`NOMINATION_CATEGORIES` in `nominate.js`/`industry-leader.js`) that must not be conflated with the other 4. All 4 trade-category copies are currently **byte-identical in content** (only cosmetic key-order differs) — no live drift today, only the absence of a guard against future drift.

`custom_sectors`/`custom_categories` (the DB-backed additive layer) already has a real, more complete admin UI than previously documented — list/add/delete for both, with location-scoping (`country`/`region_id`/`area_id`) and an `available_for_small` flag already wired through to the public entry form via `get_public_data`. The one genuine gap found: **no edit/update existed for either table** — an admin had to delete-and-recreate to fix a typo, losing scoping unless manually re-entered.

**But a bigger problem was found first:** the entire admin UI for this feature (list container, both modals, every form field) had no corresponding HTML anywhere in `src/partials/*.html` — see L.6.

**Implemented this task:**
- `settings.js`: added `editCustomSector(id, name)` (rename-only, no schema needed) and `editCustomCategory(id)` (fetches the row, reuses the existing add-category modal pre-filled including its location cascade, then updates instead of inserting on save). `saveNewSector()`/`saveNewCategory()` now branch on an edit-mode id.
- `src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`: both modals given an `Edit`/`Rename` title-swap.
- Tests: 14 new tests in `tests/settings.test.js` covering add, rename, edit (including location-scope prefill), and the not-found error path.

**Deferred, not implemented:** consolidating the 4 duplicate base-list files into one shared module — confirmed safe in principle (zero current drift) but needs a loading strategy that works for both the Node/CommonJS side (`api/_lib/award-categories.js`) and the plain-`<script>` browser side (`config.js`/`home-data.js`/`submit-entry.js`), which is more than a one-line change; recommended as the next bounded task.

### L.6 — Confirmed, previously-undocumented defect: the custom sectors/categories admin UI had no HTML

Grepping `src/partials/*.html` for `customSectorsContainer`, `addSectorModal`, `newSectorName`, `newCategorySector`, etc. — every element `settings.js`'s `loadCustomSectorsCategories`/`openAddSectorModal`/`openAddCategoryModal`/`saveNewSector`/`saveNewCategory` reads or writes — returned **zero matches**. `loadCustomSectorsCategories()` (called from `settingsModule.init()`) would silently no-op (`container` is `null` → early return); there was no trigger button anywhere to even attempt to open either modal. This feature, including its DB tables, validation, and location-scoping, was completely unreachable by any admin.

**Fixed this task:**
- `src/partials/11-settings.html`: added a "Sectors & Categories" content-card (in the existing Seasons & Areas tab, alongside Award Seasons and Manage Areas) with the `customSectorsContainer` div and an "Add Sector" button.
- `src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`: added the `addSectorModal` (name input, rename-capable) and `addCategoryModal` (sector select, name, `available_for_small` checkbox, country/region/area cascading selects — the country `<select>` statically lists England/Northern Ireland/Scotland/Wales, matching `locationModule.populateCountryDropdown`'s own list, since `openAddCategoryModal` resets this field's value but never populates its options from JS).
- Tests: `tests/settings.test.js` now mocks `locationModule` (previously entirely absent from this test file, meaning this whole feature had zero executable test coverage even at the unit level before this task) and covers `loadCustomSectorsCategories` rendering, both modals' open/add/edit/delete paths.
- **Real-browser verification:** served the actual built `dist/index.html`, confirmed via direct DOM inspection that all the restored elements exist with the expected structure (including the Northern Ireland option), then invoked `window.settingsModule.openAddSectorModal()` and `editCustomSector()` directly against the real page (full visual click-through wasn't reachable without a live login — documented as a limitation) and confirmed both produced the correct DOM state (modal title text, pre-filled input) with zero console errors.

### L.7 — Priority C: Location/area architecture — INVESTIGATED (fork), ONE MIGRATION PREPARED (NOT APPLIED)

Full findings preserved in the conversation record. Headline, previously-undocumented finding: **the admin UI already assumes Northern Ireland is a valid area country in three places** (`location.js:161`'s country-filter dropdown, `areas-manager.js:33,81`'s grouped view, and `areas-manager.js:107`'s own CSV-import help text, which literally instructs admins that "country must be: England / Scotland / Wales / Northern Ireland") — but `areas.country`'s `CHECK` constraint (`migrations/067-create-missing-areas-table.sql:42`) only permits `('England', 'Scotland', 'Wales')`. An admin following the UI's own instructions to import a Northern Ireland area gets a database constraint-violation error. This is a genuine, internal inconsistency, independent of the earlier Glamorgan/Gwent naming question (which was re-checked and **confirmed to be a deliberate, consistent, intentional design choice** — historic-county umbrella groupings used identically across every migration/seed/import-mapping file found, not a live bug).

Also corrected: `is_small` is a real, already-admin-editable DB column (`areas.is_small`, edited via `areasManager.saveEdit()`), not a separate undocumented JS constant as earlier tasks had hypothesized. The existing "Manage Areas" admin screen is a real CRUD UI (list, edit `display_name`/`area_type`/`is_small`/`sort_order`, bulk CSV import) — more complete than BTA-061's "read-only card" characterisation, though it does not yet expose `country`/`region_id` for editing (a smaller, separately-flagged safe gap, not fixed this task).

**Implemented this task:** authored (not applied) `migrations/090-allow-northern-ireland-areas.sql`, which drops and re-adds `areas`'s `country` CHECK constraint (found by introspection, since it was created unnamed) to permit `'Northern Ireland'` alongside the existing 3 values. This only *widens* what's allowed — every existing row already satisfies the new constraint, and no application code currently inserts an NI row (since it's currently blocked), so nothing changes in behaviour unless and until the migration is applied. It does **not** seed any actual Northern Ireland counties/areas — which specific areas to add is a business decision (Decision register item 23, below), not something this task invents.

**Not implemented, deliberately left as a decision:** whether to fix today's inconsistency by *removing* Northern Ireland from the UI (to match the still-unapplied schema) instead of widening the schema to match the UI. Removing it would itself be a product decision (effectively declining Northern Ireland coverage), which section 6B's own instruction ("consider all relevant UK nations... rather than silently assuming England-only coverage") cautions against making unilaterally. Both options are recorded in Decision 23.

### L.8 — Priority D: Sponsorship catalogue — BLOCKED by a newly-found schema conflict, NOT implemented

Directly re-verified (not delegated) this task. `sponsorship_opportunities` has **two conflicting `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` definitions** with incompatible column names:

| | `database-crm-setup.sql:215` | `migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql:134` |
|---|---|---|
| Name column | `opportunity_name` | `name` |
| Price column | `value NUMERIC NOT NULL` | `price NUMERIC` |
| Benefits | `benefits TEXT` (JSON string) | `benefits TEXT[]` (array) |
| Availability | `total_slots`/`available_slots` | `max_sponsors`/`current_sponsors` |
| Event/award link | `event_id`/`award_id` FKs | *(none)* |
| Validity window | `valid_from`/`valid_until` | *(none)* |

Because both use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, whichever ran first against a given database silently "wins" and the other is a no-op — **the live column shape is unknown without a direct database check**, which this task could not perform (Supabase connection remains unavailable; per the task's own instruction, not retried). Confirmed via grep: **zero** JS/HTML files anywhere reference `sponsorship_opportunities` (no admin UI, no public page, no API handler), and it is **not** present in any of `api/data-proxy.js`'s table allowlists (`ALLOWED_TABLES`/`MUTABLE_TABLES`) — so today it is inert and harmless, but also means nothing could be built on it without first (a) resolving which schema is actually live, (b) adding it to the appropriate allowlists with public-read/admin-write permissions, and (c) a genuinely new admin screen and public page section. None of this meets section 10's "safe to implement without approval" bar given the schema ambiguity alone. Recorded as part of Decision register item 21.

### L.9 — Navigation: documented as a structural exception, not implemented

`home.html`'s main nav (`<nav class="header-nav">`) mixes two kinds of items: in-page anchors (`#categories`, `#regions`, `#find-a-trade`) tied to section IDs that only exist on that one page, and two real page links (`about.html`, `submit-entry.html` via the "Enter Now" CTA). No central navigation config of any kind exists (`grep` for `nav_links`/`footer_nav`/similar across `settings.js`/`home.js`/`cms_config` returns nothing), and the other 13 public pages each have their own simpler, differently-structured header, not a shared nav.

**Classified as a structural exception, not fixed:** making anchor destinations freely admin-editable would let a misconfigured value silently break the page's own single-page scrolling; making the two real-page links' *destinations* editable would itself be a business-flow decision ("entries open" → "submit-entry.html" is deliberate product wiring, not routine copy), and the task's own section 4D explicitly warns against allowing arbitrary admin-editable URLs. The *labels* ("Enter Now", "About") could safely be made text-editable later as a small, low-risk follow-up if the business specifically wants it — flagged as a candidate for a future bounded task, not implemented here since nothing in this task's evidence suggested it was a live pain point.

### L.10 — Priority E: Season/award dates — re-confirmed, no further action needed

BTA-068 already implemented the safe admin-UI improvements this priority calls for (public-impact hint on the Season modal, corrected award-form season-select help text, confirmation on newly setting a season default, strengthened delete-season warning) and recorded Decision 22 (per-award vs per-season date duplication) as open. Re-confirmed via `git diff --stat` that `settings.js`/`src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`'s BTA-068 changes remain intact and unmodified by this task except for the unrelated sector/category edits added alongside them in the same files. Decision 22 is preserved exactly as BTA-068 recorded it — not reopened, not resolved, no date fields consolidated or repurposed.

Items 17 (results_status discrepancy), 18 (winners/award_assignments canonical source), 19 (nomination form scope), 20 (season-stage rules), 21 (sponsorship catalogue — reinforced by L.8's new schema-conflict finding, options unchanged), 22 (per-season vs per-award dates) all carried forward unchanged, verified present in the task sheet before this edit.

**23. Northern Ireland area support.** The admin UI (country dropdown, CSV-import grouping and help text) already assumes Northern Ireland is a valid area country; the database schema does not (`areas.country` CHECK constraint). *Why it matters:* an admin following the UI's own on-screen instructions today gets a confusing database error, not a graceful validation message. *Options:* (a) apply `migrations/090-allow-northern-ireland-areas.sql` (prepared, not applied) to widen the schema, then separately seed real Northern Ireland areas/counties once the business supplies the actual list; (b) remove Northern Ireland from the UI's dropdown/grouping/help text to match the current (unwidened) schema, explicitly declining NI coverage for now. *Recommendation:* (a) — the task's own instruction not to "silently assume England-only coverage" argues against (b), and (a) carries zero risk to existing data; it is **not** a complete fix on its own, since no real NI area data exists yet and inventing it is out of scope. *Blocked until approved:* actually seeding Northern Ireland areas, applying migration 090 to any database.

**RESOLVED, BTA-070 (2026-11-02):** the user explicitly chose option (b) — Northern Ireland is permanently excluded from British Trade Awards' geographic coverage. All UI references removed (`location.js`'s `populateCountryDropdown`, `areas-manager.js`'s grouped view and CSV-import help text, the category-location-scope select added in BTA-069, `home-data.js`'s `REGION_DATA['northern-ireland']` branch and its associated image-placeholder entries — all confirmed dead/unreachable since neither public entry form ever offered an NI country-picker button). `migrations/090-allow-northern-ireland-areas.sql` deleted (it existed only to support NI, which is now explicitly out of scope). This decision is now also recorded permanently in `CLAUDE.md` so it is not rediscovered as an open question in a future audit. See BTA-070 Section M.2 for full evidence and the repository-wide audit results. **The broader "future geographic configuration must be CMS-managed" requirement is separate and remains an architecture goal, not fully implemented** — see new Decision/finding in Section M.4.

## M. BTA-070 — Geographic Scope, Sponsorship Catalogue and Complete Website Content Management (2026-11-02)

### M.1 — Executive verdict

**The public website is NOT yet fully CMS-driven. Verdict: NO** — materially advanced again this task, with the two specific architectural gaps BTA-069 left blocked (sponsorship catalogue; geographic scope) now resolved or substantially implemented.

What changed: (1) Northern Ireland is now permanently excluded, documented in both `CLAUDE.md` and this register, and removed from every live code path; (2) the `sponsorship_opportunities` schema-conflict blocker from BTA-069 is resolved by a new, cleanly-designed `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` table with a full admin CRUD screen and a public page that automatically switches from hardcoded cards to the live catalogue once packages are published, falling back gracefully while it's empty; (3) area country/region are now admin-editable (previously read-only after creation). Remaining gaps: the sector/category base-list duplication (4 files) flagged in BTA-069 is still not consolidated; the taxonomy/`awards.sector`/`category` free-text architecture is unchanged; the main navigation and a handful of other structural items remain documented exceptions rather than CMS fields, per their own justification.

| Upstream | `+0 -0` (unchanged) |
