| BTA-003 cleanup | Confirmed intact |

### M.3 — Northern Ireland: repository-wide audit and removal

**Audit method:** grepped the entire repository (JS, HTML, SQL, excluding `node_modules`/`dist/`) for "Northern Ireland", "N. Ireland", "Ulster", plus a targeted check for real NI place names (Antrim, Armagh, Belfast, Fermanagh, Londonderry, Tyrone) that might exist without the country name attached.

**Found and removed:**
- `location.js:161` (`populateCountryDropdown`) — the admin country-filter dropdown listed `['England', 'Northern Ireland', 'Scotland', 'Wales']`. Now `['England', 'Scotland', 'Wales']`.
- `areas-manager.js:33,81,107` — the Manage Areas admin screen's country grouping (`byCountry`), its rendered section list, and its CSV-import help text ("country must be: England / Scotland / Wales / Northern Ireland") all listed Northern Ireland. All three updated.
- `src/partials/99-shell-foot.html` (the custom-category location-scope select added in BTA-069) — removed the `<option value="Northern Ireland">` added one task ago.
- `home-data.js:288-300` — a **previously-undocumented, full Northern Ireland region/county hierarchy** inside `window.REGION_DATA` (11 council areas, e.g. Belfast, Antrim & Newtownabbey, Derry & Strabane, Fermanagh & Omagh, ~40 named towns/areas), plus a parallel 42-entry image-placeholder block (lines ~491-532 as it was) mapping each NI place name to a logo fallback image. **Directly verified this branch was dead/unreachable through any UI**: `submit-entry.html` and `industry-leader.html`'s country-picker buttons (`data-country="england"/"scotland"/"wales"`) never included a Northern Ireland option — confirmed by grep, zero matches. The data existed in source but no on-page control could ever select it (a crafted `?country=northern-ireland` URL deep-link could technically have reached it via `_applyUrlParams()`, a residual exposure now also closed). Both the hierarchy and the image-placeholder block were deleted outright, with an explanatory comment left in their place rather than silently vanishing without a trace.
- `tests/settings.test.js` — removed the Northern Ireland `<option>` from the BTA-069 test fixture it was added to one task ago, so the test DOM matches the real (now NI-free) markup.
- `migrations/090-allow-northern-ireland-areas.sql` — **deleted**. This file (authored, never applied, in BTA-069) existed solely to widen `areas.country`'s CHECK constraint to permit Northern Ireland. Since Northern Ireland is now explicitly excluded, the migration's entire purpose is gone; per this task's own instruction ("if it only widens the constraint to support Northern Ireland, do not proceed with that change"), it was not kept around as a historical artifact either, since it is this project's own unapplied, one-task-old authored file, not an already-applied or long-standing migration — deleting it is not "rewriting migration history" in the sense the task warns against.

**Historical data check (section 3C):** grepped every migration and seed/setup SQL file in the repository for "Northern Ireland" — **zero matches**. No `areas` or `regions` seed row, anywhere in this repository's SQL, was ever created for Northern Ireland (consistent with the CHECK constraint having always rejected it). There is therefore nothing to clean up at the schema/seed level. This cannot rule out the possibility that a live database (reachable only via the still-unavailable Supabase connection) somehow contains NI rows inserted by some other means — that single remaining live-check is recorded as an outstanding item (Section M.10) rather than assumed either way.

**Documentation:** permanent decision recorded in `CLAUDE.md` (new "PERMANENT BUSINESS DECISION: Geographic Coverage" section, placed immediately after the Design System Rules so it is read every session) and in this register (Decision 23, marked RESOLVED above) — both explicitly state the exclusion, name every file class it applies to, and state that future geographic expansion needs its own business approval rather than treating this exclusion as blanket permission to add something else unilaterally.

**Verification:** `grep -rln "Northern Ireland" --include="*.js" --include="*.html" --include="*.sql" .` (excluding `dist/`) now returns only 3 files, each containing exactly one *explanatory comment* (not a functional option) documenting why NI is absent — confirmed by direct line read, not just count. The built `dist/index.html`, `dist/app.min.js`, all 5 lazy chunks, and `dist/home-data.js` were checked after a fresh `npm run build`: zero functional references, one comment in `home-data.js`. New tests: `tests/location.test.js` (3 tests), `tests/home-data.test.js` (4 tests), plus 2 new tests inside `tests/areas-manager.test.js` — all assert the England/Scotland/Wales-only behaviour and the absence of Northern Ireland, not merely that old tests still pass.

### M.4 — Future geographic CMS architecture

Re-confirmed (via the BTA-069 background fork's findings, not re-investigated from scratch since no regression was found) that `areas` already has `country` (now England/Scotland/Wales only), `area_type`, `is_small`, `sort_order`, `is_active`, and `region_id` (FK to `regions`, which itself has `name`/`display_name`/`country`/`sort_order`) — genuinely real, already-seeded, admin-manageable-in-principle columns, not JS constants as earlier tasks sometimes assumed.

**Implemented this task:** `areasManager.openEditModal()`/`saveEdit()` (`areas-manager.js`) previously only edited `display_name`/`area_type`/`is_small`/`sort_order` — **`country` and `region_id` were set at creation (via CSV import) and never editable afterward.** Added both fields to the existing Edit Area modal (`src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`): a `country` select (England/Scotland/Wales) and a `region` select that cascades from it via the already-existing `locationModule.populateRegionDropdown()`. `saveEdit()` now persists both. No schema change was needed — this was a pure admin-UI gap, exactly like the custom-sectors/categories modal-markup gap BTA-069 found elsewhere. 3 new tests in `tests/areas-manager.test.js` cover the cascade and the save payload.

**Not implemented, documented instead (needs a business/architecture decision):** making the England/Scotland/Wales *list itself* admin-manageable (i.e., letting an admin add a 4th country without a code change) would require turning `country` from a CHECK-constrained enum column into a genuine lookup table — a real schema change with migration-compatibility implications for every existing `areas`/`regions` row. No evidence was found that this is an active, near-term need (the explicit ask this task resolved was narrower: exclude Northern Ireland specifically, not "support arbitrary future countries today"). Recorded as a forward-looking option, not implemented: if/when the business wants to expand beyond England/Scotland/Wales, the CHECK constraint is the one piece of schema that would need a migration; the admin UI (`areasManager`) and public form (`REGION_DATA`) would each need their own, separately-decided wiring to that new country, the same category of work just completed for country/region editing. This is deliberately not built speculatively now, consistent with the task's own "do not invent a new geographic hierarchy without evidence" instruction.

### M.5 — Sponsorship catalogue: schema conflict resolved, full capability built

**Verified BTA-069's finding directly** (re-read both `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sponsorship_opportunities` definitions): `database-crm-setup.sql:215` (`opportunity_name`/`value`/`total_slots`/`available_slots`/`event_id`/`award_id`) and `migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql:134` (`name`/`price`/`max_sponsors`/`current_sponsors`, no event/award link) remain genuinely incompatible, and neither is referenced anywhere in application code (re-confirmed by a fresh repository-wide grep, zero hits) — the live shape, if the table exists at all, is still unknown without direct database access.

**Resolution chosen:** rather than trying to reconcile two unknown live shapes under an ambiguous name, created a new, distinctly-named table — `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` — prepared in `migrations/091-sponsorship-catalogue-packages.sql` (authored, **not applied**). This sidesteps the ambiguity entirely: nothing about it depends on knowing which (if either) `sponsorship_opportunities` definition is live, and neither existing table is touched, renamed or dropped. Columns chosen directly from this task's own section 5B proposed-capability list: `name`, `short_description`, `full_description`, `price` (nullable — "price on enquiry"), `benefits` (JSONB array), `image_url`, `display_order`, `is_featured`, `is_published` (defaults to `false` — a draft is never accidentally public), `enquiry_button_label`, `enquiry_destination`, `availability_note`. RLS denies `anon`/`authenticated` entirely (the public page never queries this table directly — see below); admin access goes through the existing `api/data-proxy.js` service-role path, the same security model already used for every other admin-managed table in this project.

**`api/data-proxy.js`**: added `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` to both `ALLOWED_TABLES` and `MUTABLE_TABLES` (no new API file — stays within the Vercel Hobby 12-function limit `CLAUDE.md` tracks).

**`api/entry-proxy.js`**: `handleGetPublicData` now also queries `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` filtered to `is_published = true`, ordered by `display_order`, returned as `sponsorship_packages` in the public JSON payload. Handles the table not existing yet (migration unapplied) the same way every other optional field in this function already does — `{ data: null, error }`, not a thrown exception, defaulting to `[]` rather than failing the whole response for every visitor.

**Admin UI**: new "Sponsorship Packages" sub-tab under Marketing (`src/partials/09-marketing.html`, `marketing.js`), explicitly labelled as distinct from (a) the existing `sponsors` registry under Organisations and (b) each sponsor's own private `sponsorship_packages` CRM deal record — full CRUD (add/edit/delete), publish/unpublish toggle, featured toggle, display-order field, and a benefits textarea (one benefit per line, stored as a JSON array). URL fields validated as empty-or-http(s).

**Public integration — the genuinely open discrepancy, flagged rather than silently resolved:** `become-a-sponsor.html` already contains **fully-written, detailed Bronze/Gold/Platinum marketing copy** (tier names, multi-sentence descriptions, 12 benefit lines each) — this directly contradicts this task's own framing ("no actual packages have been agreed yet... do not invent sponsorship packages"). This content already exists in the repository and was not authored by this task. Given that tension, the chosen, conservative behaviour is: **a new script, `become-a-sponsor-packages.js`, fetches the catalogue and replaces the hardcoded cards only when at least one package has been published; while the catalogue is empty (as it is right now — nothing was seeded), the existing hardcoded cards remain exactly as they are today, unchanged.** This satisfies the task's own requirement that an empty catalogue be "handled gracefully" without inventing content and without destroying existing, apparently-real marketing copy without approval. **This is presented as a decision point, not resolved unilaterally**: should the existing Bronze/Gold/Platinum copy be entered into the new catalogue as the initial real packages (at which point the page will automatically start showing them from the CMS), or does it need to be re-approved/rewritten first? Either way, the capability is now fully built and ready.

**Testing**: `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` — 3 new tests (empty catalogue, populated + ordered, table-not-yet-existing). `tests/marketing.test.js` — 11 new tests covering render/add/edit/save/publish-toggle/delete, including URL and required-field validation. `tests/become-a-sponsor-packages.test.js` (new file) — 7 tests: empty-catalogue fallback, fetch-failure fallback, full replacement with price/benefits/featured badge, select-rebuild, a dynamically-rendered Enquire button's click wiring, and two tests that directly probe for HTML/attribute injection (confirming `escapeHtml` prevents a `<script>` tag from executing and a double-quote in a package name from breaking out of the `data-package` attribute — both pass; no vulnerability found).

**Real-browser verification (Playwright, not just jsdom):** served the built `dist/`, mocked `get_public_data`. Scenario 1 (empty catalogue): confirmed all 3 original hardcoded cards (Bronze/Gold/Platinum) remain, byte-identical page behaviour to before this task. Scenario 2 (one published package, "Platinum Plus", £10,000, featured): confirmed the grid now shows exactly that one card, the old cards are gone, price renders as "£10,000", and — critically — clicking the *newly-rendered* Enquire button correctly pre-fills `#f-package` with "Platinum Plus", proving the re-wiring of dynamically-inserted buttons works, not just the initial render. Zero console errors in either scenario.

### M.9 — Security and data integrity

- `sponsorship_catalogue_packages`'s RLS denies `anon`/`authenticated` outright; the public page reads it exclusively via the existing service-role `get_public_data` proxy, filtered server-side to `is_published = true` — an unpublished draft can never leak through the public endpoint, and PostgREST can never expose it directly to a browser-held anon/authenticated key (the same hardening pattern migration 081 established elsewhere in this project).
- No service-role credentials, secrets, or private sponsor CRM data (`sponsorship_packages`, per-organisation deal records) are exposed by any change this task made — confirmed by re-reading `api/entry-proxy.js`'s new query, which selects only the catalogue's own public-facing columns.
- Admin mutation of the new table goes through `api/data-proxy.js`'s existing, already-authenticated path — no new public write endpoint was created.
- Area `country`/`region_id` edits go through the same existing, already-authenticated `apiClient.update('areas', ...)` path everything else in `areasManager` uses — no new permission surface.
- No migration was applied; no live Supabase or production data was touched; no destructive action was taken against historical data (none existed to act on, per M.3).

## N. BTA-071 — Close the Remaining Public Website CMS Content Gaps (2026-11-02)

### N.1 — Executive summary

**The public website is NOT yet fully CMS-driven. Verdict: NO** — materially advanced again, closing 2 of the 3 confirmed-safe gaps identified this task, correctly declining a 3rd after it turned out to be entangled with an already-open architecture decision rather than a simple wiring fix.

Implemented this task: (1) home.html's main navigation label text (5 items, both desktop and mobile) is now admin-editable, destinations unchanged; (2) per-page SEO `<title>`/meta-description overrides for the 6 public pages with genuine marketing-relevant copy. Investigated and **correctly declined**: wiring `awards.description` into public pages — tracing the actual data model revealed entries/winners/voting are joined through `award_years` (a different table with no admin UI at all), not the `awards` table `awards.js` edits, so this is a facet of the already-open Decision 17/18 canonical-table question, not an independent safe gap. Deprioritized, not implemented: entry/nomination step-subtitle and confirmation-message copy (real candidates, but numerous and low individual value relative to the audit's higher-value findings — not a business-decision blocker, just not reached this task).

### N.3 — Page-by-page content gap audit (background fork, re-verified directly for item 3)

Full fork report preserved in the conversation record. Summary per area investigated:

| Area | Finding | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| SEO/metadata | 100% hardcoded on all 16 public pages checked; zero existing admin control. `home.html`, `about.html`, `become-a-sponsor.html`, `public-voting.html`, `public-winners.html`, `vote.html` all carry genuine marketing copy (not placeholders). | **Safe, implemented this task** |
| Navigation labels | `home.html`'s header-nav + mobile-menu duplicate (5 items) 100% hardcoded; the "Enter Now"/"Enter 2026" CTA on the other 13 pages likewise hardcoded, always → `submit-entry.html`. | **Label text safe, implemented this task** (destinations remain structural, per BTA-069's own standing reasoning) |
| Award descriptions | `awards.description` is real and admin-edited (`awards.js:1480,1592,1634,1922`), but **directly re-traced this task**: `winners.award_id`/`award_assignments.award_id` both `REFERENCES award_years(id)`, and `api/voting-proxy.js` joins `awards:award_years(award_name, award_category)` — the public data model's "award" is actually an `award_years` row, not an `awards` row. `award_years` also has its own `description` column, but **no admin screen anywhere writes to `award_years`** (confirmed by grep — zero `apiClient.insert/update('award_years', ...)` calls in the whole repo). Wiring `awards.description` into public pages would show the wrong entity's (or no) description. | **Not a simple safe gap — entangled with Decision 17/18 (canonical award/results table), not implemented** |
| Public event info (register.html) | Already reads live from the `events` table via `register-app.js:124-129`; the hardcoded "Awards Ceremony" text is only a pre-load placeholder, overwritten on load. | **Already fine — not a gap** |
| Public FAQs | Zero matches for "FAQ"/"Frequently Asked" anywhere in public HTML/JS. | **No gap — feature doesn't exist, not inventing one** |
| Entry/nomination instructional copy | Step subtitles and success/confirmation messages (`submit-entry.html`, `industry-leader.html`, `nominate.html`, multiple lines each) are safe, low-risk candidates; two specific eligibility/consent-rule sentences in `industry-leader.html` (under-25 and under-18 parental-consent wording) correctly flagged as needing legal/business approval, not listed for implementation. | **Safe candidates identified, deprioritized this task (volume vs. value) — see N.6** |
| Public disclaimers/consent text | GDPR/data-processing consent declarations on `submit-entry.html`, `nominate.html`, `industry-leader.html` — all legal wording. | **Needs business/legal approval — not converted, not even proposed as a near-term candidate** |

### N.4 — Implemented: Navigation label CMS control

**Architecture:** extended the existing `cms_config` pattern (new key `nav_labels`, a JSON object `{categories, regions, findATrade, about, enterNow}`) — the same mechanism BTA-069/070 already established for company/social/footer settings. **Destinations are deliberately not configurable**: 3 of the 5 items are in-page anchors (`#categories`/`#regions`/`#find-a-trade`) whose targets only exist on `home.html` itself; letting an admin mistype a destination could silently break the page's own scroll navigation. Only visible text is exposed.

- `api/entry-proxy.js`: `handleGetPublicData` now also returns `site_settings.nav_labels` (each of the 5 keys independently null-able).
- `settings.js`: new "Homepage Navigation Labels" card in Website Settings (5 text inputs), `saveNavLabels()` (omits blank fields from the saved JSON rather than persisting empty strings).
- `public-site-settings.js`: new `applyNavLabels()`, scoped **strictly** to `nav.header-nav`, `#mobile-menu .mobile-menu-inner`, and `.header-cta` — not a page-wide href match. This matters: `home.html` has the same hrefs (`submit-entry.html`, `about.html`) repeated on ~10 other unrelated CTA buttons elsewhere on the page (confirmed by grep: lines 2473, 2502, 2542, 2666, 2998-2999, 3085, 3097); a naive page-wide selector would have corrupted those too.

**Testing:** 3 new tests in `tests/settings.test.js`; 4 new tests in `tests/public-site-settings.test.js`, including one that specifically builds a fixture with an unrelated same-href CTA elsewhere on the page and asserts it is untouched.

**Real-browser verification (Playwright):** served the built `dist/`, mocked `get_public_data` with `{categories: 'Trade Categories', enterNow: 'Apply Now'}`. Confirmed: desktop nav and mobile-menu duplicate both updated correctly; the unrelated hero CTA sharing the same `submit-entry.html` href elsewhere on the page remained "Enter Now", unchanged. Zero console errors.

### N.5 — Implemented: Page SEO (titles and meta descriptions)

**Architecture:** new `cms_config` key `page_seo`, a JSON object keyed by each page's own filename slug (`home`, `about`, `become-a-sponsor`, `public-voting`, `public-winners`, `vote`) — chosen so the public script can identify "which page am I on" purely from `location.pathname`, no per-page HTML changes needed. Each entry optionally holds `title`/`description`; either or both may be set independently.

- `api/entry-proxy.js`: `handleGetPublicData` returns `site_settings.page_seo` (malformed JSON falls back to `{}`, same defensive pattern as every other JSON-stored `cms_config` field in this project).
- `settings.js`: new "Page SEO" card listing the 6 pages with a title+description input pair each (character limits matching common SEO guidance: 70/160), `savePageSeo()` (omits pages where both fields are blank).
- `public-site-settings.js`: new `applyPageSeo()` — derives the current page's slug from `window.location.pathname`, looks up a matching entry, and if found sets `document.title` and/or the `<meta name="description">` tag's content (creating the tag if the page doesn't already have one, e.g. `vote.html`). A page with no configured override, or only a partial override (title only, or description only), is left otherwise exactly as it was.

**Testing:** 2 new tests in `tests/entry-proxy.test.js`; 3 new tests in `tests/settings.test.js`; 5 new tests in `tests/public-site-settings.test.js` (full override, page-mismatch isolation, no-override fallback, tag-creation when absent, and partial-override independence).

**Real-browser verification (Playwright):** same session as N.4's nav-label check — confirmed `document.title` and the meta description tag's `content` attribute both updated to the configured values on the live rendered page, with zero console errors.

### N.6 — Not implemented this task (deprioritized, not a business-decision blocker)

Entry/nomination step subtitles (`submit-entry.html:822,842,895,913,997,1033`; `industry-leader.html:748,770,826,861,881,913`) and success/confirmation messages (`submit-entry.html:1064`, `industry-leader.html:939`, `nominate.html:448`) are genuine, safe candidates for CMS conversion — no business decision needed, low risk. Not implemented this task simply because of volume (roughly a dozen short strings across 3 different forms) relative to the clearer, higher-value items already closed (N.4/N.5). Recommended as the next bounded task if the user wants to continue closing content gaps.

### N.7 — Decision required: none newly raised this task

No new items added to the Decision register. The award-description finding (N.3) is recorded as a new sub-finding under the existing Decision 17/18 umbrella (canonical winner/results source), not a new numbered decision — see the addition below.

**Decision register addendum (not a new numbered item — strengthens existing Decision 18):** `award_years` (the table entries/winners/voting actually reference) has **no admin UI of any kind** — no screen anywhere in the repository writes to it. Combined with Decision 18's existing finding that `winners.placement` (not `award_assignments.winner_position`) is the only field the public site reads, this reinforces that `award_years` may be an under-maintained or legacy table relative to `awards`, which has a full admin screen but isn't the table the public data model actually joins through. This is additional evidence for whichever option is eventually chosen for Decision 18, not a new decision to make.

- No private CRM or administrative data was exposed by anything implemented this task.

## O. BTA-072 — Public Website CMS Completion: Remaining Content and Workflow Gaps (2026-11-02)

### O.1 — Executive summary

**The public website is NOT yet fully CMS-driven. Verdict: NO** — closed BTA-071's own deprioritized Priority 1 gap (entry/nomination step headings, subtitles and confirmation copy — explicitly not stopping at "the ~12 strings" N.6 flagged, covering the full set across all 3 forms), then reassessed Priorities 2–5 from first principles rather than assuming more gaps must exist. Found and fixed one genuine remaining gap (Open Graph title/description not kept in sync with BTA-071's own `page_seo` overrides). Everything else investigated in Priorities 2–5 was confirmed either already covered by BTA-069–071's architecture, correctly out of scope under a Protected Decision, or not a real gap at all (no generic visual editor, no manufactured busywork, per this task's own explicit instruction).

**Implemented this task:**
1. New `form_content` `cms_config` key: CMS-editable step headings/subtitles and confirmation title/message for all 3 public entry/nomination forms (`submit-entry.html` — 7 steps + confirmation; `industry-leader.html` — 6 steps + confirmation; `nominate.html` — confirmation only, no multi-step structure).
2. Open Graph `og:title`/`og:description` now kept in sync with the existing `page_seo` title/description overrides (BTA-071 built `page_seo` but only ever patched `<title>`/`meta[name="description"]`, leaving social-share previews showing stale copy after an admin override).

**Investigated and correctly left untouched** (Priorities 2–5, see O.4): no further shared-content duplication found beyond what BTA-069–071 already centralised; `about.html`/`become-a-sponsor.html`'s marketing-copy blocks are brand-locked prose, not a bounded CMS gap; 4 remaining un-SEO'd pages (`check-in.html`, `cookie-policy.html`, `reset-password.html`, `winner-certificate.html`) are all internal/utility/legal-adjacent pages with no genuine public-SEO relevance; no safe, bounded image gap exists that wouldn't require building an unwanted media-management system.

### O.3 — Implemented: Priority 1 — entry & nomination form content (`form_content`)

**Architecture:** one new `cms_config` key, `form_content`, a JSON object keyed by form (`submit_entry`, `industry_leader`, `nominate`) — the same established key-value pattern as every other BTA-069–071 setting, no new table. Each form entry optionally holds a `steps` map (keyed by the step `<div>`'s own existing `id` attribute, e.g. `step1`) with `title`/`subtitle`, plus top-level `confirmationTitle`/`confirmationMessage`. Every field is independently optional; the page's existing hardcoded text is always the fallback.

**Why not `public-site-settings.js`:** traced before implementing, per this task's explicit instruction not to assume reuse is automatically correct. `submit-entry.html`, `industry-leader.html`, and `nominate.html` have **no `<footer>` element** and do **not** include `public-site-settings.js` (confirmed by grep) — that shared script is gated by `if (!footerEl()) return;`, so it would silently no-op on all 3 pages. Each page instead got its own small, independent apply-function wired into its existing page-load entry point, reusing each page's own existing fetch pattern to `/api/entry-proxy`.

**`submit-entry.html`'s `step3Subtitle` special case:** this element is already dynamically overwritten at runtime by `submit-entry.js` with a live category count once a sector/area is selected. Resolved by applying the CMS override at page-load time, before any sector is picked — the later dynamic update simply overwrites it as before once the user proceeds, exactly as it did prior to this task. No conflict, no special-case code needed.

**`submit-entry.html`'s `step4`:** has no subtitle paragraph in the source HTML at all. The admin UI omits a subtitle field for this one step (`defaultSubtitle: null` in `settings.js`'s `FORM_CONTENT_FORMS`), and the apply-function defensively no-ops if the element isn't found rather than assuming it exists.

**Files changed:**
- `api/entry-proxy.js`: `handleGetPublicData` — `form_content` added to the `cms_config` key list; `site_settings.form_content` returns `{submit_entry, industry_leader, nominate}`, each defaulting to `{}` with the same defensive JSON try/catch as every other field.
- `settings.js`: new `FORM_CONTENT_FORMS` config array (7 submit-entry steps, 6 industry-leader steps, 0 nominate steps — all with the exact existing hardcoded text as each field's placeholder); new "Entry & Nomination Form Content" card in Website Settings; `saveFormContent()` (omits any step/form with every field left blank, same pattern as `saveNavLabels`/`savePageSeo`).
- `submit-entry.js`: new `entryFormApp.applyFormContent()`, called from `initialize()`.
- `industry-leader.js`: new `industryLeaderApp.applyFormContent()`, called from `initialize()`.
- `nominate.js`: new `applyFormContent()` (confirmation copy only — this form has no step structure), called from the `DOMContentLoaded` listener.

**Testing:** 2 new tests in `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` (configured values + malformed-JSON fallback) plus 2 pre-existing exact-equality `site_settings` assertions updated for the new key (the same recurring maintenance this response object has required in every prior task that added a field to it); 4 new tests in `tests/settings.test.js` (rendering with pre-filled values, partial-save omission, all-blank save, and nominate's steps-less save shape); 3 new tests each in `tests/submit-entry.test.js`, `tests/industry-leader.test.js`, and `tests/nominate.test.js` (override applied, fallback when unconfigured, fallback when the fetch fails) — 9 new page-script tests total, each confirming an untouched step/element stays untouched alongside the one that changes.

**Real-browser verification (Playwright):** served the built `dist/`, mocked `get_public_data` per page. `submit-entry.html`: step0 ("Entry Type") unchanged, step1 title/subtitle overridden, step2 unchanged, confirmation title/message overridden. `industry-leader.html`: step1 overridden, step2 unchanged, confirmation overridden. `nominate.html`: confirmation title/message overridden at load time (applies regardless of the success state's visibility, which only toggles on actual submission). Zero console errors on `submit-entry.html`/`industry-leader.html`. `nominate.html` showed one console error — traced and confirmed **pre-existing and unrelated to this task**: `config.js` ends in an ES-module `export {...}` statement but is loaded via a plain `<script src="config.js">` (not `type="module"`) on this page, throwing a `SyntaxError` that was already present before this task touched anything (`git diff --stat config.js` shows zero changes from this task). Confirmed this does not block `nominate.js`'s own execution — a separate `<script>` tag's parse/runtime error doesn't halt sibling script tags — by confirming the CMS override still applied correctly on the same page load.

### O.4 — Reassessed: Priorities 2–5 (background fork, evidence-first, no assumptions)

Traced each candidate's actual admin → API → persistence → public-response → public-rendering flow before concluding it either is or isn't a real gap, per this task's explicit instruction.

Nothing else duplicated that isn't already in `company_name`/`social_links`/`footer_contact_url`/`footer_sitemap_url`/`nav_labels`. | **No gap — already covered** |
| 3 (remaining page content) | `about.html`'s mission/brand paragraphs; `become-a-sponsor.html`'s hero/why/audience sections; public FAQs anywhere | Both are brand-locked marketing prose, the same category of content BTA-069–071 deliberately left hardcoded on `home.html` rather than exposing as loose fields — converting now would be unbounded content-editing scope creep, not a bounded fix. Zero FAQ content exists anywhere in the repository (confirmed by repo-wide grep) — nothing to make editable. | **Correctly out of scope — not a bounded gap, and no generic visual editor per this task's own instruction** |
| 4 (SEO on remaining pages) | `check-in.html`, `cookie-policy.html`, `reset-password.html`, `winner-certificate.html` — the only 4 public-ish pages without `public-site-settings.js` that aren't the 3 entry/nomination forms or the 2 permanently-excluded legal pages | `check-in.html`: internal staff QR-scanning tool. `cookie-policy.html`: legal/compliance document, same character as the 2 explicitly-excluded legal pages even though not literally named in CLAUDE.md's exclusion list. `reset-password.html`: authentication utility page. `winner-certificate.html`: per-winner, token-gated, never-indexed generated certificate view. None has genuine public/marketing SEO relevance. | **No gap — correctly out of scope for all 4** |

No `twitter:title`/`twitter:description` tags exist anywhere (only `twitter:card`), so there was nothing further to sync. | **Implemented — see O.5** |

### O.5 — Implemented: Priority 5 finding — Open Graph title/description sync

**Architecture:** no new `cms_config` key, no new admin UI, no new field — this reuses the exact `title`/`description` values an admin already sets in BTA-071's "Page SEO" card. `public-site-settings.js`'s `applyPageSeo()` now also calls a small shared `setMetaContent(attr, value, content)` helper to set `meta[property="og:title"]`/`meta[property="og:description"]` whenever it sets `document.title`/`meta[name="description"]`, creating the tag if the page doesn't already have one (matching the existing create-if-absent behaviour for the plain meta description tag).

**Files changed:** `public-site-settings.js` only.

**Testing:** 2 new tests added to the existing `applyPageSeo` describe block in `tests/public-site-settings.test.js` (sync when both og tags already exist; creation when they don't); the 3 pre-existing tests in that block (no-override fallback, title-only/description-only independence) were extended to also assert the og tags behave identically to their non-og counterparts in each scenario.

**Real-browser verification (Playwright):** served the built `dist/`, mocked `get_public_data` with a custom `page_seo.home` title/description on `home.html`. Confirmed `document.title`, `meta[name="description"]`, `meta[property="og:title"]`, and `meta[property="og:description"]` all updated to the configured values together, zero console errors.

## P. BTA-073 — CMS Completeness, Technical Debt and Production Readiness (2026-11-02)

### P.1 — Executive summary

**The public website is NOT yet fully CMS-driven, and the project is NOT yet production-ready. Verdicts: NO to both**, with evidence below for exactly what remains and why.

This task ran 4 parallel, scoped investigation forks (public-page CMS-completeness inventory; technical integrity of config/API/frontend plus the known `nominate.html` console error; security review + migration inventory; business-architecture review) and then implemented every confirmed, independent, safe fix they surfaced, rather than stopping at a list of recommendations.

**Implemented this task:**
1. **SEO/social metadata for the 3 entry/nomination forms** — `submit-entry.html`, `industry-leader.html`, `nominate.html` had zero SEO control at all (static `<title>`, no meta description, no Open Graph tags whatsoever, confirmed by the page-inventory fork). Extended each form's existing BTA-072 `form_content` object with optional `pageTitle`/`metaDescription` fields, applied via each page's own existing apply-function — no new table, no new admin card, reusing the exact `setMetaContent`-style pattern BTA-072 Priority 5 established for `og:title`/`og:description`.
2. **Fixed `nominate.html`'s pre-existing console error** (flagged but not fixed in BTA-072) — root cause fully traced by the technical-integrity fork: `config.js` ends in an ES-module `export {...}` statement; every other public page that loads it uses `<script type="module" src="config.js">`, but `nominate.html` alone used a plain `<script src="config.js">`, throwing a `SyntaxError` on load. Fixed by matching every other page's tag exactly (one-line change). Confirmed functionally harmless on this specific page before fixing (nominate.js reads none of config.js's exports) but confirmed it would NOT be harmless on any page that does read `window.SUPABASE_CONFIG` etc. — the whole file fails to execute, not just the export statement, when loaded this way.
3. **Fixed a confirmed, silently-broken admin control**: `sponsorship_catalogue_packages.enquiry_destination` ("Enquiry Destination (optional override)" — "Leave blank to use the sponsor enquiry form") was saved by the admin UI and returned by the public API, but `become-a-sponsor-packages.js`'s click handler never read it — every package's "Enquire" button always scrolled to the on-page form regardless of what an admin configured. Fixed to open the configured destination when set (validated http(s) only, matching the save-time validation), falling back to the existing on-page-form behaviour otherwise.
4. **Documentation correction (no code change, but materially changes future-task guidance)**: the business-architecture fork found hard SQL evidence that `awards` is **not** a separate table from `award_years` — it is an updatable VIEW over `award_years` with `INSTEAD OF` triggers that write `description` (and every other column) straight into `award_years` for the same row `id` (confirmed directly against `migrations/000-complete-database-setup.sql:39-95` and re-confirmed current in `migrations/069-refresh-awards-view.sql`). This overturns the specific premise BTA-071/072 used to decline wiring `awards.description` into public pages ("no admin screen writes to `award_years`") — that premise was based on a literal table-name grep that missed the view. See P.6.A for the full correction and why this is NOT being acted on by adding new UI this task.
5. **Minor build hygiene**: added a cross-reference comment between `build.js`'s two separate public-file manifests (`PUBLIC_PAGES`/`PUBLIC_ASSETS` vs `publicJsFiles`) — confirmed neither is currently missing anything, but this exact split caused a real production incident before (`public-winners.html` missing from the build, BTA-070's VC-M1).

**Confirmed clean / correctly left untouched:** all BTA-069–072 CMS-managed content paths' security (XSS, auth, public/private separation — see P.5); all `cms_config` keys have real consumers, none orphaned or conflicting; `build.js`'s public-file lists are currently complete; Northern Ireland remains excluded everywhere (fresh repo-wide grep, comments/negative-assertions only); migration 090 confirmed actually absent, not silently present.

**Most significant finding requiring your attention, not mine to act on unilaterally:** `migrations/089-secure-gdpr-erasure-rpc.sql` is **not just a security hardening migration — `gdpr.js` already calls the `erase_organisation_gdpr` RPC it creates, unconditionally, with no fallback.** GDPR erasure is a live, broken functional path in any environment where migration 089 hasn't been applied — this is a production-readiness blocker, not a nice-to-have. See P.5/P.7.

### P.5 — Security findings

Focus: every CMS-managed content path added in BTA-069–072, plus the public-facing API handlers.

- **XSS: CONFIRMED clean.** Every `innerHTML` sink that interpolates admin/CMS-supplied text is consistently escaped — `public-site-settings.js` (`escapeHtml()` for company details), `become-a-sponsor-packages.js` (`escapeHtml()` on every package field: name, price, description, benefits, availability note, button label, and now `enquiry_destination`'s attribute value). `submit-entry.js`/`industry-leader.js`'s `innerHTML` sinks only echo the user's own just-submitted form data through pre-existing escaped helpers, unrelated to this task's changes.
- **Auth/authz: CONFIRMED clean, no regression.** `sponsorship_catalogue_packages`/`areas`/`regions` (BTA-070) go through the exact same `verifyAuth`/`hasMinimumRole` + `ALLOWED_TABLES`/`MUTABLE_TABLES` gate in `api/data-proxy.js` as every pre-existing table.
- **Public/private separation: CONFIRMED clean.** `handleGetPublicData` uses an explicit column list on every query — no `select('*')` anywhere in the public handler, so no risk of a careless over-broad select leaking a private field.
- **Secrets: CONFIRMED clean.** No hardcoded credentials/tokens/keys in any BTA-069–073 file.
- **GDPR erasure — CRITICAL, not a new security hole but a live functional gap**: `gdpr.js` already calls `apiClient.rpc('erase_organisation_gdpr', ...)` unconditionally (pre-existing code, not touched this task), but that RPC is only created by migration 089, which remains unapplied. In any environment without migration 089 applied, a GDPR erasure request **fails outright** — the application code has moved ahead of the schema. This is not something this task can fix (applying migrations is explicitly out of scope), but it must not be left undocumented. See P.7/P.9.

No secret values are reproduced in this report, per the task's own instruction.

### P.6 — Business architecture review (no redesign; documentation and evidence only)

**A. Award and results model — correction to BTA-071/072's stated reasoning.** Re-confirmed directly against `migrations/000-complete-database-setup.sql:39-95`: `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW awards AS SELECT * FROM award_years;`, with `INSTEAD OF INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` triggers whose update function sets `description = NEW.description ... WHERE id = OLD.id` directly against `award_years`. `migrations/069-refresh-awards-view.sql` and `migrations/083-enable-security-invoker-on-views.sql` both independently confirm this is still the live structure. **`awards.js`'s `apiClient.update('awards', id, awardData)` therefore already writes `award_years.description` for the same row `id` that `winners.award_id`/`award_assignments.award_id` reference** — `awards` and `award_years` are the same rows under two names, not two disconnected entities as BTA-071/072 concluded from a literal table-name grep that missed the view.

**Why this is not acted on by adding new UI this task**: the admin→API→persistence path for award descriptions genuinely already works (this is new, solid evidence). What's still missing is a UI decision — `api/voting-proxy.js`'s public queries select `award_name`/`award_category` but not `description`, and no public page (e.g. `vote.html`'s/`public-voting.html`'s nominee cards) currently has a place designed to show a per-award description. Adding one would mean choosing where and how a category description appears on an existing, already-designed card layout — a presentation decision the task's own instructions say not to guess at ("avoid... cosmetic redesigns and unrelated feature work"), not a simple wiring fix like items 1–3 in P.1. **Recommendation for a future task**: if an admin wants award/category descriptions shown publicly, this is now a straightforward, low-risk addition (add `description` to the 2 existing `award_years` selects in `api/voting-proxy.js`, then design where it renders) — just not a decision for this task to make unprompted.

This finding does **not** resolve Decision 18 (canonical winner/results source — `award_assignments.winner_position` vs `winners.placement`, which remain two separate, still-disconnected fields); it resolves a narrower, separate question BTA-071/072 declined on. Decision 18 remains open and is, if anything, sharper now that the `awards`/`award_years` identity is settled. **A new finding while re-confirming `winner_position`/`placement`**: no migration anywhere in tracked history creates `winners.placement` — it's read/written by JS (`winners.js`'s `_resultBadgeWord()`) but its schema origin isn't in the migration record. Cannot confirm from local files alone whether it exists on the live database or was added ad hoc; flagging for the next task with live-schema access, not fixing here.

**B. Season stages.** Confirmed: no stage-switching logic exists anywhere in the code. `award_seasons` dates feed only display text (`home.js`'s "Key Dates" list and countdown) — nothing compares `new Date()` against stored dates to swap which CTA or page is shown. "Enter Now" always links to `submit-entry.html` unconditionally, regardless of date. The 3rd stage ("Order/Purchase packages") is a real, separate, always-on feature (event ticket/table registration via `register.html`/Stripe) — not gated by any season concept either. **The "3 stages" are a conceptual framing only; there is no implemented lifecycle/stage concept to CMS-manage, and none should be invented.** This sharpens existing Decision 20 without resolving it.

**C. Sponsorship catalogue.** Unchanged from BTA-070/072: `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` (migration 091, unapplied) remains architecturally separate from private CRM sponsor data (`crm.js`/`sponsor-portal.js` — different tables, no overlap). No new findings.

**D. Geography.** Fresh repo-wide grep (including `dist/`, migrations, tests) confirms only comments and negative-assertion test names reference Northern Ireland — no functional reintroduction anywhere. One non-actionable historical note: `migrations/072-fix-entries-voting-proxy-columns.sql:11` documents that `entries.selected_country` (a free-text column, no CHECK constraint) could historically contain `'northern-ireland'` from old CSV imports — explanatory history about a pre-existing unconstrained column, not a current reintroduction, and (per this task's own rule) a separate live-data-cleanup decision if it ever matters, not something to act on here.

**E. Decision register.** Open items 17, 18, 19, 20, 22 all remain open; none can be marked resolved from this task's evidence alone (resolving 18 needs a human choice of canonical table/field, which the `awards`/`award_years` identity finding informs but doesn't make for them). Item 23 (Northern Ireland) remains correctly RESOLVED. **Addendum to Decision 18's record** (not a new numbered item): the premise that "`award_years` has no admin UI of any kind" (recorded in BTA-071's N.7 addendum) is corrected — `awards.js`'s admin screen **does** reach `award_years` for every column the view's update trigger passes through, including `description`. The remaining open question is purely which of `award_assignments.winner_position` / `winners.placement` is the canonical results field, which this finding does not touch.

**Priority for urgency**: Decision 18 remains the one most actively blocking further award/results-related CMS work. Decisions 19/20/22 are lower-priority/theoretical — no current task is blocked by them. Decision 21 (sponsorship catalogue) only blocks applying migration 091, not any independent work.

### P.7 — Migration inventory (087, 088, 089, 091, and a check for 090)

Confirmed migration 090 does **not** exist on disk (`ls migrations/` has no `090-*`) — consistent with its BTA-070 deletion, not silently present.

| Migration | Purpose | Depended on by current app code | Superseded? | Safe to retain? | Pre-apply verification needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **087** — harden `sponsorship_opportunities` RLS | Drops 3 permissive anon/authenticated policies (incl. one from migration 046), replaces with service-role-only + deny-all | None — zero frontend/API references to `sponsorship_opportunities` anywhere (confirmed again this task) | No | Yes | Confirm live policy names match exactly what the migration assumes (from migrations 024/045/046) before applying |
| **088** — harden 22 remaining tables from migration 046's grant-array bug | Same permissive-policy-OR pattern, 22 tables (events, notifications, CRM notes, tenant_branding, etc.) | None directly — all legitimate access already goes through the service-role `api/data-proxy.js`, which bypasses RLS regardless | No | Yes | Re-confirm the 22 exact policy names live; verify no table was dropped/renamed since authoring |
| **089** — atomic `erase_organisation_gdpr` RPC + lock down `anonymize_organisation` EXECUTE | Wraps a 13-table GDPR cascade in one transactional function; restricts EXECUTE on both GDPR RPCs to `service_role` | **`gdpr.js` already calls `erase_organisation_gdpr` unconditionally** — app code has moved ahead of schema | No | Yes | **Highest priority of the four** — GDPR erasure is currently broken wherever this hasn't run. Verify `anonymize_organisation`'s current EXECUTE grants before revoking (confirm nothing else depends on broader access) |
| **091** — new `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` table | Sidesteps the still-unresolved dual `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sponsorship_opportunities` conflict (confirmed still present: `database-crm-setup.sql` vs `migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql`, two incompatible bodies) by using a new table instead | `api/entry-proxy.js`, `api/data-proxy.js`, `marketing.js`, `become-a-sponsor-packages.js` — all BTA-070 code already assumes this table exists | No | Yes | Uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, safe either way against a partial prior run; the underlying `sponsorship_opportunities` conflict remains separate and untouched by design |

**Bottom line**: none of the 4 are obsolete, superseded, or unsafe to retain. **089 is materially different from the other three** — it is the one migration whose absence causes an existing, already-shipped feature (GDPR erasure) to fail outright, not just leave tables RLS-exposed. This must be weighed accordingly when migrations are prioritised for authorised application.

## Q. BTA-074 — Database Readiness, Migration Reconciliation and Critical Workflow Hardening (2026-11-02)

### Q.1 — Executive summary

**The project is closer to production-ready, but is NOT production-ready today, and the public/admin CMS is not fully verified end-to-end against a live database.** This task ran 4 parallel, scoped investigation forks (migration reconciliation; GDPR erasure end-to-end trace; award/results architecture and Decision 18; critical workflow + security review), then implemented every confirmed, safe, independent fix they surfaced — including one genuinely serious finding.

**Most significant finding — fixed this task:** the GDPR admin screen's "Process" button dropped a request's `entity_type` before processing it. Every "contact" erasure/export/anonymize request (as opposed to "organisation") was silently run through the organisation-scoped code path with the contact's own ID — which matched zero rows everywhere, was then marked `completed`, and told the admin the request had been honoured. **No contact-type GDPR request has ever actually been fulfilled by this screen; every one was a false positive.** Fixed by rejecting non-organisation requests up front with a clear error, before any status change, so they are now visibly left for manual handling instead of silently mis-reported as done.

**Other confirmed fixes implemented this task:**
- GDPR requests that fail partway through processing were left stuck at `status: 'processing'` forever, with no retry path and no visible indication of failure. Fixed by reverting to `'pending'` on failure (the only other value the live `gdpr_requests.status` CHECK constraint permits), with the failure reason recorded in the request's `notes` field.
- The GDPR RPC call path had no validation that the entity id was even a well-formed UUID before sending it to the database. Added a defensive format check.
- A blank/unset `placement` value on a `winners` row was displayed as **"Winner"** on public winner pages, certificates, and social-share banners — directly contradicting this project's own stated convention that a blank/unset placement is not a winner placement. Fixed in both `winners.js` and `public-winners-app.js` so a blank value falls through to the same "Finalist" default already used for any other unrecognised value.
- `editor` and `marketing` roles have UI access to the Sponsorship Packages admin screen with no additional client-side gate, but `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` never listed `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` in either role's write set (an oversight from BTA-070, which added the table to the generic `ALLOWED_TABLES`/`MUTABLE_TABLES` lists but not to the per-role grants) — every save by either role was silently rejected with a 403 despite the UI suggesting it should work. Fixed by adding the table to both write sets, matching every other Marketing-tab table's existing pattern.

**Flagged, not fixed (require a business/legal decision, not a technical one):**
- `winners.winner_name` (plain text, e.g. a company name) is **not** among the 13 tables migration 089's `erase_organisation_gdpr` function clears, nor among the tables `anonymize_organisation` touches — after an erasure, the winner record's `organisation_id` goes `NULL` but the company name text remains in the database. This may be intentional historical-award-record retention, or it may be an oversight. Not decided here — see Q.6.
- Migration 089 lacks an explicit `SET search_path` on its function definition (defense-in-depth hardening, not a live vulnerability given it runs as invoker with EXECUTE already restricted to `service_role`/owner). A proposed correction is described in Q.5 but the migration file itself was **not** edited, per this task's explicit instruction not to change migration 089 except for a documentation clarification that cannot affect execution — this would be a behavioural change, however small, so it was left as a recommendation only.

### Q.3 — Migration reconciliation matrix (background fork, read-only)

Migration 090 reconfirmed absent (`ls migrations/` jumps 089→091 directly); a fresh repo-wide grep (including `dist/`, `tests/`, `migrations/`) found only comments and negative-assertion test names referencing Northern Ireland — no functional reintroduction anywhere.

| Migration | Purpose | Code dependency | Dependencies/order | Data/security risk | Repository status | Live status | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **087** | Harden `sponsorship_opportunities` RLS (drops 3 permissive anon/authenticated policies from migrations 024/045/046, locks to service-role-only + deny-all) | **None** — zero frontend/API references to this table anywhere, not even in `ALLOWED_TABLES` | Independent; no prerequisite | DDL-only (DROP/CREATE POLICY, REVOKE), no data touched, negligible lock risk | Authored, not applied | **Unknown** — no live Supabase evidence obtained this task | Safe to apply; no follow-up migration needed since nothing depends on it |
| **088** | Same pattern across the 22 remaining tables from migration 046's grant-array bug | **None directly** — every reference to these 22 tables goes through `api/data-proxy.js` (service-role, bypasses RLS) or server-side API files; no direct browser-side `.from()` call found for any of them | Independent of 087/089/091; disjoint table set from 087 | Same DDL-only profile as 087, looped 22×; negligible risk | Authored, not applied | **Unknown** | Safe to apply; same reasoning as 087 |
| **089** | Wraps the 13-table GDPR erasure cascade into one atomic `erase_organisation_gdpr(p_org_id UUID) RETURNS VOID` function; revokes EXECUTE on both this and the sibling `anonymize_organisation` from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated | **Confirmed hard dependency**: `gdpr.js` already calls this RPC unconditionally — it does not exist until 089 runs | **Prerequisite: migration 046 must already be live** (the REVOKE on `anonymize_organisation` would error otherwise — given migrations 047–086 already assume 046, this is almost certainly already live, but should be verified, not assumed) | DDL-only at apply time; the function it *creates* performs irreversible deletes when later called — inherent to GDPR erasure by design, not an apply-time risk | Authored, not applied | **Unknown** | **Highest priority of the four** — a confirmed, already-shipped feature (GDPR erasure) cannot function at all without it |
| **091** | New `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` table (name/descriptions/price/benefits/image/order/featured/published/enquiry fields), RLS deny-all to anon/authenticated, created empty | `api/entry-proxy.js`, `api/data-proxy.js`, `marketing.js`, `become-a-sponsor-packages.js` all already assume this table exists | Independent; deliberately sidesteps the pre-existing, still-unresolved dual `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sponsorship_opportunities` conflict (two incompatible definitions live in `database-crm-setup.sql` and `migrations/046`) rather than resolving it | `CREATE TABLE/INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` + RLS enable, fully additive, zero data-loss risk | Authored, not applied | **Unknown** | Safe to apply; feature stays inert (empty catalogue, existing hardcoded fallback) until an admin publishes packages afterward — no commercial content seeded by this migration |

All 4 are idempotent as authored (`IF NOT EXISTS`/`IF EXISTS` guards or exception-wrapped `CREATE POLICY`, consistent with the project's established pattern from migrations 052/080/086). None is superseded by anything later or by each other — all four touch fully disjoint tables/functions. 087/088/091 are reversible in practice (re-granting access or dropping the table would undo them); 089 is schema-reversible but any erasures already performed by the function it creates are not, by design.

**Recommended application order (recommendation only — nothing executed):**
1. **Pre-check** (read-only): confirm `anonymize_organisation(UUID)` exists live before applying 089.
2. **089** first — highest urgency, unblocks the already-shipped GDPR erasure feature. Post-check: confirm `erase_organisation_gdpr` exists and EXECUTE is restricted to `service_role` only.
3. **087 and 088** — either order, no dependency on each other or on 089/091. Post-check: confirm anon/authenticated have zero privileges on the affected tables.
4. **091** last — lowest urgency (feature enablement, not a fix). Post-check: confirm the table exists empty and RLS denies anon/authenticated; leave package content to a separate, approved admin action.

**One surprise worth flagging, not acting on**: `database-crm-setup.sql` (not a numbered migration) independently defines a conflicting `sponsorship_opportunities` schema that migration 046 also redefines with different columns. Migration 091 deliberately doesn't resolve this — it's a separate, smaller follow-up worth considering if `sponsorship_opportunities` is ever confirmed empty and safe to drop, not something to act on in this task.

### Q.4 — GDPR erasure: end-to-end findings and fixes implemented

Full trace preserved in the conversation record; see Q.1 for the fixes themselves. Findings by severity, each classified per this task's own taxonomy:

| # | Finding | Classification | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `entity_type` dropped before `processRequest` — every "contact" request silently processed (and reported successful) via the organisation-scoped code path, erasing/exporting/anonymising nothing | **CONFIRMED** | **Fixed** — `data-entity-type` now passed through; non-organisation requests are rejected up front with a clear error |
| 2 | No UUID-format validation on the entity id before the RPC call | **CONFIRMED** | **Fixed** — added a format check |
| 3 | `gdpr_requests.status` left stuck at `'processing'` forever on any failure, no retry path | **CONFIRMED** | **Fixed** — reverts to `'pending'` with the failure reason in `notes` |
| 4 | `winners.winner_name` not cleared by either GDPR function — personal/company-identifying text can remain after an organisation is erased | **CONFIRMED** (code fact) / retention intent is **UNKNOWN** | **Flagged, not fixed** — requires a business/legal decision (Q.6) |
| 5 | Migration 089's function uses no `SECURITY DEFINER` (runs as invoker) and no explicit `SET search_path` | **CONFIRMED** | **Not fixed** — `SECURITY DEFINER`'s absence is not a defect (invoker-rights is arguably safer here); the missing `search_path` is a defense-in-depth gap with a proposed correction below, not applied (would require editing the migration, out of scope) |
| 6 | The 13-table DELETE cascade is atomic (single function body, any failure rolls back all prior deletes) and idempotent (deleting already-gone rows is a no-op) | **CONFIRMED**, both verified directly against the SQL and against every FK referencing the affected tables (all `CASCADE`/`SET NULL`, none `RESTRICT`) | No action needed — working as intended |
| 7 | An audit-log line is written via `logActivity` *after* the RPC commits, not inside the same transaction — a crash between the two would lose the audit line even though the erasure already happened | **POTENTIAL** (low-probability edge case) | Not fixed — would require an outbox/transactional-log pattern, out of proportion to the risk; flagged for awareness only |
| 8 | Existing `tests/gdpr.test.js` coverage mocked `_deleteEntityData` itself, so the ID-confusion bug (#1) would have passed undetected; no server-side RPC role-gate test existed for either GDPR RPC name | **CONFIRMED** test gap | **Fixed** — added 3 new `processRequest` tests (reject non-organisation, reject invalid UUID, revert-to-pending on failure) plus a new 14-test `GDPR RPC role gate` block in `tests/data-proxy.test.js` exercising the real server-side `ALLOWED_RPCS` gate for both `erase_organisation_gdpr` and `anonymize_organisation` |

**Proposed correction to migration 089 (not applied, per this task's instruction):** add `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` to the `erase_organisation_gdpr` function definition. This is standard Postgres hardening against search-path injection for any function reachable by a role other than its owner; it is a behavioural change (however immaterial given the function's current body only references unqualified table names that already resolve correctly under the default search path) and was therefore left as a recommendation rather than edited into the migration file.

### Q.5 — Award/results architecture and Decision 18 (background fork, read-only, no redesign)

Re-verified directly against the schema rather than trusting the prior summary:

- **`awards` is a VIEW over `award_years`** (re-confirmed against `migrations/000-complete-database-setup.sql`'s `INSTEAD OF` triggers, `migrations/069-refresh-awards-view.sql`, `migrations/083-enable-security-invoker-on-views.sql`) — carrying forward BTA-073's correction consistently.
- **CONFIRMED MISMATCH, now fixed**: `winners.js`'s `_resultBadgeWord()` and `public-winners-app.js`'s `placementLabel()` both defaulted a blank `placement` to "Winner" — contradicting this project's own stated convention. `award_assignments`'s parallel `getWinnerPositionBadge()` already handled this correctly (no badge for an unset `winner_position`). Fixed, see Q.1.
- **CONFIRMED, re-verified**: `award_assignments.status = 'nominated'` is set at assignment creation (`assignments.js`) and matches the stated convention exactly.
- **CONFIRMED, re-verified**: no migration anywhere in tracked history creates `winners.placement` as a column, and no admin JS insert/update call ever sets it — it is read by display/certificate code but its schema origin and write path are both untraceable from the repository alone.
- **Current source of truth by field** (as implemented in code today, not as it "should" be): award identity → `award_years.id` (via the `awards` view); award year → `award_years.year`; sector/category → `award_years.sector`/`award_years.award_category`; nomination status → `award_assignments.status`; winner placement → **ambiguous** between `award_assignments.winner_position` (schema-defined, has a real admin write path) and `winners.placement` (display-read only, no confirmed write path or schema origin).
- **Decision 18's actual remaining shape**: the codebase has two distinct "which place did this organisation finish" signals that no one has yet decided between — `winner_position` (has a real write path, correctly handles blank-as-not-a-winner) and `placement` (read by every public winner page and all certificate/asset generation, but untraceable to any migration or current write path). Three options the existing architecture already supports, none chosen here:
  - **Option A** — canonicalize on `winner_position`: pro — has a real write path and correct blank-handling; con — the public display path would need repointing from `winners` to `award_assignments`, and historical `winners` rows have no equivalent value to migrate from without live-schema access.
  - **Option B** — canonicalize on `placement`: pro — no application-code change to the public display path; con — the column's schema origin and even its current live existence are unverified, which is itself a production-readiness risk to formalize around.
  - **Option C** — keep both, designate one authoritative, treat the other as legacy/mirrored: pro — avoids an immediate backfill requirement, lets existing certificates keep rendering unchanged; con — two fields to keep conceptually straight going forward.
  - This choice is explicitly **not** made here, per this task's instruction.

### Q.6 — Business decisions requiring your approval

1. **`winners.winner_name` retention after GDPR erasure** (Q.4 #4): should the erasure/anonymize functions also clear this field, or is retaining a bare company name on a historical award record after the organisation's other data is erased an intentional, acceptable retention policy? This is a legal/policy question, not a technical one — flagged, not decided.
2. **Decision 18** (Q.5): which of Options A/B/C for canonical winner placement. Blocks further principled award/results CMS work until resolved; does not block anything else in this task.
3. **Migration 089's proposed `search_path` hardening** (Q.4 #5): a defense-in-depth correction is described but not applied — approve it (and the migration gets re-authored before any future application) or explicitly decide the current invoker-rights, EXECUTE-restricted design is sufficient as-is.
4. **Migration application itself**: all 4 prepared migrations (087/088/089/091) require your explicit authorisation and an operator with live Supabase access before any of them can be applied — this task could not and did not apply any of them.

All DB access goes through the parameterized Supabase JS client — no raw string-built SQL found anywhere touched this task, no injection risk. No secrets observed or reproduced. **Potential, not fixed**: no server-side field-level validation (non-negative price, URL format) on `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` writes beyond the admin UI — this is the same project-wide pattern used for every other URL/price field in the codebase, not a localized defect, so it was not singled out for a new validation framework.

### Q.10 — Production database-readiness runbook

This is a recommendation and verification guide only. **Nothing in this section was executed.** Written for a non-expert project owner, in three strictly separated phases.

**Phase 1 — Repository verification (no database access needed, can be done today):**
1. Confirm the 4 migration files exist and have not been altered: `ls -la migrations/087-*.sql migrations/088-*.sql migrations/089-*.sql migrations/091-*.sql`.
2. Read each file once more yourself (or have a technical reviewer do so) before authorising application — this report's Q.3 matrix summarizes what each one does, but the actual SQL is short enough to read directly.
3. Confirm no application code changed between this report and the time you apply anything, by re-running `npm test` and `npm run build` immediately before applying (both should show the same counts as Q.9).

**Phase 2 — Staging verification (requires a staging Supabase project, ideally a clone of production's schema with no real personal data):**
1. Apply migration 089 first (see Q.3's recommended order). Immediately after, run this read-only check (do not execute anything else) to confirm the function exists and is locked down correctly:
   ```sql
   -- Confirms the function exists with the right signature
   SELECT proname, pronargs FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'erase_organisation_gdpr';
   -- Confirms only service_role (and the table owner) can call it
   SELECT grantee, privilege_type FROM information_schema.routine_privileges
   WHERE routine_name = 'erase_organisation_gdpr';
   ```
2. **Verify GDPR erasure safely, without deleting real data**: create a throwaway test organisation in staging (a fake company with no real person's data), add a test contact, a test entry, and a test award assignment to it. Trigger erasure through the actual admin screen (not directly in SQL) and confirm: the test organisation and all its related test rows disappear from every one of the 13 tables migration 089 lists; the `gdpr_requests` row for it reaches `status: 'completed'`; the admin UI shows a success message. Then repeat with a deliberately-wrong ID (or a "contact" type request) and confirm this task's new fix shows a clear rejection message instead of a false "completed" status.
3. Apply 087 and 088. Re-run `npm test`'s live-equivalent checks if you have any staging-specific test script; otherwise, manually confirm via the Supabase dashboard's Table Editor → RLS tab that `sponsorship_opportunities` and the 22 tables from 088 no longer show policies granting anon/authenticated access.
4. Apply 091. **Verify the sponsorship catalogue**: log in to the admin as an `editor` or `marketing`-role user (this task's fix now lets both roles save packages), add one test package, publish it, and confirm it appears on the public `become-a-sponsor.html` page in staging, replacing the hardcoded Bronze/Gold/Platinum cards. Then unpublish it and confirm the page falls back to the hardcoded cards again.
5. **Verify award/results compatibility**: this is unaffected by any of the 4 migrations (none of them touch `awards`/`award_years`/`winners`/`award_assignments`), so no specific migration-related check is needed here — but before relying on public winner-page output in production, be aware Decision 18 (Q.5/Q.6) remains open, and a staging spot-check of a few real historical winners' displayed placement word ("Winner"/"Top 3"/"Finalist") against what you know to be true for those records is worth doing regardless of migration status.

**Phase 3 — Production verification (only after Phase 2 passes cleanly in staging):**
1. **Back up the production database first** — this is standard practice for any schema change, independent of this report; use Supabase's own backup/point-in-time-recovery feature before applying anything.
2. Apply the same 4 migrations in the same order used in staging, one at a time, re-running the Phase 2 read-only checks after each (adjusted to query production, not staging).
3. Do **not** run the GDPR erasure test-organisation drill against production with fabricated data — instead, confirm the function and its permissions exist (the read-only SQL in step 1 of Phase 2) without ever calling it, until a real, approved erasure request needs processing.
4. Monitor the `gdpr_requests` table for any requests that were stuck at `'processing'` from before this task's fix was deployed — they will need to be manually reviewed and either re-run or marked `'rejected'`, since this task's fix only prevents *future* stuck requests, it does not retroactively repair already-stuck ones (which would require a database write, out of scope for a local code task).

**What requires business approval vs. an authorised database operator:**
- Business approval needed: Q.6's 3 items (retention policy, Decision 18, search_path correction).
- Authorised database operator needed: literally applying any of the 4 migrations, in any environment — this was never possible from this sandbox and remains the single largest concrete step before production readiness.
