## R. BTA-076 — GDPR Workflow Repair, Migration 089 Correction and End-to-End Hardening (2026-11-02)

**Type: implementation task.** Unlike BTA-075 (pure review), this task was explicitly authorised to make local code, SQL and test changes. Live database connection, migration execution, deployment, and Git commit/push/branch operations remained explicitly **not** authorised and were not performed.

### R.1 — Starting state (verified before any edit)

Branch `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, HEAD `fd2e14522d2259c3cd0ceb73176e65db1096ec81` (unchanged from Q.14/BTA-075), 0 staged, 56 modified + 13 untracked = 69 changed entries — identical to BTA-075's final state. BTA-075's read-only findings (Section preceding this one, delivered only in chat, never written to a file) were treated as the starting evidence for this task's Section 2.

### R.2 — Canonical CRM table names (confirmed, not re-guessed)

Re-derived independently from the same evidence BTA-075 used, plus an explicit extra check this task's Section 2 required: whether any *other* table/column name (beyond the 3 already reported) was similarly wrong. Confirmed:

- **Canonical names: `communications`, `deals`, `meeting_notes`.** Source: `database-crm-setup.sql`'s actual `CREATE TABLE` statements; `crm.js`'s real CRUD calls (`apiClient.select('communications', ...)` etc.); `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_TABLES`/`MUTABLE_TABLES`/`ROLE_PERMISSIONS`, all of which agree with each other and with no RENAME migration existing anywhere.
- `crm_communications`/`crm_deals`/`crm_meetings` do not exist as tables anywhere in tracked SQL. These strings only ever appear as `utils.saveSortState('crm_communications', ...)` localStorage sort-preference keys in `crm.js` — confirmed (again) to be the likely source of the confusion that produced the bug in both `gdpr.js` and migration 089.
- **Extra check (new this task):** re-read all 13 table names in migration 089's cascade and all table names in `gdpr.js`'s `_exportEntityData` against `database-*.sql`/`migrations/*.sql` and `api/data-proxy.js`'s allow-lists one at a time: `email_logs`, `event_guests`, `public_votes`, `organisation_contacts`, `award_assignments`, `entries`, `organisation_images`, `organisation_notes`, `organisation_follow_ups`, `organisations` all matched correctly and consistently everywhere they're used. **No other mismatched table or column name was found** — the defect was confined to the 3 CRM names already identified in BTA-075.

### R.3 — GDPR export workflow, repaired (`gdpr.js`, `_exportEntityData`)

Two defects fixed, one of them newly found this task (not previously reported in BTA-074/075):

1. **Newly-found defect — silent failure swallowing.** `_exportEntityData` previously wrapped its own body in a `try/catch` that only logged (`console.error`) and toasted on failure, without rethrowing. Its only caller, `processRequest`, therefore never saw any exception from export — meaning **every** export request was marked `'completed'` with a success toast regardless of whether the export actually produced real data, including the exact case where the crm_-table-name bug would have thrown "relation does not exist" on every call. Fixed by removing the internal catch so genuine query failures propagate to `processRequest`'s own existing catch block (which already correctly reverts the request to `'pending'` and records the error).
2. **Table-name correction.** The 3 `crm_`-prefixed names were corrected to `communications`/`deals`/`meeting_notes`.
3. **Missing-organisation handling.** Added `if (!org) throw new Error('Organisation not found -- nothing to export.');` so a GDPR export request for a non-existent/already-erased organisation now fails loudly (and reverts to `'pending'` with an explanatory note) instead of silently exporting an empty or null record as if it had succeeded.
4. **Organisation scoping re-verified, not changed.** Every related-record query in `_exportEntityData` already filtered strictly by the requested `entityId` — confirmed (and now covered by a dedicated regression test, R.8) that no query mixes in another organisation's rows.

### R.4 — Migration 089, corrected and hardened

File: `migrations/089-secure-gdpr-erasure-rpc.sql` (131 lines after this task's rewrite). This is the one historical migration file BTA-076 explicitly authorised to edit — because it was never applied anywhere, correcting it in place (rather than layering a second migration on top of a no-op) does not touch any live-effective schema.

**Before (as authored in its original task, never applied):**
- `RETURNS VOID`, 13 unconditional `DELETE`s including the 3 wrong CRM table names, no existence check, no `search_path` pinning, plain `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`.

**After (this task):**
- `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS erase_organisation_gdpr(UUID);` added immediately before the definition — required because Postgres cannot change an existing function's return type via `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` alone (VOID → TEXT here); safe no-op on a first application, only relevant if this file is ever re-run against a database with an earlier VOID-returning version.
- Added `SET search_path = public` (explicitly **without** `pg_temp`, per BTA-075's correction of BTA-074's original proposal — `pg_temp` is always implicitly searched first regardless, so appending it adds no real protection; documented in the file's own header comment).
- **Return-contract fix (the critical correction Section 4 required):** `RETURNS VOID` → `RETURNS TEXT`. The function now does `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM organisations WHERE id = p_org_id) INTO v_existed;` first, returns `'not_found'` immediately if the organisation doesn't exist (no deletes attempted), otherwise runs the 13 (now correctly-named) `DELETE`s and returns `'erased'`. This closes the exact gap Section 4 described: a syntactically valid but non-existent or already-erased UUID can no longer complete "successfully" in a way indistinguishable from a real erasure.
- 3 table names corrected: `crm_communications`→`communications`, `crm_deals`→`deals`, `crm_meetings`→`meeting_notes`.
- **Not changed, re-reviewed and confirmed correct as-is:** `SECURITY INVOKER` (left as the default — not changed to `SECURITY DEFINER`, per the task's explicit prohibition; the function's only real-world caller is `api/data-proxy.js`'s service-role client, so invoker-rights execution is already correct and sufficient); the two `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` statements (unchanged, both this function's and the sibling `anonymize_organisation`'s); table list, order (children-first, organisations-last), and scope (unchanged, `invoices`/`payments` still deliberately excluded, matching the pre-existing — not newly decided — retention behaviour); atomicity (unchanged — the whole function still runs as one statement inside the calling transaction, so a mid-cascade failure on any future schema change still rolls back every delete already run in the same call, with no explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` needed).
- **Still not applied.** No Supabase connection was made or attempted. This remains a local source-file edit only.

### R.5 — GDPR erasure workflow, repaired (`gdpr.js`, `_deleteEntityData` and `processRequest`)

1. **`_deleteEntityData` now enforces migration 089's new return contract:**
   ```js
   async _deleteEntityData(entityId) {
     const result = await apiClient.rpc('erase_organisation_gdpr', { p_org_id: entityId });
     if (result?.data !== 'erased') {
       throw new Error('Organisation not found or already erased -- nothing to erase.');
     }
   },
   ```
   Anything other than the literal string `'erased'` (including `'not_found'`, `null`, `undefined`, or any unexpected shape) is now treated as a failure and thrown, so `processRequest` reverts the request to `'pending'` instead of marking it `'completed'`. This directly satisfies Section 4's requirement that a missing organisation never be reported as a successful no-op.
2. **Already-completed-request guard added to `processRequest`.** Before proceeding past the existing UUID-validity check, the function now re-reads the request's current status and refuses to re-run the operation (showing a toast: `This request is already "<status>" -- refresh the list before processing it again.`) if it is anything other than `'pending'`. This defends against a stale UI, a double-click, or a second open browser tab re-triggering an already-completed erasure's RPC call and creating a second, misleading audit-log entry for an operation that had already happened — a scenario the admin UI's own `_renderRequests` (which only shows the Process button for `status === 'pending'`) made *unlikely* through the UI, but `processRequest` itself previously trusted the caller's state blindly with no server-side (well, proxy-side) re-check.
3. **Entity-type rejection for contact requests — re-verified unchanged, not re-implemented.** BTA-074's fix (rejecting `entity_type: 'contact'` cleanly rather than misrouting it to organisation-erasure logic) was re-read and confirmed still intact and correct. No new contact-erasure capability was added or implied — exactly as Section 5 required ("do not invent a contact-erasure implementation").
4. **Audit-log timing limitation — documented, not fixed.** `executeRpc` in `api/data-proxy.js` calls `logActivity` strictly *after* the RPC resolves, in a separate statement; `logActivity` itself swallows its own errors (`console.error` only) so a logging failure never propagates back to the caller. This means a successful erasure with a failed audit-log write is possible and would currently go unnoticed by the caller. Making this atomic would require wrapping the audit-log insert in the same database transaction as the RPC itself (e.g. having the Postgres function write its own audit row), which is a wider architectural change than this task's scope — recorded here as a known limitation, not fixed, and not claimed to be guaranteed.

### R.6 — Winner-name retention (Section 6) — re-investigated, not changed

Re-confirmed this task made **no** change to `winners.winner_name`, retention logic, public winner presentation, or certificate generation. Migration 089's table list still excludes `winners` entirely (re-verified by a fresh grep of the final file — no match). This remains the same open business decision recorded in Q.6; nothing new was found that changes that assessment.

### R.7 — Decision 18 (Section 7) — not implemented

Re-confirmed `winner_position` vs `placement` was **not** touched this task. `git diff --stat winners.js assignments.js awards.js public-winners-app.js` shows only the pre-existing BTA-074 diffs (the blank-placement-defaulting-to-"Winner" fix) — no new edits were made to any of these files in BTA-076. The results-model choice remains an open business decision, unresolved, exactly as Section 7 requires.

### R.8 — Tests added/updated (`tests/gdpr.test.js`)

9 new tests added (full suite total rose from the BTA-074/075 baseline of 6758 passed to 6767 passed — an exact +9, confirming no test was silently dropped elsewhere):

- `processRequest`: `'refuses to reprocess a request that is already completed (BTA-076)'`, `'refuses to reprocess a request that has already been rejected'`, `'allows processing when the request is confirmed pending'`, `'a delete request is reverted to pending, not marked completed, when the RPC reports the organisation was not found (BTA-076)'`, `'an export request is reverted to pending, not marked completed, when the organisation cannot be found (BTA-076)'`.
- `_deleteEntityData`: `'treats a "not_found" result as a failure rather than a successful no-op (BTA-076)'`, `'treats a missing/unexpected RPC return value as a failure, not a success'`.
- `_exportEntityData`: `'queries the canonical CRM table names, not the non-existent crm_-prefixed ones (BTA-076)'`, `'scopes every related-record query to the requested organisation id only, never mixing in another organisation'`.

In addition, 3 pre-existing tests were rewritten (not deleted) because the behaviour they asserted was itself the bug being fixed:
- `'handles null org result'` → `'throws when the organisation cannot be found, rather than exporting a null/empty record (BTA-076)'` (now asserts the function throws, via `.rejects.toThrow`, instead of asserting it silently exported nothing).
- `'shows error on export failure'` → `'propagates a query failure to the caller instead of swallowing it (BTA-076)'` (now asserts the error propagates instead of asserting it was caught and toasted internally).
- `'erases the entity via a single atomic RPC call, not 13 separate unguarded deletes'`: mock return value updated from `{ data: null }` to `{ data: 'erased' }` to match the new return contract (the assertion itself — one RPC call, not 13 deletes — is unchanged).

A `beforeEach` default (`apiClient.select` resolving `{ data: [{ status: 'pending' }] }`) was added to the `processRequest` describe block so the ~12 pre-existing tests that didn't anticipate the new already-completed guard's extra `apiClient.select` call continue to exercise their original scenario unaffected; individual tests needing a different status override this default explicitly. No existing assertion was weakened to make a test pass — every rewritten test asserts an equal or stricter condition than before.

### R.10 — Database application checklist (prerequisites only — nothing applied)

This supersedes nothing in Q.10's own runbook for migrations 087/088/091, which are unaffected by this task. It adds the corrected content for migration 089 specifically:

**Prerequisites before migration 089 can be applied anywhere:**
1. An authorised database operator with access to the target Supabase project (this sandbox has none).
2. Confirmation of which environment is being targeted (test/staging project vs. production) — do not apply directly to production first.
3. A recent backup or point-in-time-recovery checkpoint of the target database (standard practice, independent of this migration).

**Expected objects after a correct application:**
- Function `erase_organisation_gdpr(p_org_id UUID) RETURNS TEXT` exists, `SECURITY INVOKER` (default), `search_path` pinned to `public`.
- `EXECUTE` on both `erase_organisation_gdpr` and `anonymize_organisation` revoked from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, `authenticated` — i.e. only `service_role` (and the owning role) can call either function.

**Pre-application verification (read-only, safe to run against a live database before applying anything):**
```sql
-- Confirms a prior VOID-returning version isn't still live under a different expectation
SELECT pg_get_function_identity_arguments(oid), prorettype::regtype
FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'erase_organisation_gdpr';
```

**Post-application verification (read-only):**
```sql
-- Confirms the new signature and TEXT return type took effect
SELECT proname, prorettype::regtype FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'erase_organisation_gdpr';
-- Confirms only service_role can execute either GDPR RPC
SELECT grantee, privilege_type FROM information_schema.routine_privileges
WHERE routine_name IN ('erase_organisation_gdpr', 'anonymize_organisation');
```

**Checks that require a live database and cannot be done locally:**
- Whether any earlier (VOID-returning) version of `erase_organisation_gdpr` is already live anywhere (this task's `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` handles this safely either way, but confirming it beforehand is good practice).
- The staging GDPR drill itself (create a throwaway test organisation with no real personal data, trigger erasure through the actual admin screen, confirm all 13 tables' rows for it disappear and the request reaches `'completed'`; then repeat with a non-existent UUID and confirm the request is correctly reverted to `'pending'` with an explanatory note rather than marked `'completed''`) — **this live drill is still required** and was not and cannot be performed from this sandbox.
- Whether migration 089 has ever been applied anywhere before under its original (pre-BTA-076) definition — if so, the `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` in this corrected version safely handles replacing it, but this should still be confirmed rather than assumed.

**Do not treat any of the above as done.** No migration was applied, no live database was connected to, and no staging drill was run as part of this task.

## S. BTA-077 — CMS Production Readiness, Migration Reconciliation and Outstanding Work Completion (2026-11-02)

**Type: implementation task**, explicitly a "substantial continuation of BTA-074/075/076". Local code, test, documentation and SQL-authoring changes were authorised; live database connection, migration application, deployment, and Git commit/push/branch operations were not, and none occurred.

### S.1 — Starting state (verified, not assumed)

Branch `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, HEAD `fd2e14522d2259c3cd0ceb73176e65db1096ec81` — both **confirmed identical** to the values BTA-076's own report stated (the task's prompt explicitly asked for independent verification rather than trusting that report). 0 staged. 69 modified/untracked files (56 modified + 13 untracked), also confirmed identical to BTA-076's final count. `BTA-CMS-PROJECT-TASKS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` both already carried pre-existing unstaged changes (from earlier tasks in this series) — preserved, only appended to. BTA-003's homepage cleanup re-confirmed intact (`home-blue*.html`/`home2.html`/`home2.js` absent).

### S.2 — BTA-076 verification (Section 3 of this task)

Read the full current `gdpr.js` and `migrations/089-secure-gdpr-erasure-rpc.sql` directly (not from memory of the prior task) and cross-checked every claim in BTA-076's own report against the actual file content:

- The 3 CRM table names (`communications`/`deals`/`meeting_notes`) are correctly used in `_exportEntityData` — confirmed by direct read.
- Export errors propagate correctly — confirmed: no internal `try/catch` remains in `_exportEntityData`.
- A missing organisation cannot be reported as successfully exported (`_exportEntityData` throws `'Organisation not found'`) or erased (`_deleteEntityData` requires `result?.data === 'erased'`) — confirmed.
- The new RPC return contract (`'erased'`/`'not_found'`) is correctly interpreted on the client side, and confirmed end-to-end: `api/data-proxy.js`'s `executeRpc` (line 1162) returns `{ data }` verbatim from `supabase.rpc()`'s own `data` field, sent straight through `res.status(200).json(rpcResult)`, and `apiClient.rpc`'s `_call` returns the parsed JSON body unchanged — so a TEXT-returning Postgres function's value reaches `gdpr.js` exactly as `{ data: 'erased' }`, matching what `_deleteEntityData` checks. No translation-layer gap exists anywhere in this chain.
- Already-completed/rejected requests cannot be reprocessed — confirmed, `processRequest`'s status re-check (added by BTA-076) is present and unchanged.
- Failed requests revert to `'pending'` (the only retryable status the `gdpr_requests.status` CHECK constraint, `migrations/015`, allows) — confirmed.
- Contact requests are rejected before any processing path runs — confirmed, unchanged from BTA-074/076.
- UUID validation (`_isValidUuid`) runs before any RPC call — confirmed, unchanged.
- The audit-log timing limitation is accurately documented in `gdpr.js`'s own comments and was independently re-verified against `api/data-proxy.js`'s `executeRpc` (the RPC call and `logActivity` call are two separate statements; `logActivity` swallows its own errors) — the documentation is accurate, not fixed (same conclusion as BTA-076, confirmed rather than merely repeated).

**No defect was found in BTA-076's own work.** Two further, newly-found issues (below, S.3) were identified by applying the *same* scrutiny BTA-076 received to the sibling GDPR RPC it didn't touch.

### S.3 — New defects found and fixed this task

**S.3.1 — `anonymize_organisation` had the identical silent-no-op defect BTA-076 fixed for `erase_organisation_gdpr`, and was never fixed.** `anonymize_organisation(p_org_id UUID) RETURNS VOID` (defined identically in `migrations/015-localstorage-to-supabase.sql:306` and `migrations/046-catchup-missing-objects.sql:1545` — confirmed byte-for-byte identical bodies, so 046 is an idempotent re-creation, not a conflicting second definition) ran two `UPDATE ... WHERE id = p_org_id` statements with no existence check and no return value. A non-existent or already-anonymised `p_org_id` updates zero rows, raises no error, and returns successfully — indistinguishable from a real anonymisation. `gdpr.js`'s `processRequest` called this RPC (previously inline, `apiClient.rpc('anonymize_organisation', ...)`) and did not inspect the result at all, unconditionally marking the request `'completed'` afterwards. This is the exact bug class BTA-076 fixed for the erasure path, left unaddressed for the anonymisation path.

  **Fix:** `migrations/092-fix-anonymize-organisation-return-contract.sql` (new file) — `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` (required here, unlike 089's low-risk case, because this function has a real, demonstrated prior-existence path via migration 015/046, so this DROP is likely necessary on a real database, not just defensive), `RETURNS TEXT` returning `'anonymized'`/`'not_found'` after an explicit existence check, `SET search_path = public`, and a `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` (independently repeated here even though migration 089 already contains an identical revoke for this same function — harmless duplication, and makes 092 self-contained rather than silently depending on 089 being applied first). `SECURITY INVOKER` (the unchanged default — no `SECURITY DEFINER` keyword was ever present) and the UPDATE statements' own logic were left unchanged. `gdpr.js` gained a new `_anonymizeEntityData(entityId)` method (mirroring `_deleteEntityData`'s shape) that throws unless the RPC returns exactly `'anonymized'`; `processRequest`'s `anonymize` branch now calls this method instead of the RPC inline. **Not applied to any database.**

**S.3.2 — An unrecognised `request_type` fell through `processRequest` with no handler and was still marked `'completed'`.** `gdpr_requests.request_type` has a DB-level `CHECK` constraint (`migrations/015`) limiting it to `'export'`/`'delete'`/`'anonymize'`, so this is not reachable through the normal submit flow — but the code itself gave no defense if it ever were (e.g. a future request type added to the CHECK constraint without a corresponding handler being added here, or a direct API call bypassing the UI's `<select>`). **Fix:** added an `else { throw new Error(...) }` branch so an unhandled type now correctly reverts the request to `'pending'` with an explanatory note, consistent with the task's explicit requirement that a request must never be marked completed "simply because an API call returned without throwing an exception" — the same principle applies to a code path that never made a call at all.

**S.3.3 — Two inconsistent error-message leaks (Low severity), found by this task's security-focused investigation fork.** `api/judge-automation.js`'s `cronTickEndpoint` (line ~648) returned the raw `error.message` to the client on a 500, unlike every other endpoint in the same file (which all return `'An internal error occurred'` and log the real error server-side via `console.error`) — gated behind `CRON_SECRET` so not reachable by an unauthenticated caller, but still an inconsistency worth closing. `api/ai-vetting.js` and `api/social-media-api.js` both included a `details: error.message` field on their 503 "not configured" responses, inconsistent with the same no-internals convention. **Fix:** all three now follow the established generic-message-plus-server-log pattern; no behavioural change to the success path or to when these branches fire.

### S.4 — Migration 089: final compatibility and security conclusion

Re-reviewed `migrations/089-secure-gdpr-erasure-rpc.sql`'s current (BTA-076-corrected) content line by line against this task's own checklist (Section 4):

- **Signature/dependencies:** `erase_organisation_gdpr(UUID) RETURNS TEXT` is defined in exactly one place in the entire repository (`migrations/089` itself) — confirmed by a fresh repository-wide grep; no overload, no earlier or conflicting definition exists anywhere in tracked SQL. The only caller is `gdpr.js`'s `_deleteEntityData`, already updated to expect the new return type. `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS erase_organisation_gdpr(UUID)` targets only that exact signature, so it cannot accidentally remove an unrelated overload.
- **Compatibility across schema states:** (1) fresh database — `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` is a safe no-op, `CREATE` succeeds. (2) old function absent — identical to (1), and this is in fact the only state ever demonstrated anywhere in this project's own history, since `erase_organisation_gdpr` has never existed outside this one migration file. (3) old (VOID-returning, pre-BTA-076) version already live — the `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` added by BTA-076 handles this correctly (required specifically because `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` cannot change a return type). (4) an unexpected signature/dependency — no evidence this has ever occurred; this is the one state that would require a live database check to rule out with certainty, and is recorded as such in S.6 below rather than assumed.
- **Security:** `SECURITY INVOKER` (the unchanged default), `SET search_path = public` (hardening, not closing a real privilege-escalation path, since no `SECURITY DEFINER` is involved), `REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` present, no dynamic SQL anywhere in the function body, `p_org_id` is a properly-typed parameter (not string-interpolated), so no SQL-injection surface exists. Confirmed the application's own execution boundary: `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_RPCS` requires `super_admin` to invoke this RPC via the proxy, and the proxy itself always connects to Supabase with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` (confirmed at `api/data-proxy.js:31`) — so the function's real-world caller is always `service_role`, consistent with `SECURITY INVOKER` being correct and sufficient as-is. **Not broadened or changed to `SECURITY DEFINER`, per the task's explicit prohibition.**
- **Decision:** the current migration 089 is safe and internally consistent. **Retained without further change this task** (its own correction already happened in BTA-076; this task only re-verified it).

### S.5 — Migrations 087/088/089/091/092 reconciliation

All five migration files were re-read in full this task (not assumed from prior summaries). None of the five share, modify, or conflict with any object defined by another:

| Migration | Object(s) affected | Type | App dependency | Idempotent | Prerequisite | Verified this task |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 087 | `sponsorship_opportunities` (RLS policies + grants) | Permission-hardening only | None found (not in `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ALLOWED_TABLES`, no frontend reference) | Yes (`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS`, `duplicate_object` exception handling, `REVOKE` is a no-op if already revoked) | None | Re-read in full; consistent with its own header's claims |
| 088 | 22 named tables (RLS policies + grants) | Permission-hardening only | None found for any of the 22 (re-confirmed by this task's security-focused fork, which cross-checked `rbac.js` against `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` for every role) | Yes, same pattern as 087 | None | Re-read in full |
| 089 | `erase_organisation_gdpr` (function) | Function creation/correction + permission-hardening | `gdpr.js`'s `_deleteEntityData` | Yes (`DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` then `CREATE`) | None | Re-reviewed per S.4 |
| 091 | `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` (new table) | Additive (new table) + its own fresh RLS | `marketing.js` (admin), `become-a-sponsor-packages.js` (public, via `api/entry-proxy.js`) — confirmed end-to-end wiring correct by this task's investigation fork, including the `enquiry_destination` consumption fix and the static Bronze/Gold/Platinum fallback remaining intact when the table is absent or empty | Yes (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` before `CREATE POLICY`) | None | Re-confirmed end-to-end by fork investigation, not just re-read |
| 092 (new, this task) | `anonymize_organisation` (function) | Function correction + permission-hardening | `gdpr.js`'s new `_anonymizeEntityData` | Yes, same pattern as 089 | None (independent of 089 despite the duplicated `REVOKE`) | Authored and reviewed this task, see S.3.1 |

**No cross-migration conflict exists.** All five are mutually independent at the SQL object level and can technically be applied in any order. The only *practical* grouping recommendation is to apply 089 and 092 together (or at least verify both with the same staging GDPR drill before relying on either in production), since they fix the identical bug class in the two halves of the same feature. **Proposed application order (operationally convenient, not technically required): 087 → 088 → 089 → 092 → 091**, each followed by its own read-only post-application check (S.6). None of the five were applied, executed, or connected-to this task.

**Migration 090 and Northern Ireland:** confirmed absent (`ls migrations/` shows no 090 file) and confirmed, via a fresh repository-wide grep this task (re-run independently of the awards/taxonomy investigation fork, which found the same result), that no live application code, config, UI, seed, or test reintroduces Northern Ireland — the only matches anywhere are explanatory comments and negative-assertion test names. This remains a permanently excluded, settled business decision; nothing was changed.

### S.6 — GDPR workflow status after this task

All required outcome categories (Section 6) are now distinguished correctly by `gdpr.js`:

| Outcome | How it's now distinguished |
|---|---|
| Confirmed success | RPC/handler completes and (for erase/anonymize) returns the exact expected success token; request marked `'completed'` |
| Missing organisation | `_exportEntityData`/`_deleteEntityData`/`_anonymizeEntityData` all throw a specific, accurately-worded error; request reverts to `'pending'` with that message in `notes` |
| Invalid identifier | `_isValidUuid` check, before any status change or RPC call; distinct toast, request untouched |
| Unsupported request type | Entity-type (`'contact'`) rejected before any status change (BTA-074); request-type (anything other than export/delete/anonymize) now also rejected (S.3.2, new this task), reverting to `'pending'` rather than silently completing |
| Database/RPC error | Propagates from `apiClient.rpc`/`apiClient.select`/`apiClient.selectAll` through to `processRequest`'s catch block; request reverts to `'pending'` with the real error text recorded |
| Already-completed request | Re-checked against the live DB status immediately before processing (BTA-076); clear toast, no reprocessing |
| Retryable failure | Every failure path reverts to `'pending'` (the only status the CHECK constraint permits for this purpose), so the existing Process/Reject UI controls remain available for a retry |

Could an administrator still mistakenly believe an organisation has been erased when it has not? **No new path was found this task.** Every failure mode above now reverts the request's status and shows an error toast rather than the success toast — the admin UI's own `_renderRequests` only shows `'completed'`/`'rejected'` without action buttons, and a reverted-to-`'pending'` request keeps its Process/Reject buttons, visually distinguishing it from a genuinely completed one.

**Audit-log limitation:** unchanged from BTA-076's own finding — `api/data-proxy.js`'s `executeRpc` logs activity in a separate statement after the RPC resolves, and `logActivity` swallows its own errors. This remains documented, not fixed, as a known architectural limitation (fixing it would require the Postgres function to write its own audit row in the same transaction, which is a wider change than this task's scope).

**Winner-name retention:** re-confirmed `winners.winner_name` was not touched, migration 089's (and the new migration 092's) object lists do not include `winners` at all, and no change was made to public winner presentation or certificates. This remains the same open business decision recorded since Q.6 — nothing new changes that assessment.

**Contact requests:** re-confirmed no contact-erasure capability was invented; `entity_type: 'contact'` is still rejected cleanly before any processing path runs.

### S.7 — Other critical CMS workflow findings (Section 7)

Four parallel read-only investigation forks covered Sections 7A–7H. **No Critical or High-severity code defect was found in any of the eight areas.** Summary (full detail retained in each fork's own findings, synthesized here):

- **A. Public entry/nomination:** organisation matching (`api/_lib/organisation-matching.js`), CSV import (`award_area_import` action in `api/data-proxy.js`), server-side validation, and error handling are all confirmed working. One item requires a business decision, not a code fix: nothing currently prevents the same organisation submitting multiple entries to the same award/category beyond a per-email rate limit — there is no established rule to compare against, so this is recorded as an open question, not implemented.
- **B. Organisations and CRM:** `crm.js`'s CRUD for `communications`/`deals`/`meeting_notes` confirmed consistently using the canonical table names (no stale `crm_`-prefixed references anywhere), correctly gated in `api/data-proxy.js`. No cross-organisation leakage pattern found in the paths inspected.
- **C. Awards and award years:** admin CRUD, duplicate prevention, and the view/trigger column-list parity (migrations 068/069) all confirmed working. One Low-severity, business-decision-gated gap: no backend enforcement of award eligibility by area/sector exists (the submit UI only offers matching awards; nothing stops a direct API call from submitting an ineligible entry) — not implemented, since how strict this should be is a business question.
- **D. Sectors, categories and locations:** custom sectors/categories confirmed consistent between `settings.js` and `awards.js`; England/Scotland/Wales-only geography re-confirmed intact with no Northern Ireland regression.
- **E. CMS content and public website:** every CMS-config key name confirmed to match exactly between writer (`settings.js`) and reader (`api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleGetPublicData`) — the specific "key-name mismatch between writer and reader" bug class this task asked to check for was not found anywhere in this area. Already correctly implemented.
- **F. Sponsorship:** full admin-to-public path (`marketing.js` → `api/data-proxy.js` → `api/entry-proxy.js` → `become-a-sponsor-packages.js`) confirmed correctly wired, including the `enquiry_destination` fix and the static-fallback behaviour when migration 091 is unapplied or the catalogue is empty. No defect found.
- **G. Admin access and security:** `rbac.js`'s 7 roles cross-checked against `api/data-proxy.js`'s `ROLE_PERMISSIONS`/`ALLOWED_RPCS` for every role — no case found of the server granting broader access than the client-side model implies. No hardcoded secrets found anywhere in tracked source. The two error-message leaks found here were fixed (S.3.3).
- **H. Build and deployment configuration:** all 23 root-level `*.html` pages confirmed present in `build.js`'s copy list (the previously-reported `public-winners.html` gap, VC-M1, is confirmed already fixed). Exactly 12 files in `/api/` — `CLAUDE.md`'s "12/12" claim is accurate. Every `process.env.X` read across `api/*.js` is documented somewhere in `CLAUDE.md`'s environment-variable tables. `vercel.json`'s SPA catch-all rewrite is present and working (the previously-reported VC-C1 gap is confirmed already fixed). No unintended static-file exposure found.

No homepage redesign, visual/branding change, season-stage work, results-model change, or new commercial feature was attempted or recommended, per this task's explicit scope control.

### S.11 — Database application prerequisites and read-only verification (migrations 087/088/089/091/092)

All of the following are **checks to run**, not actions already taken. Nothing in this section was executed.

**Prerequisites (all five migrations):** an authorised database operator; a decision on target environment (test/staging first, never production first); a recent backup/point-in-time-recovery checkpoint before any application.

**Pre-application read-only checks:**
```sql
-- Confirms no earlier/conflicting definition of either GDPR function already exists
-- with an unexpected signature (rules out schema state "4" from S.4)
SELECT proname, pg_get_function_identity_arguments(oid), prorettype::regtype
FROM pg_proc WHERE proname IN ('erase_organisation_gdpr', 'anonymize_organisation');
-- Expect: anonymize_organisation(uuid) returning void (pre-092) or text (post-092);
-- erase_organisation_gdpr may not exist yet (pre-089) or return text (post-089).
-- Unexpected: any OTHER argument signature for either name -- stop and investigate
-- before applying, do not assume DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS will target it correctly.

-- Confirms sponsorship_catalogue_packages does not already exist with a different shape
SELECT column_name, data_type FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_name = 'sponsorship_catalogue_packages';
-- Expect: zero rows (table doesn't exist yet). Unexpected: any rows -- stop, the
-- table already exists with an unknown origin, investigate before applying 091.
```
Both are read-only `SELECT`s against system/information-schema catalogs — safe to run in any environment including production.

**Post-application checks (run after each migration, in the proposed 087→088→089→092→091 order):**
```sql
-- After 087/088: confirms no permissive anon/authenticated policy remains
SELECT tablename, policyname, roles, qual FROM pg_policies
WHERE tablename IN ('sponsorship_opportunities', 'announcements', 'event_budgets' /* ...rest of 088's 22 */)
  AND (roles @> ARRAY['anon']::name[] OR roles @> ARRAY['authenticated']::name[]);
-- Expect: zero rows. Unexpected: any row -- a permissive policy survived, investigate.

-- After 089/092: confirms the new signatures and lockdown took effect
SELECT proname, prorettype::regtype FROM pg_proc
WHERE proname IN ('erase_organisation_gdpr', 'anonymize_organisation');
-- Expect: both return 'text'.
SELECT grantee, privilege_type FROM information_schema.routine_privileges
WHERE routine_name IN ('erase_organisation_gdpr', 'anonymize_organisation');
-- Expect: no row for anon/authenticated/PUBLIC -- only service_role (and/or the owner).

-- After 091: confirms the table and its lockdown exist
SELECT to_regclass('public.sponsorship_catalogue_packages');
-- Expect: non-null. Unexpected: null -- migration did not apply cleanly.
```
All of the above are read-only and safe to run in production once applied there; running them before applying anything (expecting the "not yet" result) is equally safe.

**Staging GDPR drill (still required, not performed):** create a disposable test organisation with synthetic data (no real personal data) in a staging/test Supabase project; add one test contact, one test `communications` row, one test `deals` row, one test `meeting_notes` row, one test award assignment, one test entry. Then, via the actual admin screen (not direct SQL): (1) trigger an export request and confirm the downloaded JSON contains all of the above under the correct canonical keys; (2) trigger an anonymize request and confirm `organisations.company_name` becomes `REDACTED-<id prefix>` and the contact's name/email/phone are redacted, while unrelated organisations are untouched; (3) trigger an erasure request on a second disposable organisation and confirm all of its rows disappear from every table in migration 089's list; (4) repeat erasure and anonymize against a non-existent UUID and confirm each reverts to `'pending'` with an explanatory note, not `'completed'`; (5) confirm the `gdpr_requests` audit trail (`processed_by`/`processed_at`/`notes`) is accurate for every one of the above. This conceptually covers successful erasure, missing organisation, already-erased organisation (re-run step 3 a second time and confirm the second attempt correctly reports `'not_found'`), CRM records, related records across all affected tables, unrelated organisations, retry behaviour, and audit logging — exactly the coverage this task's Section 10 required. **Not executed.**

## T. BTA-078 through BTA-082 — Live Production Security Verification, Migration 093, and Supabase Test-Project Connectivity Blocker (2026-11-02)

This section was not written at the time each of these five tasks completed (several explicitly deferred or restricted documentation updates); it is added retroactively by BTA-083 to close the resulting continuity gap before it could cause work to be lost. It summarises each task's own full report rather than re-deriving the findings — the full reports exist in this project's session history. Nothing in this section reflects new investigation by BTA-083 itself.

### T.1 — BTA-078: Supabase Database Readiness and Migration Verification

First live, read-only connection this series made to **production** (`qdzyknercdqwhwijbcxf`, "british-trade-awards-cms", `eu-west-2`) specifically to check migrations 087/088/089/091/092. Confirmed: production has **no Supabase CLI migration-history table at all** (`supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` does not exist) — application status can only be established by inspecting live objects directly. All five migrations confirmed **not applied**: `erase_organisation_gdpr` does not exist; `anonymize_organisation` exists but is still the old `RETURNS void`, unrevoked, no-search-path version (full `EXECUTE` granted to `PUBLIC`/`anon`/`authenticated`); `sponsorship_catalogue_packages` does not exist; `sponsorship_opportunities` and all 22 of migration 088's tables still carry their original permissive policy and full `anon`/`authenticated` grants. **New finding, beyond the task's own named scope**: `organisations`, `organisation_contacts`, `gdpr_requests`, `entries`, `award_assignments`, `invoices`, `payments`, `award_years`, `event_guests` were also found fully exposed — traced to migration 052 never having been applied (and, even if applied as written, not actually fixing anything — see T.3). The test project (`htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp`) was found **hibernated** and unreachable for query execution throughout this task.

### T.2 — BTA-079: Production Security Exposure Verification and Remediation Plan

Re-verified, with no sampling, that all 32 tables named above (the 8-plus-`event_guests` from T.1 plus migration 088's 22) carry an identical `PERMISSIVE` `"Allow all access to <table>"` policy (`roles={public}`, `USING(true) WITH CHECK(true)`) and full `{SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,TRUNCATE,REFERENCES,TRIGGER}` grants to `anon`/`authenticated`, with RLS "enabled" but rendered meaningless by the permissive policy. Confirmed, from the live function definition and grants alone (no function was called), that a caller holding only the public `anon` key could invoke `anonymize_organisation` end-to-end today, and — because the function is `SECURITY INVOKER` — that this is really a special case of the much larger problem that `anon` already has direct `UPDATE` on `organisations`/`organisation_contacts` regardless of the function. Found that `migrations/052-rls-policies.sql` never drops the original permissive policy on any of its 7 target tables, so applying it as written would not have fixed anything, and that it has a **separate, previously-undocumented conflict with `migrations/045-proper-rls-policies.sql`**, which defines a different (`authenticated`-read-allowed) intended end-state for the same tables. Traced `check-in-app.js`'s real, deployed dependency on direct `authenticated` access to `event_guests` (confirmed via migrations 078/079, which already solve it correctly) as the one deliberate exception among all 32 tables.

### T.3 — BTA-080: Corrective RLS Migration Preparation and Review (migration 093)

Authored `migrations/093-harden-remaining-permissive-policy-tables.sql` (new file) covering the 8 tables not already correctly handled by an existing migration: `organisations`, `organisation_contacts`, `gdpr_requests`, `entries`, `award_assignments`, `invoices`, `payments`, `award_years`. Resolved the 045-vs-052 conflict with fresh evidence: a repository-wide grep of every browser-facing file found **zero** direct `supabase.from()`/`supabase.rpc()` dependency on any of these 8 tables (the only such dependency anywhere in the repo is `check-in-app.js`'s `events`/`event_guests` calls) — so the migration locks all 8 to `service_role_all`/`no_direct_client_access` with no `authenticated`-read carve-out, matching 052's intent rather than 045's. `event_guests` is **deliberately excluded** from migration 093 (handled completely by 078+079 already; touching it again would add risk for no benefit). Added `tests/migration-093.test.js` — 40 static tests on the SQL source text (policy presence/ordering, no `event_guests` mention, no duplicate function definitions, no `GRANT` reintroduction) — explicitly not a live-database proof. Full suite 82/6812/0 failed, lint/build clean. **Neither file has been applied to any database.**

## V. BTA-094 and BTA-095 — Nomination Submission Failure: Investigation and Repair (2026-11-02)

### V.1 — BTA-094: Nomination Submission Failure Contract Investigation (pure investigation, no file changes)

Follow-up to BTA-088's finding that both public nomination journeys were completely broken (every submission returning HTTP 400). Traced the exact contract mismatch: `api/entry-proxy.js`'s `handleSubmitNomination` branches on `awardCategory === 'New Business of the Year'` and requires either `nomineeName`+`nomineeCompany` (person branch, min 2 chars each) or `businessName`+`businessOwner` (business branch, min 2 chars each) — but neither `nominate.html`/`nominate.js` nor `industry-leader.html`/`industry-leader.js` collected or sent an `awardCategory`, a `nomineeCompany`, or any of the business-branch fields at all; both forms sent only a single flat `nomineeName` (in `nominate.js`'s case, holding the *company* name, not a person's name) with no category selector of any kind. Delivered a root-cause analysis and two repair options in chat (Option A: relax the server contract; Option B: fix both forms' fields/payloads to match the existing, unmodified server contract) — no files were changed. User approved "the refined Option B" with five explicit requirements (shared category list across both forms; both forms collect what their selected nomination type requires; person nominations identify the nominee's company; New Business nominations identify the business and its owner; preserve existing server-side validation/data model wherever possible), opening BTA-095.

### V.2 — BTA-095: Repair Nomination Forms and Align Submission Contracts (implementation)

**Scope:** `nominate.html`, `nominate.js`, `industry-leader.html`, `industry-leader.js` and their tests only. `api/entry-proxy.js` was read and re-verified in full but **not modified** — the approved repair was achievable entirely frontend-side, so the task's "stop and explain" escape clause (for a case requiring a schema/business decision) was never triggered.

**What changed:**
- Both forms gained an identical `awardCategory` selector (`NOMINATION_CATEGORIES`, 8 categories ending in "New Business of the Year") feeding the handler's existing `awardCategory` field.
- Both forms now show/hide a `personFields` block (`nomineeName` + new `nomineeCompany`) vs. a `businessFields` block (new `businessName` + `businessOwner`) based on whether "New Business of the Year" is selected, explicitly clearing the hidden branch's values on every category change so a stale value from a previously-selected branch can never be submitted under the other branch.
- Both forms' submit payloads now send exactly the field set `handleSubmitNomination` reads for the selected branch — no more, no less — restoring real (previously always-failing) submissions to a 200 response.
- Form fields with no dedicated structured destination in the unmodified handler (nominee website/phone for the person branch on `nominate.html`; the nominator's own area on `nominate.html`) are folded into the handler's existing generic `supportingInfo` free-text field rather than silently dropped or invented as new schema columns.
- `nominate.html`'s `nominatorArea` field (the nominator's *own* location) is deliberately **not** mapped to the handler's `county_city`/`region` parameters, which scope the *nominee's* organisation lookup — doing so would have silently mis-scoped that lookup. This is a pre-existing limitation (the nominator's own location was never structurally usable there); it is now documented in code rather than left implicit.

**Tests added/updated:** `tests/nominate.test.js` (+10 new, 1 existing corrected to match the now-split name/company fields — 29/29 pass), `tests/industry-leader.test.js` (+14 new — 51/51 pass), `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` (+5 new in a `frontend payload contract (BTA-095)` block that feeds the *exact* payload shapes both forms now send into the real, unmodified `handleSubmitNomination` handler via the existing mocked-Supabase harness — 2 confirm both forms' person-branch and New-Business-branch payloads now succeed, a 5th re-confirms the *old*, broken `nominate.js` shape still correctly returns 400, proving the server contract itself was not relaxed — 54/54 pass in this file). No existing test was weakened or removed.

**Full verification:** `npx jest` → 82 suites, **6842 passed (3 skipped), 0 failed** (+30 net new tests over the pre-BTA-095 baseline of 6812, matching the three files' additions). `npm run lint` → clean (0 errors). `npm run build` → succeeded (`Build complete in 4606ms`, output at `dist/`). Diff reviewed: `git diff --stat` against the 7 target files shows only those 7 files touched by this task (`industry-leader.html` +31/−*, `industry-leader.js` +203, `nominate.html` +55, `nominate.js` +244, `tests/entry-proxy.test.js` +578, `tests/industry-leader.test.js` +321, `tests/nominate.test.js` +336; 1704 insertions / 64 deletions total). `api/entry-proxy.js`'s working-tree diff was checked line-by-line and confirmed to contain only a pre-existing, unrelated change from an earlier task (the EA-C1 `contact_email` template-substitution fix) — no nomination-field destructuring or validation logic was touched by BTA-095.

**Remaining limitations (not in scope to fix here):** `nominate.html` still has no structural way to scope the nominee's organisation lookup by the nominee's own location (only the nominator's own area is collected, and is intentionally not wired to `county_city`/`region` — see above); this is unchanged from before BTA-095 and would require either a new "nominee location" field or a business decision about whether the nominator's area is an acceptable proxy. No new durable project-wide rule was established by this task (it is a targeted contract-alignment bug fix, not a new architectural pattern), so `CLAUDE.md` was **not** updated.

## W. BTA-096, BTA-097 and BTA-098 — Test Data Manager Verification, Audit and Server-Side Migration (2026-11-02/03)

### W.1 — BTA-096: Post-Implementation Verification of BTA-095 (read-only)

Independently re-verified BTA-095's nomination-form repair against the actual repository state rather than trusting the prior report: confirmed the real 8-file change set (7 code/test files + this task sheet, resolving an apparent "7 vs 8 files" discrepancy that was just an omission from one itemized list, not a real gap), re-confirmed category consistency, the person/New-Business field split, and the shared-contract test's behaviour by re-running it; re-ran the full suite/lint/build and got identical results to BTA-095's own report. Found one genuine, precisely-characterised limitation: `nominate.html` has no nominee-location field, so `findMatchingOrganisation` always receives a null area and therefore always creates a new organisation rather than ever risking a wrong-location match — a duplication risk, not a mismatch risk (refining BTA-095's own framing). No files changed; pure verification.

### W.2 — BTA-097: Test Data Manager Security Dependency and Migration 093 Compatibility Audit (read-only)

Independently re-traced `test-data-manager.js` line-by-line against migration 093 and its predecessors (045/052/078/079), re-confirming BTA-085's finding that it directly accesses 7 of migration 093's 8 target tables (all but `gdpr_requests`) via the browser `authenticated` client, with **no role check anywhere in its invocation path**. Found two things beyond BTA-085's own write-up: (1) the "hidden in production" UI gate (`auth.js`'s old `isDevEnv` check) could be defeated by any logged-in user simply visiting the production URL with `?testMode=1` appended — no console needed; (2) a working precedent for the fix already existed in this codebase — `api/data-proxy.js`'s `test_data_fixture` action (super_admin-gated, environment-guarded via `isTestDataEnvironmentSafe()`), already used by `test-data-manager.js` itself for email-list fixtures, with 32 of the 34 tables the tool touches already present in `MUTABLE_TABLES`. Verdict: migration 093 is **unsafe to apply** until this dependency is resolved; recorded as must-remain-unapplied. No files changed; pure investigation, authorising BTA-098's implementation.

### W.3 — BTA-098: Secure Test Data Manager — Server-Side API Migration (implementation)

Implemented the Option B approach BTA-097 identified, reusing its own precedent exactly rather than inventing a new pattern.

**New server-side module:** `api/_lib/test-data-fixtures.js` (new file, in `_lib/` per this project's Vercel-12-function-limit rule — no new API file created). Ported every database-touching function from the old `test-data-manager.js` (`executeTestDataGeneration` → `generateTestData`, `generateEntries`, `generateMarketingData`, `generateCRMData`, `generatePaymentsData`, `generateMediaData`, `generateRunningOrder`, `seedCounties`, `generateEventExtras`, `generateMarketingExtras`, `generateExtras`, `removeTestData`, `showTestDataInfo` → `getStatus`, `generateMockOrder`, `removeMockOrders`) to run against an injected service-role client, preserving every literal, fixed UUID prefix, and `TEST_MODE_`/`TEST-ENT-`/`TEST-INV-`/`TEST-PAY-` marker exactly as before — this is a faithful move of existing logic, not a redesign. The two email-list-fixture steps now call the already-existing `executeTestDataFixture()` (injected as an `emailFixtures` dependency) instead of making an HTTP round-trip back to the same server process.

**New dispatch:** `api/data-proxy.js` gained a `test_data_manager` operation (actions: `status`, `generate_test_data`, `remove_test_data`, `generate_mock_order`, `remove_mock_order`), gated identically to the existing `test_data_fixture` action — `hasMinimumRole(role, 'super_admin')` and `isTestDataEnvironmentSafe()` (`NODE_ENV !== 'production'` **and** `SUPABASE_URL` in a small explicit non-production allowlist), both re-checked independently on every single call, reading only trusted server-side `process.env` values, never anything from the request body or headers. No table name, column, filter, or deletion criterion is ever accepted from the client — every one is hardcoded inside `test-data-fixtures.js`. `award_years`/`counties` did **not** need adding to `MUTABLE_TABLES`: the new module uses the service-role client directly (the same pattern `executeTestDataFixture()` already used), bypassing the generic CRUD allowlist entirely, so no generic table permission was broadened.

**`test-data-manager.js` rewritten as a thin client:** every `STATE.client.from(...)` call removed (confirmed zero remain). Its five user-facing methods (`generateTestData`, `removeTestData`, `showTestDataInfo`, `generateMockOrder`, `removeMockOrders`) now call `apiClient.post('test_data_manager', { action })` and render the same confirm dialogs / success and error toasts / result modals as before, sourced from the API response instead of direct queries. Pure-UI helpers (`showConfirmDialog`, `showModal`, `showInfoModal`, `showManualInstructionsModal`, `reloadPage`) are unchanged.

**UI reveal gating fixed (`auth.js`, `app.js`):** the old `isDevEnv` hostname/`?testMode=1`-query-parameter check in `showDashboard()` is removed entirely. A new `authModule.refreshTestModeVisibility()` shows the Test Mode dropdown only after a live `test_data_manager`/`status` call succeeds (meaning the server has already confirmed both super_admin and a safe environment) — a 403 or any error leaves it hidden, fail-closed. It only runs at all if the client already knows (via the existing `rbacModule.currentRole`) that the user is super_admin, avoiding an unnecessary call for everyone else. Ctrl+Shift+D in `app.js` can now only hide the dropdown and re-trigger that same server check — it can no longer reveal it by itself. The dropdown's visibility is now a mirror of the real server gate, not an independent (and, as BTA-097 found, spoofable) guess — but per the task's own instruction, this is a UX honesty fix, not a substitute for the server-side enforcement, which is unconditional and independent of whether the UI is ever shown.

**Explicitly out of scope and confirmed untouched:** migration 093 (still unapplied, not edited); `payment-success.html`'s separate anon-key `payments` read (confirmed unchanged — its 1-line working-tree diff is `public-site-settings.js`, a pre-existing unrelated change from an earlier task); `event_guests`/`check-in-app.js`'s access and the 078/079 migrations (not touched); no Supabase connection, migration execution, RLS/grant change, or deployment occurred.

**Tests:** `tests/test-data-fixtures.test.js` (new, 14 tests) — exercises the real module against a mocked Supabase-shaped client, asserting every generated write carries a reserved marker, every cleanup delete is scoped to one, the email-fixture steps delegate to the injected dependency rather than touching `email_lists`/`email_list_subscribers` directly, and that errors never leak raw database detail. `tests/data-proxy.test.js` (+23 tests, new `test_data_manager operation` describe block mirroring the existing `test_data_fixture` block's structure) — covers missing/invalid auth, every non-super_admin role individually, role-lookup failure, production `NODE_ENV`, missing/wrong/contradictory `SUPABASE_URL`, a simulated client-supplied bypass attempt (mirroring the old `?testMode=1` escape), unknown actions, all 5 valid actions succeeding together, and a 500 that doesn't leak internal detail. `tests/test-data-manager.test.js` (rewritten, 19 tests replacing tests for the now-deleted internal DB-generation methods) — confirms every user-facing method calls the correct action, handles success/partial-success/failure/thrown-error responses, and that the pure-UI helpers still work; also asserts the removed methods (`executeTestDataGeneration`, `_safeWrite`, `uid`, etc.) no longer exist on the client object.

**Verification results:** targeted (`test-data-fixtures.test.js` + the new `data-proxy.test.js` block + `test-data-manager.test.js`) all pass. Full suite: **83 suites passed, 6795 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (net: +1 suite, and a net reduction in total test count versus the BTA-096/097-era baseline of 6842 — expected and correct, since the ~100+ tests that existed only to exercise the now-deleted client-side DB-generation methods were necessarily removed along with that code, not weakened; equivalent-or-better coverage of the same behaviour now exists in the two new/expanded server-side test files). `npm run lint` — 0 errors (and `api/_lib/test-data-fixtures.js`, which `npm run lint`'s own `api/*.js` glob does not reach, was separately confirmed clean via a direct `npx eslint` run). `npm run build` — succeeded; the `admin` lazy chunk shrank from 55KB to 12KB, consistent with ~2,000 lines of generation logic having moved out of the browser bundle.

**Remaining limitations / open items (not actioned, carried forward):** migration 093 is not yet re-assessed against this change — a fresh BTA-097-style re-trace should confirm zero remaining browser dependency on its 8 tables before any future decision to apply it; `payment-success.html`'s anon-key read and `document-management.js`/`judge-login.html`'s unrelated direct dependencies (BTA-085/097) remain open, separate items.

## Y. BTA-100 — Secure Public Payment Confirmation and Migration 093 Compatibility (2026-11-03)

### Y.1 — The original dependency, and what it actually was

`payment-success.html` directly queried `payments` with the anon-key Supabase client, filtered by `.eq('entry_id', entryId)`. Tracing the real payment flow (`api/stripe-payment.js`'s `createPublicCheckout`/`handleCheckoutSessionCompleted`) found this query **had never worked**, independent of any security question: (1) `payments` has no `entry_id` column anywhere in this repository's SQL (`migrations/000-complete-database-setup.sql:610-619`, `database-payments-setup.sql:113-133` — only `organisation_id`/`invoice_id`); (2) nothing in the public entry-fee checkout flow ever inserts a `payments` row — the webhook (`handleCheckoutSessionCompleted`, `api/stripe-payment.js:214-294`) writes the real `payment_reference` onto **`entries.payment_reference`** and creates an `invoices` row, never a `payments` row; (3) the URL's `entry` parameter is actually `entry.entry_number` (e.g. `ENT-2026-0001`), not an `entries.id` UUID, set by `createPublicCheckout`'s `success_url` (`:931`). The anon query therefore always fell through to the "no verified payment found" / catch-fallback path, even for genuinely successful payments — a pre-existing functional bug, not only a security exposure.

### Y.2 — New architecture

**`api/stripe-payment.js`** gained `getPublicPaymentConfirmation` (new `GET ?action=public-payment-confirmation&session_id=...` route, no auth required, rate-limited at 20/minute/IP via a new `checkPublicConfirmationRate`). Authorisation is **possession of the genuine Stripe Checkout Session ID** that Stripe itself substitutes into `success_url` on redirect (never client-chosen or guessable) — not an entry ID, not a token the client supplies. The handler: validates the `session_id` format (`/^cs_[a-zA-Z0-9_]+$/`); calls `stripe.checkout.sessions.retrieve(sessionId)` (a 404 — never Stripe's own error text — on any failure); returns `{status: 'pending'}` if not yet paid; otherwise extracts `session.metadata.entry_id` (recorded server-side by Stripe at session-creation time, never re-suppliable by the caller) and performs **one** service-role-client lookup, `supabase.from('entries').select('payment_reference').eq('id', entryId)`, scoped only by that Stripe-verified ID — the client's own query string is never read for this. Returns exactly `{status, paymentReference}` — no amount, no customer email, no entry details, no internal errors.

**`payment-success.html`** no longer loads `@supabase/supabase-js` or constructs any Supabase client. Its inline script now calls the new endpoint only when `session_id` is present in the URL, and otherwise (or on any non-2xx/network failure) falls back to the pre-existing, already-safe "Entry reference: …" display — the same graceful-degradation UX the page already had, now reached by a safer and more honest path (it degrades for the right reason — no session_id, or a genuinely non-existent/unpaid session — not because the query could never have matched anything).

### Y.3 — Direct browser dependency removed

`payment-success.html`'s `.from('payments')` anon-key call (`:178` in the pre-BTA-100 version) is gone — confirmed by grep (`supabase.createClient` / `.from('payments')`: 0 matches in the file).

### Y.4 — `document-management.js` re-investigated (no change made)

Re-confirmed BTA-099's finding with a fresh search this task: `documentModule`'s functions (`document-management.js`, registered as `ModuleRegistry.register('documentModule', ...)`) are invoked nowhere outside the file itself and its own test file — no nav link, button, or `data-action` anywhere in `src/partials/` or any other source file calls it. It is loaded unconditionally in every admin session (same `build.js` module-list pattern `test-data-manager.js` had before BTA-098) and has no role check of any kind. **Not a migration-093 target** (`documents`/`document_versions` aren't among its 8 tables), so not a blocker for 093, but a live, confirmed security gap in the broader access-control model — the same category of issue BTA-097 required a decision for with `test-data-manager.js`. No code change made, per this task's explicit instruction not to redesign or retire it without a decision. **Recommended as its own future task**, requiring your approval on direction: wire it into the UI with a proper `super_admin` role check (if the feature is wanted), or formally retire it (if not).

### Y.5 — Fresh migration 093 compatibility conclusion

Re-ran the same repository-wide, table-specific search as BTA-099 for all 8 target tables (`organisations`, `organisation_contacts`, `gdpr_requests`, `entries`, `award_assignments`, `invoices`, `payments`, `award_years`), excluding `api/`, `tests/`, `dist/`, `scripts/`. **Result: zero browser-side hits remain for all 8 tables.** Only `clear-test-data.js` (a standalone, service-role, RLS-exempt Node CLI tool — re-confirmed, not merely assumed) still touches `organisations`/`entries`, unaffected by any RLS/grant change. **This is a repository-evidence conclusion only** — the live Supabase schema/policy state was not connected to or re-verified this task (last live-verified at BTA-078/079, which predates BTA-095/097/098/099/100's fixes). Migration 093 was not applied, edited, or executed.

### Y.6 — Tests and results

New: `tests/payment-success.test.js` (9 tests, extracts and executes the page's actual inline script against a mocked `fetch`) — confirms the new API is called with `session_id` (and only then), a paid confirmation displays the reference, a pending/failed/non-ok/missing-session_id response all fall back safely with no raw error ever shown, and no more than one fetch occurs per load. `tests/stripe-payment.test.js` (+14 tests, `getPublicPaymentConfirmation` describe block) — missing/malformed `session_id` rejected before any Stripe call (including SQL-injection-shaped and path-traversal-shaped inputs); a non-existent session returns 404 without leaking Stripe's error text; an unpaid session returns `pending` with no DB lookup at all; a paid session returns only `{status, paymentReference}`; a client-supplied `entry_id` in the query string is proven ignored (the DB `.eq()` call is asserted against the Stripe-verified metadata value only); a paid session with no `entry_id` in metadata (e.g. an event-ticket session) degrades safely; no Authorization header is required; rate limiting kicks in after 20 requests/minute/IP; and an unexpected exception returns a generic 500 without leaking internal detail. Full suite: **84 suites, 6818 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (exactly the expected +23 over BTA-099's 6795 baseline). `npm run lint`: 0 errors. `npm run build`: succeeded. No pre-existing test was weakened; all prior Stripe/webhook/refund/event-checkout tests in `stripe-payment.test.js` continue to pass unchanged.

### Y.7 — Remaining risks and decisions

- The defense-in-depth `entries` lookup trusts that `session.metadata.entry_id` was set correctly at checkout-session creation time (it is, per `createPublicCheckout`) — if a future checkout path sets this metadata differently, the fallback to `session.payment_intent` alone still returns a safe (if less CMS-canonical) reference, never an error.
- `document-management.js`'s orphaned-but-invocable status (Y.4) is unresolved and requires your decision.
- No live re-verification of this task's repository-evidence conclusion has occurred; Supabase connectivity (BTA-081/082's blocker) and explicit authorisation are still required before migration 093 can be applied anywhere.

### Y.8 — Git/worktree state

Branch `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, HEAD `fd2e14522d2259c3cd0ceb73176e65db1096ec81` — unchanged. 0 staged. Files touched by BTA-100: `api/stripe-payment.js`, `payment-success.html`, `tests/stripe-payment.test.js` (modified), `tests/payment-success.test.js` (new) plus this section. No migration, SQL, Supabase connection, deployment, commit, or push occurred.

### Z.3 — BTA-105 (fresh, this task): re-verification and production readiness assessment

#### Z.3.1 — Fresh test-project re-verification (no drift)

Re-ran, fresh, this task: `pg_policies` for all 8 target tables — identical to BTA-104's post-application result (only `no_direct_client_access`/`service_role_all` per table). `information_schema.role_table_grants` for `anon`/`authenticated` — empty (no privileges) on all 8 tables; `service_role` — full `{DELETE,INSERT,REFERENCES,SELECT,TRIGGER,TRUNCATE,UPDATE}` retained on all 8, as intended. `list_migrations` — `093_harden_remaining_permissive_policy_tables` still recorded. The 4 unrelated tables (`event_guests`, `documents`, `document_versions`, `user_roles`) — unchanged from their own established baseline. **No drift since BTA-104.**

#### Z.3.2 — Fresh production findings (live, read-only, this task)

Project identity re-verified before every query: `qdzyknercdqwhwijbcxf`, `british-trade-awards-cms`, `eu-west-2`, `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`. `list_migrations` → `[]` (still no tracked history; migration 093 is **not** recorded or applied).

**Policies, all 8 target tables** — exactly the BTA-101 baseline, unchanged: each of `organisations`, `organisation_contacts`, `entries`, `award_assignments`, `invoices`, `payments`, `award_years` carries exactly one policy, `"Allow all access to <table>"` (role `public`, `USING (true)`, `WITH CHECK (true)`); `gdpr_requests` carries exactly three: `"Allow all access to gdpr_requests"`, `"Allow anon read on gdpr_requests"`, `"Allow authenticated full access on gdpr_requests"`. **This is precisely the policy set migration 093's `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` statements target by exact name** (the generic per-table drops plus `gdpr_requests`'s 3 named drops) — no 9th/unexpected policy was found on any of the 8 tables, and none of migration 045's policy-name variants (`*_select`/`*_insert`/`*_update`/`*_service`/`*_anon_select`) exist on production to be defensively dropped (those `DROP IF EXISTS` statements will simply no-op, as designed).

**Grants, all 8 target tables** — `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role` each hold the full `{DELETE,INSERT,REFERENCES,SELECT,TRIGGER,TRUNCATE,UPDATE}` set on every one of the 8 tables. Migration 093's `REVOKE ALL ... FROM anon` / `FROM authenticated` will remove exactly this; it does not touch `service_role`, which is correct — the application's server-side proxies (`api/data-proxy.js`, `api/entry-proxy.js`, `api/stripe-payment.js`) connect with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` and must keep full access.

**Schema** — column-level inspection (`information_schema.columns`) of all 8 tables confirms every table exists on production with the names migration 093 expects. Production's schema is a strict subset of test's for several tables (test has since gained extra columns — e.g. `entries`' GDPR-consent columns, `award_years`' extended scheduling/display columns, `invoices`' extended billing columns, `organisations`' social/company-profile columns, `payments`' gateway/refund columns — none yet applied to production). **This is irrelevant to migration 093's compatibility**: every statement in the migration operates on policies and grants by table/policy name only; it references no column anywhere, so the column-level schema drift between test and production does not affect whether the migration will run correctly on production.

**4 unrelated tables** — `event_guests`, `documents`, `document_versions`, `user_roles` on production **still each carry only their own original `"Allow all access to <table>"` permissive policy** (not yet hardened by migrations 078/079, unlike on test) — this is expected and pre-existing (part of the broader, already-tracked 22-table/migration-088 exposure in `CLAUDE.md`'s banner, not something migration 093 is meant to address), and is **unaffected either way** by migration 093, which does not reference any of these 4 tables.

#### Z.3.3 — Migration 093 compatibility assessment (confirmed)

Re-read the live migration file this task; it is unchanged (same 183 lines verified in BTA-101–104). Every `DROP POLICY`, `CREATE POLICY`, and `REVOKE` statement targets exactly the objects found live above:
- Every one of the 8 referenced tables exists in production. **(Confirmed.)**
- Every policy the migration drops by name either exists (and will be removed) or doesn't exist (safe no-op via `IF EXISTS`). **No permissive policy exists on any of the 8 production tables that migration 093 fails to address — confirmed, not assumed, against the live policy list.** **(Confirmed.)**
- The intended `service_role_all` (`USING (auth.role() = 'service_role')`) and `no_direct_client_access` (`USING (false)`) policies are identical in design to what is already live and independently verified working on test. **(Confirmed via test's proven post-application state.)**
- No statement references any column, so the test/production column-level schema drift cannot cause a statement to fail. **(Confirmed.)**
- The migration touches only the 8 named tables' policies/grants plus a final `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` — no statement can affect `event_guests`/`documents`/`document_versions`/`user_roles` or any other object. **(Confirmed by reading every statement; the migration contains no wildcard or dynamic-table logic beyond its own fixed 7-element array plus the separate `gdpr_requests` block.)**
- The migration is idempotent (re-confirmed): a second accidental application would no-op throughout (`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS`, `duplicate_object`-guarded `CREATE POLICY`, no-error `REVOKE` of an already-revoked privilege).

**No uncertainty remains about the migration's compatibility with production's actual current schema/policy/grant state** — this was the one assessment this task could resolve beyond repository evidence, and it is now resolved with live evidence.

#### Z.3.4 — Application dependency re-check (fresh, repository evidence)

A fresh, repository-wide grep this task for `.from('<table>')` across all `.js` and `.html` files for all 8 target tables found **zero matches in any browser-facing file** — all 19 `.js` matches are in `api/` (server-side, service-role), `tests/`, `scripts/` (offline CLI), or `clear-test-data.js` (standalone CLI); the `.html` grep returned zero matches entirely. - **Test Data Manager (BTA-098):** re-confirmed intact. `test-data-manager.js` has zero `STATE.client`/`supabase.createClient` references. `api/data-proxy.js`'s `test_data_manager` dispatch (confirmed by direct read, lines ~2258–2270) requires `hasMinimumRole(role, 'super_admin')` **and** `isTestDataEnvironmentSafe()` (which rejects `NODE_ENV === 'production'`) before any action — both gates present and unchanged. #### Z.3.7 — Production readiness decision: **Ready with prerequisites**

No technical incompatibility was found. Every policy migration 093 drops matches production's live policy set exactly; every grant it revokes matches production's live grants exactly; no column or other schema dependency exists; zero live application dependency was found on any of the 8 tables; the identical SQL is already proven, by live independent verification, to produce the intended end-state on test. ## BB. BTA-107 — Front-End & CMS Integration Audit, Post-Production-Hardening (2026-11-10)

Read-only, local-only audit verifying the public front-end (nomination, voting, auth) and server-side proxies still correctly route around migration 093's now-live production RLS/grant hardening (BTA-106).

### BB.1 — API & data-proxy verification

Fresh repository grep for `.from('<table>')` against all 8 migration-093 tables across `nominate.js`, `industry-leader.js`, `submit-entry.js`, `public-voting.js`, `auth.js`, `judge-portal.js`, `register.html`-related scripts, and `vote`-prefixed files: **zero matches.** Direct inspection confirms the actual submission mechanisms:
- `nominate.js` (nomination form) submits via `fetch('/api/entry-proxy', ...)` — server-side, service-role.
- `public-voting.js` submits via `fetch('/api/voting-proxy', ...)` — server-side, service-role.
- `auth.js` only constructs a Supabase client for `supabase.auth.*` session calls (`STATE.client = supabase.createClient(...)`, used for login/session only) — confirmed by the same grep finding zero `.from()` calls in this file.
- `api/voting-proxy.js` touches `entries` twice (lines 94, 292) via its own service-role client (`createClient(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY)`, line 18-20) — unaffected by the anon/authenticated grant revocation, since `service_role` retained full grants in migration 093 (confirmed live in BTA-106).
- `api/registration-proxy.js` does not touch any of the 8 target tables at all.

**Conclusion: every front-end path that could interact with a migration-093 table already does so exclusively through a service-role server-side proxy. Nothing in the public-facing nomination, voting, or auth flows depends on the `anon`/`authenticated` grants that were just revoked on production.**
