## CC. BTA-108 — Local Environment Setup & Smoke-Test Readiness (2026-11-10)

Read-only inspection of environment/config/build tooling to determine the exact local startup procedure. No server was started and no credentials were fabricated — this sandbox has no `.env` file present (confirmed: `ls .env` → not found), so a live browser smoke test against a real Supabase instance cannot be performed from here; that step requires the user (or whoever runs this locally) to supply real credentials.

### CC.1 — Environment variables required

Full list from `.env.example` (copy to `.env`, never commit it):

| Variable | Required for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` | Core app (frontend + all API proxies) | Anon key is injected client-side via `build.js` into `<meta name="supabase-*">` tags; service key is server-side only, never exposed to the browser |
| `RESEND_API_KEY`, `FROM_EMAIL`, `FROM_NAME` | Email sending | — |
| `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `STRIPE_PRICE_ID` | Payments | `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is **not** an env var — entered into the CMS itself (Settings/Events), stored in that browser's `localStorage` |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | AI vetting | Optional feature |
| `CRON_SECRET` | Daily automation cron | Fails closed (500) if unset |
| `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | Email bounce/complaint webhook | Fails closed (500) if unset |
| `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | CORS allowlist for `data-proxy.js` | Defaults to `APP_URL` if unset |
| `APP_URL` | Absolute URL generation (og:image, email links) | — |
| `SENTRY_DSN`, `BTA_LOGO_URL`, `DEV_EMAIL`, `CONTACT_EMAIL` | Optional, all have safe fallbacks | — |
| Social media vars (`TWITTER_*`, `LINKEDIN_*`, `FACEBOOK_*`, `INSTAGRAM_*`) | Social posting only | Not needed for a core smoke test |

### CC.2 — Exact local startup commands

This repository has **no `dev` script and no `vercel` CLI listed as a dependency** (confirmed: `package.json` scripts are `lint`/`lint:fix`/`format`/`format:check`/`test`/`test:coverage`/`typecheck`/`validate`/`build`/`prepare` only; `vercel` absent from `dependencies`/`devDependencies`; no `node_modules/.bin/vercel`). Because the project is structured entirely as a Vercel deployment (`vercel.json`'s `functions`/`crons`/`rewrites`, `api/*.js` as individual serverless handlers, no bundled Express/Node server anywhere in the repo), the correct, standard way to run the full stack (static frontend **and** the `/api/*` functions) locally is the Vercel CLI's own dev server, invoked via `npx` since it isn't installed:

```bash
# 1. Install dependencies (if not already)
npm install

# 2. Create your local env file
cp .env.example .env
# then fill in real SUPABASE_URL / SUPABASE_ANON_KEY / SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY
# (and STRIPE_*/RESEND_* if testing those flows) — use the TEST Supabase
# project (htcsewpztvxafksdzuhp), never production, for any local smoke test

# 3. Build the static admin bundle + public pages into dist/
#    (also injects SUPABASE_URL/ANON_KEY into the built HTML's meta tags)
node build.js

# 4. Run the full stack locally (serves dist/ + api/* together, matching
#    vercel.json's rewrites/headers/function config)
npx vercel dev
```

`vercel dev` will prompt to link the project to a Vercel account/project on first run; it then serves the built static files and proxies `/api/*` requests to local invocations of each handler, reading env vars from the project's own `.env` (standard Vercel CLI behaviour) — matching production's request shape far more closely than a plain static file server would, since the admin app and all public pages route their data operations through these same `/api/*` handlers.

**Caveat:** `node build.js` runs `npx eslint *.js api/*.js --max-warnings 0` as its first step (confirmed, `build.js:84`) and aborts the build on any lint error — this task's earlier full lint run (BTA-107) found 0 errors, so this is not currently a blocker.

### CC.3 — Admin CMS & public front-end health check (code inspection, not a live run)

- `config.js`'s `SUPABASE_CONFIG` reads from `<meta name="supabase-url">`/`<meta name="supabase-anon-key">` tags (or `window.__SUPABASE_URL__`/`window.__SUPABASE_ANON_KEY__` as a fallback) — both tags are confirmed populated by `build.js` from `process.env.SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` at build time (`build.js:262-269`), and the same patching is applied to every public HTML page that needs it (`build.js:341-349` — `judge-login.html`, `judge-portal.html`, `reset-password.html`, `check-in.html`), so once `.env` is filled in and `node build.js` has run, both the admin dashboard and every public page that needs a browser-side Supabase client will point at the configured project automatically — no manual endpoint editing required.
- The admin dashboard (`index.html`, assembled by `build.js` from `src/partials/`) and all data-mutating operations route through `/api/data-proxy.js`, which itself reads `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` directly from `process.env` server-side — confirmed already wired correctly in BTA-105/107, unaffected by anything in this task.
- No code change was needed or made to make any page "point at" a local/configured endpoint — the existing build-time injection mechanism already does this correctly; the only prerequisite is a populated `.env` before running `node build.js`.
- **Live browser smoke test was not performed** — this sandbox has no `.env`/Supabase credentials, and fabricating or requesting them insecurely would be inappropriate. The commands in CC.2 are verified by direct reading of `build.js`/`vercel.json`/`config.js`, not by having actually run them end-to-end this task.

## GG. BTA-116 — Post-Audit UX Safeguards & Technical Polish (2026-11-10)

Implemented the full BTA-115 resolution plan under explicit authorisation.

**M1 (Unsaved Work Guard):** New shared utility in `utils.js` — `markFormDirty`/`clearFormDirty`/`hasUnsavedFormChanges`/`trackUnsavedChanges` — backed by a new `STATE.unsavedChanges` bag (documented in `config.js`'s ownership table). Wired into Settings (`#settings` tab-pane, tracked once at app init; cleared generically on any successful API call while that tab is active), CRM meeting notes (`createMeetingNoteForm`, tracked on open, cleared on `hidden.bs.modal`), and Organisation edit (`companyProfileModal`, cleared at the top of every `openCompanyProfile` render — covers both the save-then-refresh and cancel-then-refresh paths). `app.js`'s existing `beforeunload` handler now also checks this flag alongside its pre-existing save-spinner and Email Builder checks.

**M2 (Human-Readable Errors):** `utils.js`'s `apiClient._call` no longer throws `"API error <status>"` — the JSON-parse-failure path and the generic `!res.ok` fallback both now throw plain-English messages. Three pre-existing tests in `tests/api-client.test.js` asserted the old raw-string messages; updated to assert the new message rather than reverting the fix (two of the three failures were a cascade from the first — `MAX_RETRIES` never got reset to `2` once the first assertion failed mid-test, not a second independent defect).

**M3 (Query Escaping) — implemented with a documented deviation:** `api/data-proxy.js:1801` (CRM segment-builder's `contains` operator) now wraps the user term in `escapeLikePattern()` before building the `%...%` pattern, exactly as specified. The second named site, `api/data-proxy.js:735` (the generic filter dispatcher), was **not** blanket-escaped — tracing its callers (`crm.js`, `awards.js`, `events.js`, `gdpr.js`) found they already pre-build the full `%...%` pattern themselves before it reaches this dispatcher, so escaping the whole value there would escape the wildcard delimiters themselves and break every existing substring-search feature. Instead, the fix was applied at the genuine root cause: `events.js`'s organisation-search autocomplete (`_searchOrgsForAttendee`) was the one caller among those four that built its pattern from an **unescaped** raw term — the other three were already either escaped (`gdpr.js`'s own `_escapeLike`), fed from a fixed `<select>` (`crm.js`, no free text), or a hardcoded literal pattern, not user input (`awards.js`).

**M4 (FK Indexing):** New `migrations/094-add-fk-indexes.sql` written (hyphenated to match this project's existing migration-file naming convention, rather than the literally-requested underscore style) — adds `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` for the 7 FK columns BTA-115 found unindexed on the test project. **Not applied to any database** — file only, per the standing migration process (must be verified on test and separately authorised before production, same as migration 093).

**UI/accessibility polish (m1–m4):** "Payload URL" → "Webhook URL" (`src/partials/99-shell-foot.html`); `entries.js`'s delete-failure toast no longer concatenates a technical prefix onto `error.message` (and falls back to a friendly default if empty); `public-voting.js`'s vote-submit button now gets a visible `disabled` state (previously only `aria-busy`) during submission; all 10 of `nominate.html`'s validated fields gained `aria-describedby` linking them to their `.invalid-feedback` error elements.

**Verification:** `node build.js` — clean, 0 lint errors. `npx jest` — 84 suites, 6818 passed, 3 pre-existing skips, 0 failed (after the test-assertion fix above).

**Git/worktree state:** commit `a5d330e` (13 files changed, 173 insertions, 23 deletions, including the new migration file) on `claude/supabase-cms-setup-test-e6oiza`, pushed — `9cab542..a5d330e`. Working tree clean immediately after.

## HH. BTA-117 — Project Continuity Finalization & Closeout (2026-11-10)

(Note: this programme's task numbering has no BTA-111 — the sequence runs BTA-110 directly to BTA-112; recorded here as a correction rather than silently smoothed over, per this project's continuity protocol.)
- **BTA-115/116:** a 6-vertical, zero-Critical-finding enterprise audit (parallelised across 3 background investigations covering non-technical usability, public/admin UI, API/auth/data-integrity, database/RLS, build/environment, and code hygiene/test coverage), followed by full implementation of its Moderate/Minor resolution plan — including one explicitly-documented, reasoned deviation from a literal instruction where following it exactly would have introduced a regression.
